# Turing — Shipped Ledger (CHANGELOG)

> Concise index of work that has shipped. **`git log` is the authoritative
> history** — this file is just a searchable map of *what* shipped and *where*
> the rationale lived. Full implementation detail was intentionally removed from
> the roadmap once shipped (see [IMPROVEMENTS.md](IMPROVEMENTS.md) header rule).
>
> Maintenance: when a task in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) ships, move its one-line
> entry here (under its block), reference the commit if useful, and delete it
> from ROADMAP. See `agents.md` → "Roadmap & docs maintenance".

- **T10** — Persona resolved ONCE per turn (post-flow-context, post-early-advance). (§I.5 step 2)

- **T16** — Terminal refactor — all feature flags + all `@Deprecated(forRemoval=true)` symbols + the legacy `execute()` branch DELETED. (§I.5 step 8)

## D.2 — Engine declarative additions (new node types & policies)

- **T42** — `writeSlot` node type — slot SET with server-side `{{variable}}` interpolation via `ChatFlowOps.applyWriteSlotNode`. (§VII.3.a)
- **T43** — `formCapture` node type — like `aiQuestion` but with strict regex validation (CPF, CNPJ, email, phone, CEP). (§VII.3.b)
- **T44** — CPF/CNPJ digit-check (mod-11) on `formCapture` patterns — strict regex format + checksum validation. (§VII.3.b)
- **T45** — `onJudgeReject` declarative policy (already documented as patch #22) — `advance_with_literal` / `reprompt` / `block` per `aiQuestion`. (§I.3 patch #22)
- **T46** — `functionCall` node runtime — type is no longer vapor. (§VII.4.a)
- **T47** — `subFlowSwitch` node — both backend runtime and frontend editor block shipped. (§VII.4.b)
- **T48** — `scheduleAgent` integration node — fires a routine remote and waits for its slot-bus response. (§VII.4.c)
- **T49** — `continueOnFailure: true` flag on nodes — authors mark a `functionCall` or `scheduleAgent` node where a tool/routine failure routes the flow along an outgoing edge whose `sourceHandle` is `"failure"` instead of advancing silently. (§VII.4.d)
- **T50** — `onError` edges in editor visual — analogous to try/catch; red arrow that fires when the node fails. (§VII.4.d)
- **T51** — Capture-first inversion — persists the user message into the slot BEFORE the judge runs; the judge becomes advisory (writes a `<slot>__confidence` low/high grade in a parallel slot) so the flow never stalls with `slot=null`. (§VII.4.e)
- **T52** — Few-shot baked into the platform-level judge system prompt (`LlmJudgeGuardrailStrategy.askJudge`) — 3 worked examples (compound `cargo`+tempo → capture cargo only; informal `objetivo` → capture intent; stale option click on the wrong step → reject + redirect). Cuts stochastic rejects across all flows without per-customer `aiInstruction` tuning. (§VII.4.f)
- **T53** — `abandoned` auto-escalation — opt-in per-flow `TurChatFlow.abandonHandoffMessage`; when non-blank, a judge-detected abandonment ends with that human-consultant offer and writes a `handoff_offered=abandon` tracking slot instead of just closing. Null/blank = legacy goodbye. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.25`. (§VII.4.g)

- **T54** — SSE for slot updates — `TurChatSlotEventBus` in-memory bus + `GET /chat/slots/stream` (`Flux<TurChatSessionSlotsDto>`); three write paths (node, `slots.set`, `POST /chat/slots`) instrumented. (§VII.5.a)

- **T56** — `GET /chat/share/og` — server-side rendered Open Graph card. (§VII.5.c)
- **T57** — `GET /chat/state` — snapshot of active flow (`flowId`/`flowName`/`currentNodeId`/`guardrailMethod`/`experimentKey`/`variantLabel`). (§VII.5.d)
- **T58** — `POST /chat/slot-extract` — multipart file → Tika → LLM structured output → write per slot. (§VII.5.e)
- **T59** — `TurLlmSummaryService.generate(cacheKey, data, systemPrompt, regenerate)` — reusable LLM summary with cache (TTL from Global Settings) + regenerate. (§VII.5.f)
- **T60** — **Slot audit log** — every slot write registers origin (`NODE`/`TOOL`/`ENDPOINT`/`EXTRACT`) in `chat_slot_write_log` (id, conversationId, slotName, oldValue, newValue, source, originDetail, ts). (§VII.6.a)
- **T61** — **PII slot prefix `pii_*`** — three guarantees layered on the existing slot infrastructure. (§VII.6.b)
- **T62** — **Webhook handoff channel + admin-declared slot-write webhooks.** New `TurChatWebhook` entity (`tur_chat_webhook`, Liquibase [`v2026.3.1.13.yaml`](../turing-app/src/main/resources/db/changelog/v2026.3.1.13.yaml), camelCase columns + `slotTr… (§VII.6.c)
- **T63** — **Slot delta SSE** — opt-in `GET /chat/slots/stream/delta` emits only `{added, updated, removed}` deltas instead of re-serializing the full map per write (~10× fewer bytes on 20+ slot conversations). (§VII.6.d)
- **T64** — **Multi-modal slots (`IMAGE`/`AUDIO`/`FILE`).** Three new members on [`TurAIAgentSlotType`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/persistence/model/agent/TurAIAgentSlotType.java) (+ `isMultiModal()` helper) whose runtime value is a… (§VII.6.e)
- **T65** — **Snapshot export of conversation state.** New [`TurChatSessionExportDto`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/persistence/dto/agent/TurChatSessionExportDto.java) bundles everything a debugger / regression harness needs to reconst… (§VII.6.f)
- **T66** — **Per-agent submission retention policy** (LGPD / GDPR compliance hook). (§VII.6.g)
- **T233** — **API-key auth layer for the public SN surface.** Per-site `TurSNSite.apiAuthMode` (`PUBLIC` default / `API_KEY`); when `API_KEY`, the visitor-facing endpoints (search/ac/query/click/chat/spell-check) require a Turing-registered Dev Token in the `Key` header or `apiKey` query param via `TurSNSiteApiKeyGateFilter` (else HTTP 401). Admin picker in the SN Behavior form. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.26`. (§VII.6.h)

## D.4 — A/B testing evolution

- **T67** — A/B testing native: 5 columns on `TurChatFlow` (`experimentKey`, `variantLabel`, `trafficWeight`, `experimentStartsAt`, `experimentEndsAt`) + `assignVariant` deterministic hash + sticky continuation + `isInExperimentWindow` scheduling filte… (§VII.7.a)
- **T68** — A/B significance endpoint `/experiment/{key}/significance` — two-proportion z-test with Wald CI. (§VII.7.b)
- **T69** — A/B analytics dimensions — `experimentKey` + `variantLabel` persisted in `TurChatSessionEvent`; scorecard supports 4 dimensions (agent / persona / experiment / variant). (§VII.7.c)
- **T70** — **A/B multi-armed bandit (Thompson sampling)** — new per-flow `banditEnabled` Boolean column on `TurChatFlow` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.9.yaml`); when at least one variant in an experimentKey has it set, the engine swaps the deterministic weigh… (§VII.8.a)
- **T71** — **Champion-challenger auto-promotion** — closes the A/B loop: T68 says *whether* a variant won, T71 *acts* on it by pinning the winner to 100% traffic and archiving the losers. (§VII.8.b)
- **T72** — **Per-node A/B** — A/B a single node's wording without duplicating the whole flow. (§VII.8.c)
- **T73** — **Forced variant via URL param (`?_ab_variant=<label>`)** — QA + sales-demo override that hard-pins a specific A/B arm regardless of the deterministic hash (T67), Thompson sampling (T70), or the scheduled experiment window. (§VII.8.d)
- **T74** — **Cohort filters in scorecard** — slice the A/B scorecard by visitor sub-population so an operator can answer "does this variant win on *mobile* / in *pt-BR* / in *America/Sao_Paulo*?". (§VII.8.e)
- **T75** — **Variant trace logging** — `TurChatFlowEngineService.advance(...)` now emits one structured `[A/B Trace] experimentKey:variantLabel:nodeId:userMessage` log line per turn whenever the active flow has a non-blank `experimentKey`; plain (non-… (§VII.8.f)

## D.5 — Code Interpreter hardening (the missing security layer)

- **T76** — Date-bucketed sessions + cleanup task — disk-leak fix (was accumulating ~50MB/day on a moderate site). (§VII.9.a)
- **T77** — Per-agent Python deps auto-install (global + per-agent UNION) — admin declares `pip install reportlab matplotlib qrcode` in a Custom Tool textarea; engine ensures deps before `executePython`. (§VII.9.b)
- **T78** — Multi-tenant path isolation — session dirs scoped as `tenants/{agentId}/{convId}/...`. (§VII.9.c)
- **T79** — HMAC-signed URLs + cookie-binding for `/api/v2/code-interpreter/{session}/{file}` — prevents URL replay / cross-browser leakage (`TurCodeInterpreterUrlSigner`). (§VII.9.d)
- **T80** — ⭐ **Container sandbox (Docker)** — opt-in per-deployment execution mode (`NATIVE` default \| `DOCKER`) in Global Settings. (§VII.9.e)
- **T81** — Resource limits per execution (cgroups) for the **NATIVE** path — opt-in CPU/RAM cap (`turing.code-interpreter.native.limits.*`). (§VII.9.f)
- **T82** — Pre-warmed Python interpreter pool — first call pays ~200ms startup. (§VII.9.g)
- **T83** — Structured output capture — `executePythonStructured(...)` returns a `TurCodeInterpreterResult` record (`stdout`, `stderr`, `files:[{name,url,image,sizeBytes}]`, `exitCode`, `timedOut`, `durationMs`, plus `markdown`); Groovy `code.executePy… (§VII.9.h)

## D.9 — SDK & frontend surface

- **T104** — 10 hooks in `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`: `useTuringSlotWriter` · `useTuringSlotExtract` · `useTuringHandoff` · SSE in `useTuringSlots` · `useTuringVoice` · `useTuringFlowState` · `useTuringExperiment` · `useTuringSlot<T>` generic with auto J… (§VII.13.a)

- **T107** — **`formCapture` form-event SSE** — a `formCapture` node gains an optional `formFields` array (`ChatFlowNode.FormField`: name→slot, label, type, required, placeholder, validationRule, options). (§VII.13.d)
- **T234** — **Auto-convert a question sequence into a native form** — pure client-side planner [`planFormConversion`](../frontend/apps/turing-app/src/app/console/chat-flow/chat-flow.form-converter.ts) walks the linear run of question-like nodes (`aiQue… (§VII.13.e)
- **T235** — **`formField.name` → slot picker (not free text) + lint** — `FormFieldsEditor` now picks the field's slot from the agent's catalog (`useAIAgentSlots`, same `Select` as `outputVariable`); an out-of-catalog legacy value is preserved as a "not… (§VII.13.f)
- **T236** — **LLM "tidy labels" for the form converter** — opt-in "Tidy labels with AI" button in the T234 convert-to-form dialog ([`flow-properties.tsx`](../frontend/apps/turing-app/src/app/console/chat-flow/components/flow-properties.tsx) `ConvertToFormDialog`) rewrites the raw labels the converter derived from each question's instruction (e.g. *"Qual o seu e-mail corporativo?"*) into short, clean form labels (*"E-mail"*) via the default LLM. New `POST /ai-agent/{agentId}/chat-flow/tidy-labels` → [`TurFormLabelTidyService`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/genai/flow/TurFormLabelTidyService.java) (mirrors the T97 variant pattern: `TurLlmSummaryService`, fresh random cache key + `regenerate=true`). Slot names + widget types are never touched; labels re-pair by name; nothing persists until the author confirms the conversion and saves. (§VII.13.g)
- **Headless UI foundation** — new package [`@viglet/turing-react-ui`](../frontend/packages/react-ui/) with the first design-agnostic component `TuringHtmlSandbox` (sandboxed `allow-scripts` iframe + fullscreen + optional code toggle; skinned via `classNames`/`labels`/`icons`). The admin `sandbox-player.tsx` and viglet.com `html-sandbox.tsx` are now thin skin adapters over it (zero duplicated logic; viglet.com stays react-sdk/axios-free). Plus the SDK pure helpers `splitRichContent`/`hasHtmlBlock`, `resolveSandboxUrl`/`isSandboxUrl`, `absolutizeArtifactUrls` in `@viglet/turing-sdk`. Roadmap → ROADMAP.md D.12, rationale → IMPROVEMENTS.md §XII.2. (§XII.2)
- **T295** — **`TuringRichContent`** in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` — shared headless segment renderer: splits a reply into markdown / ```html / ```d2 (SDK `splitRichContent` gained ```d2 support) and dispatches each — prose via an injectable `markdown` component, ```html via `TuringHtmlSandbox` (or an `html` override), ```d2 via an optional `d2` renderer (falls back to a markdown code fence until T297). Replaces the admin's `splitContent`+`AssistantContent` and viglet.com's inline `splitRichContent().map(...)`; both apps now share one dispatcher. Stays zero-runtime-dep (local splitter copy until the SDK is npm-published, T303). (§XII.2)
- **T296** — **`TuringMarkdown`** in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` — headless `react-markdown` wrapper, one markdown contract for both apps: a built-in `sandbox:` artifact `urlTransform` (resolve scheme + honour `baseUrl`, then `defaultUrlTransform` for XSS-safe sanitization), space-escaping for URLs with spaces, and injectable `remarkPlugins`/`rehypePlugins`/`components` + optional `className` wrapper. `react-markdown` is an optional peer dep (host provides it). Replaces the duplicated `encodeMarkdownLinkUrls`+`<ReactMarkdown>` in the admin chat list, the admin search `AiModeChat`, and viglet.com's `MarkdownBlock`. (§XII.2)
- **T297** — **`TuringD2Diagram`** in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` — headless renderer for ```d2 blocks (`render.md` already tells agents to emit them; nothing drew them before). Compiles the source to SVG via the optional `@terrastruct/d2` engine reached through a **lazy `import()`** — code-split into its own chunk and tree-shaken out of apps that never show a diagram; while compiling it shows a loading caption, and on a missing engine or invalid source it falls back to the raw source (lossless). Engine injectable via a `render` prop; skin via `classNames`/`labels`. Wired into `TuringRichContent`'s `d2` slot in the admin chat list (`D2Block` skin); the engine is an optional peer dep, added to `turing-app`. (§XII.2)
- **T299** — **`TuringChatMessage` shell** in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` — the slot-driven chat bubble (avatar · author name · status · attachments · content · actions · footer) that every chat surface rebuilt by hand. Every slot is a render-prop `ReactNode` that renders nothing (and skips its wrapper) when omitted; the row is tagged `data-role="user|assistant"` for CSS targeting and skinned per-slot via `classNames`. The fully-branded app shell (page layout, input bar, chip styling) stays per-app on purpose. The admin `chat-message-list.tsx` is now a thin skin over it (avatar/attachments/footer/status composed as slots; same look). (§XII.2)
- **T298** — **Chat atoms** in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` — three design-less primitives that surfaces re-implemented by hand: `TuringCopyButton` (`navigator.clipboard.writeText` + a self-resetting "copied" confirmation, `button`/`copied`/`idle` slots, state `icons`/`labels`, SSR/insecure-context guarded, unmount-safe timer), `TuringThinkingDots` (typing indicator — staggered `animation-delay` per dot while the app supplies the keyframe via `classNames.dot`; `role="status"` + `aria-label`), and `TuringCodeBlock` (`<pre><code>` + optional language header and a built-in `TuringCopyButton`; highlighter-agnostic `language-xxx` hint). Admin chat list's hand-rolled copy button now skins `TuringCopyButton`; viglet.com's local `ThinkingDots` now skins `TuringThinkingDots` (both keep their looks). (§XII.2)
- **T301** — **react-ui vitest (jsdom)** — first test runner for `@viglet/turing-react-ui` (vitest 4 + jsdom + RTL + jest-dom), with `TuringHtmlSandbox` render tests pinning the headless contract: iframe `sandbox="allow-scripts"` (never `allow-same-origin`) + self-contained `srcDoc`, fullscreen portal open / Esc / close-button, `classNames`/`labels`/`icons` passthrough (incl. the `className`→root alias and icon-replaces-label-text rule), and SSR-safety via `renderToStaticMarkup`. Standalone `vitest.config.ts`; tests excluded from the `tsc` `dist` build so the package stays publish-clean. (§XII.2)
- **T300** — **`@viglet/turing-react-sdk` re-exports `@viglet/turing-react-ui`** — single import surface for the admin: every headless component (`TuringHtmlSandbox`/`TuringRichContent`/`TuringMarkdown`/`TuringD2Diagram`/`TuringChatMessage`/`TuringCopyButton`/`TuringThinkingDots`/`TuringCodeBlock` + `splitRichContent`/`resolveSandboxUrl`) now flows through the SDK alongside the API hooks, so the admin imports UI and hooks from one package. `react-markdown`/`@terrastruct/d2` are declared as optional peers (mirroring react-ui; lazy-loaded), so the re-export adds no hard dep. react-ui stays axios-free — viglet.com keeps consuming it directly. Admin `chat-message-list.tsx`/`sandbox-player.tsx`/`ai-mode-chat.tsx` migrated to the single surface. (§XII.2)
- **T302** — **react-ui Storybook** — Storybook 10 (`@storybook/react-vite`) for `@viglet/turing-react-ui` with a **two-skin design contract**: every headless component has a *"Two skins, one implementation"* story rendering it side-by-side in a shadcn-neutral skin (admin console) and the blue→indigo Viglet brand skin (viglet.com), dressed only via `classNames`/`labels`/`icons` — the demo skins live in story-only `src/stories/skins.css`+`skins.tsx`, the package ships no styling. Stories for all 8 components (`TuringHtmlSandbox`/`TuringRichContent`/`TuringMarkdown`/`TuringD2Diagram`/`TuringCodeBlock`/`TuringCopyButton`/`TuringThinkingDots`/`TuringChatMessage`) + an `Introduction.mdx`; each has a `Playground` story with **Controls** (`argTypes`) and the **a11y/axe** panel (`@storybook/addon-a11y`) for the accessibility report. Stories excluded from the `tsc` `dist` build so the package stays publish-clean; root scripts `storybook:react-ui` / `build-storybook:react-ui`. (§XII.2)
- **T303** — **react-ui packaging hardening** — `@viglet/turing-react-ui` is now publish-ready for external embedders: a full `exports` subpath map (root + per-component deep imports like `@viglet/turing-react-ui/TuringHtmlSandbox`, each with `types`/`import`/`default`, plus `./package.json`) on top of the kept `main`/`module`/`types` legacy fields; `repository`/`author`/`engines` metadata. A **zero-dep gzip bundle-size budget** (`scripts/check-size.mjs`, run via `npm run size` + `prepublishOnly`) enforces a per-entry budget **and** a package-total ceiling — a new component without a budget, or a stale budget, fails the check. Bundle-size + tests wired into CI (`validate-react-ui.yml` on PRs touching the package; react-ui/sdk added to the `validate-js` matrix). **changesets** set up at the frontend root (`.changeset/config.json` covering the 3 publishable packages, ignoring apps/marketplace; root `changeset`/`version-packages`/`release` scripts) for per-package semver. New `publish-react-ui.yml` builds → size-gate → test → `npm publish`. The same `check-size.mjs` budget script was generalized to the other publishable packages (`@viglet/turing-react-sdk`, `@viglet/turing-sdk`). (§XII.2)
- **T305** — **react-ui optional token theming** — an alternative to per-slot `classNames`: a new opt-in stylesheet `@viglet/turing-react-ui/styles.css` (exported, copied into `dist/styles/` by `copy-assets.mjs` after `tsc`) themes every headless component via `--turing-ui-*` CSS custom properties. Rules are scoped under a `.turing-ui-theme` wrapper — importing the file is inert until a subtree opts in — and target the `data-turing-*` attributes each component already emits plus their stable structural DOM; every token has a baked-in default (works with zero tokens) and is overridable on the wrapper (or any ancestor — custom props inherit). Ships a typed surface (`TURING_UI_TOKENS`, `TURING_UI_THEME_CLASS`, `TuringUiTheme`/`TuringUiTokenName` from `tokens.ts`) for autocomplete + drift-protection. The `TuringHtmlSandbox` fullscreen portal gained a `data-turing-fullscreen` hook so the overlay themes too; a `prefers-reduced-motion` guard stops the thinking-dot bounce. New `tokens.test.tsx` pins the CSS↔TS↔component contract; Storybook "Token theming" story shows defaults/brand/dark presets. Bundle budget +`tokens.js` (1.1 kB), total 14→15 kB. (§XII.2)
- **T306** — **Move design-less search primitives to react-ui** — `TuringSearchBar` (pure `onSearch` + render-prop input/button), `TuringResultList` (render-prop rows + empty/loading slots, ARIA `list`/`listitem`) and `TuringPagination` (render-prop entries + ellipsis handling) migrated from `@viglet/turing-react-sdk` to `@viglet/turing-react-ui`. They touch no hook/API, so they moved whole; their structural prop types (`TuringResolvedDocument`/`TuringRawDocument`/`TuringPaginationItemData`) are **redeclared locally** rather than importing `@viglet/turing-sdk` core types, keeping the package zero-runtime-dep (structurally compatible with the SDK types, so no consumer churn). react-sdk re-exports all three via the T300 surface, so the marketplace apps + turing-app search build & render unchanged. `exports` subpaths + size budgets added (total 15→17 kB); jsdom render tests pin the headless contract. (§XII.2.a)
- **T307** — **Split `TuringSearchField` (headless view ⇄ hook wiring)** — the presentation half (the compound `Input`/`Button`/`Dropdown` + suggestions/history dropdown) moved to `@viglet/turing-react-ui` as `TuringSearchFieldView`, driven purely by props/context with the baked Tailwind defaults (`w-full px-4 py-2.5 hover:bg-gray-100 …`) **stripped** to honor the headless contract (visuals now come from `className` + the dropdown render props). The orchestrating Root (`useTuringAutoComplete` + `useTuringSearchHistory` + `useTuringUrlSearch` + the results→history save effect) stays in `@viglet/turing-react-sdk` as `TuringSearchField`, feeding the view its state/callbacks and re-attaching `.Input`/`.Button`/`.Dropdown` — so consumer markup (the 3 marketplace apps' `<TuringSearchField.Input/>`) is unchanged. Lets viglet.com reuse the search field axios-free. `exports` subpath + size budget added (total 17→18.75 kB); jsdom render tests pin the split contract. (§XII.2.a)
- **T308** — **Split `TuringWorkspacePanel` (view ⇄ SSE wiring)** — the presentation half (the `aside/ul/li` structure, the default download-link row, and the `formatBytes` helper) moved to `@viglet/turing-react-ui` as `TuringWorkspacePanelView`, taking `artifacts` + `status` (+ `title`/`itemComponent`/`emptyComponent`) as props with no SSE/API coupling. `TuringWorkspacePanel` stays in `@viglet/turing-react-sdk` as a one-call wrapper that subscribes via `useTuringWorkspace` and forwards the snapshot — same props, so consumers are unchanged. The artifact shape is **redeclared locally** in react-ui (structurally compatible with the SDK's `TurWorkspaceArtifact`) to keep the package zero-runtime-dep, letting viglet.com render the workspace list with its own Next transport. `exports` subpath + size budget added (total 18.75→20.31 kB); jsdom render tests pin the view + `formatBytes` contract. This completes the D.12.a search-UI primitive migration (T306–T308). (§XII.2.a)
- **T304** — **react-ui a11y + SSR pass** — the `TuringHtmlSandbox` fullscreen portal is now a labelled modal dialog (`role="dialog"` + `aria-modal` + `aria-label={title}`) with a **focus trap** (Tab/Shift+Tab cycle inside), initial focus moved into the dialog on open, and **focus restoration** to the trigger on close; the code/preview toggle gained an accessible name so it stays usable when shown icon-only. `TuringD2Diagram` exposes its loading state as an `aria-live="polite"` status, the rendered inline SVG as a labelled `role="img"` (new `labels.diagram`), and the source-fallback caption as `role="alert"`. `TuringCopyButton` announces its "Copied" state via `aria-live`. SSR/RSC-safety audited across all components — every `document`/`navigator`/`createPortal` access stays guarded (`typeof … === "undefined"`) and effect-scoped, so default renders are server-safe (`renderToStaticMarkup` covered). New focus-trap/dialog-ARIA render tests; `TuringHtmlSandbox.js` size budget bumped 2.6 → 3.2 kB for the trap logic. (§XII.2)

## D.8 — Document extraction & document AI

- **T99** — **OCR de CV (image)** — the `POST /chat/slot-extract` endpoint now accepts an image of a document (PNG / JPEG / GIF / WebP — e.g. a phone-photo of a CV) in addition to Tika-parseable files. Images carry no machine-readable text, so [`TurChatSlotExtractionService`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/service/chatslots/TurChatSlotExtractionService.java) detects `image/*` uploads and routes the bytes to the agent's vision LLM as a Spring AI `Media` block (reusing the same `buildSystemPrompt`/`parseJsonReply` contract as the text path, writing fills as `EXTRACT`) — no Tesseract/OCR dependency added. Both SDKs accept the image File unchanged (transparent; doc-only client change). (§VII.12.a)
- **T100** — **Per-agent extraction schema** — a slot can now declare itself a default document-extraction target via two new `TurAIAgentSlot` columns (Liquibase [`v2026.3.1.45`](../turing-app/src/main/resources/db/changelog/v2026.3.1.45.yaml)): `extract_from_document` (the flagged set *is* the agent's document schema, used when `/chat/slot-extract` is called with no `slotNames`) and `validation_pattern` (a Java regex an extracted value must fully match to be persisted — also surfaced to the LLM as a formatting hint). [`TurChatSlotExtractionService`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/service/chatslots/TurChatSlotExtractionService.java) resolves targets in precedence order (explicit names → flagged schema → whole catalog) and drops pattern-violating values; opt-in/back-compatible (an agent with no flagged slot keeps the legacy whole-catalog behaviour). Admin slot editor gains an "Extract from document" switch + "Validation pattern" field + a "Doc schema" badge (en/pt). (§VII.12.b)
- **T101** — **Multi-doc context** — new `POST /chat/slot-extract-multi` accepts several uploads at once and asks the agent's LLM to reason *across* them in a single call (headline use case: a CV + a job description → a synthesised `skill_gap` slot). [`TurChatSlotExtractionService.extractMulti`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/service/chatslots/TurChatSlotExtractionService.java) concatenates text documents under `=== Document N (filename) ===` delimiters (shared per-doc char budget) and attaches image documents as vision `Media` blocks in order, under a multi-doc system prompt (`buildMultiDocSystemPrompt`) that permits synthesis for analytical slots; reuses the T100 schema-resolution + validation. Empty files are skipped and a single non-empty file delegates to `extract` (byte-identical single-doc behaviour). Both SDKs gain `postSiteSlotExtractMulti` (parity contract updated). (§VII.12.c)
- **T102** — **Extraction confidence per field** — opt-in `confidence=true` request flag on `/chat/slot-extract` + `/chat/slot-extract-multi` switches the structured-output contract to `{value, confidence: 0–1}` per slot. [`TurChatSlotExtractionService`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/service/chatslots/TurChatSlotExtractionService.java) gains a confidence-aware prompt + tolerant parser (`parseJsonReplyWithConfidence`, accepts the object form, falls back to the plain-string form, clamps out-of-range confidences), and a shared `finishExtraction` tail across all three paths (text/image/multi-doc). The written slot value is still the scalar string; confidences come back in a new `confidences` map on the response (empty when not requested — fully back-compatible). Both SDKs surface `confidences` on `TurChatSlotExtractResponse`, a `confidence` param on both extract wrappers, and a `LOW_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD = 0.7` constant for consuming UIs to flag low-confidence fills for review. (§VII.12.d)
- **T103** — **Native binary document passthrough** — opt-in `nativeBinary=true` flag on `/chat/slot-extract` + `/chat/slot-extract-multi` sends a PDF/DOCX (and any non-image binary) to the agent's LLM as a raw Spring AI `Media` document block — preserving tables/layout — instead of flattening it to text with Tika. Generalises the T99 image path (`extractFromImage` → `extractFromBinary`, used by both images and binaries); on an empty/failed native call the single-doc path **falls back to Tika**. Whether the bytes ride inline or via a provider's Files API is decided by the configured `TurGenAiLlmProvider` (Anthropic/Gemini/OpenAI accept document input; others fall back) — there is no per-call provider switch. Both SDKs replace the positional `confidence` arg with a `SlotExtractOptions { confidence?, nativeBinary? }` object on the extract wrappers. (§VII.12.e) — completes block D.8.
- **T431** — **Per-LLM-instance file-upload capability flag** — a `fileUploadEnabled` boolean on `TurLLMInstance` (Liquibase [`v2026.3.1.46`](../turing-app/src/main/resources/db/changelog/v2026.3.1.46.yaml), default false, mirrors `tools_enabled`) lets the admin decide which providers may receive raw document binaries. The T103 binary passthrough now fires when the request opts in (`nativeBinary=true`) **OR** the agent's resolved LLM instance has `fileUploadEnabled` on — so an admin can enable native document input once on the LLM and every extraction uses it (still Tika-fallback-safe). `TurChatSlotExtractionService` resolves the instance via `resolveLlmInstance` and computes the effective passthrough; the admin LLM form + bento form gain a "File Upload Enabled" switch (en/pt). The bytes still ride as an inline Spring AI `Media` document block (Anthropic/Gemini/OpenAI document input); the upload-and-reference Files API proper remains a native-seam follow-up. (§VII.12.e)

## D.11 — Tracked follow-ups

- **T242** — **CI nightly pipeline for `-Pllm-it`** — new [`nightly-llm-it.yml`](../.github/workflows/nightly-llm-it.yml) runs the real-LLM ITs excluded from every-PR CI on a nightly schedule (+ `workflow_dispatch`), as a matrix over the three gated profiles (`-Pllm-it` / `-Pagent-eval` / `-Pnl-facet-eval`) with the `OPENAI_API_KEY` secret injected; warns (not fails) when the secret is absent since the ITs self-skip, and uploads failsafe reports. (§VI.6)
- **T244** — **Block A "cascade ascent" IT body** — implemented the last deferred Block A harness skeleton: `cascadesAscentThroughChainOfTerminatedSubFlows_landsOnTopLevelNextNode` in [`TurAgentChatExecutorContractIT`](../turing-app/src/test/java/com/viglet/turing/genai/TurAgentChatExecutorContractIT.java). A depth-3 chain A→B→C of pass-through sub-flows drives two cascade ascents in a single `loadOrInitState`, landing on A's interactive node after the SubFlow node — deterministic, no LLM, no fixture/import-util (the programmatic build replaces the originally-scoped `nested-sub-flow.chat-flow.json`). Completes removal of all Block A `@Disabled` skeletons. (§0 Block A)
- **T239** — **Code Interpreter sandbox hardening (gVisor + seccomp)** — two opt-in `docker run` flags on the T80 sandbox, both off by default (the baseline `--cap-drop ALL` + `no-new-privileges` + `--read-only` + Docker's built-in seccomp is unchanged): `turing.code-interpreter.docker.runtime` (e.g. `runsc` for gVisor user-space-kernel isolation) → `--runtime`, and `turing.code-interpreter.docker.seccomp-profile` (host path) → `--security-opt seccomp=<profile>`. Flag construction extracted to the testable static [`appendExtraSandboxHardening`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/genai/tool/TurCodeInterpreterToolService.java) (4 unit cases). Verified against Docker 29.5.3: the seccomp path runs a container under a supplied profile end-to-end; the gVisor flag plumbs through (fails fast as "unknown runtime" when `runsc` isn't host-installed, as documented). gVisor mediates syscalls itself, so a custom seccomp profile is unnecessary under it. (§VII.2.h / §VIII)
- **T237** — **Full path-aware funnel** — opt-in per-turn node-visit log (`turing.chat.analytics.node-visit-log.enabled`, default off) closes the T85 funnel gap. The engine appends the node each turn settles on to a `nodeVisitPath` accumulator on `chat_flow_state` (consecutive-dup collapsed, capped 200, via the pure [`TurChatFlowNodeVisitPath`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/genai/flow/TurChatFlowNodeVisitPath.java)) and snapshots it onto `chat_flow_submission` at flow end (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.44`). [`TurChatFlowFunnelService`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/service/chatanalytics/TurChatFlowFunnelService.java) aggregates those terminal paths into per-node `pathReached`/`pathContinued` (→ drop-off), branch-tolerant; zero when the log is off, so the funnel cleanly falls back to the V1 cursor/completed view. Funnel panel shows a per-node drop-off bar + `reached→continued` when paths exist. Verified by `TurChatFlowNodeVisitPathTest` + new `TurChatFlowFunnelServiceTest` path cases. (§VII.10.b)
- **T238** — **Conversation replay scrubber** — the T86 replay timeline in the chat-analytics `DetailPanel` gained playback controls: play/pause auto-advance (one event per ~900ms), prev/next stepping, a position slider, a "Show all" reset, and a ring highlight on the current event. Defaults to "show all" so the static timeline is unchanged until the operator scrubs; resets when the open conversation changes. Frontend-only over T86's interleaved message+slot-audit events. (§VII.10.c)
- **T240** — **Sub-agent session-detail surfaces** — the chat-analytics conversation `DetailPanel` now renders a "Sub-agent orchestration" card linking a session to its T110 orchestration graph: the parent orchestrator (from the transcript session's `parentConversationId`) and the child sub-agent invocations (sessions whose `parentConversationId` matches, resolved from the loaded window), each clickable to drill in-place. Presentation-only glue over the already-captured T110 data; no backend change. (§IX.2.c)
- **T241** — **Per-experiment success-metric override** — an experiment can now declare which conversion the T70 bandit optimises for (and the significance test defaults to) via a new `TurChatFlow.experimentSuccessMetric` (Liquibase `experimentSuccessMetric` column `v2026.3.1.43`, nullable = `GOAL_ACHIEVED`). [`TurAbBanditService`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/service/chatanalytics/TurAbBanditService.java) resolves the experiment-wide metric from the first declaring arm and threads it into `collectVariantStats`; [`TurExperimentSignificanceService`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/service/chatanalytics/TurExperimentSignificanceService.java) gained `SuccessMetric.fromNameOrDefault` and an **enum-driven `isSuccess`** (uses the enum's `storeColumn`/`matchValue` instead of a hard-coded switch — so the override actually selects a different conversion and the handoff/lead metrics light up automatically once the analytics store persists those columns). Admin chat-flow A/B settings gained a success-metric selector (gated on an experiment key). (§VII.8.a)
- **T294** — **Outbound MCP per-server transport (SSE | Streamable HTTP)** — added a `transportType` field on `TurMcpServer` (+ domain record `isStreamableHttp()`, Liquibase `transport_type` column `v2026.3.1.42`, nullable = legacy SSE) so each HTTP MCP server opts into the SSE two-endpoint protocol or the newer single-endpoint Streamable HTTP. [`TurMcpToolCallbackService.createTransport`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/genai/tool/TurMcpToolCallbackService.java) now branches to `HttpClientStreamableHttpTransport` when selected; SSE stays the default (existing servers unaffected). Admin MCP form gains an HTTP-only transport selector (en/pt). (§D.11)
- **T243** — **Shared `useStreamingChatCore` hook** — extracted the streaming/bubble/abort state machine duplicated by `useTuringChat` and `useTuringLlmChat` into [`streaming-chat-core.ts`](../frontend/packages/react-sdk/src/hooks/streaming-chat-core.ts) (`ChatMessage`/`ChatStatus` + `newId`/`patchMessage`/`isAbortError` + `useStreamingChatCore`, which owns the message list, status/error, the request counter, aborted flag, per-send `AbortController`, synchronous `messagesRef`, and the begin-send → open-bubble → stream-token → patch-final primitives). Both hooks now compose it (the chat hook layers persistence, controlled conversation ids, native forms, and the site/agent transport split on top); their public API is unchanged (`ChatMessage`/`ChatStatus` re-exported from `use-turing-chat`). Verified by the admin chat smoke suite. (§XII.1)

## E.1 — Planning & decomposition (parity)

- **T108** — **`planningStep` + `iteratePlan` node types** — LLM emits a typed TODO list into a designated slot (`__plan` by convention), downstream `iteratePlan` nodes consume items one by one and mark complete. (§IX.2.a)
- **T109** — **`agent.invoke(agentId, message, opts)` Custom Tool helper** — new [`TurCustomToolAgentHelper`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/genai/tool/TurCustomToolAgentHelper.java) bound to Groovy scripts as the `agent` variable. (§IX.2.b)
- **T110** — **Sub-agent scorecard rows ✅ shipped 2026-05-26 (backend + UI dimension picker)**. (§IX.2.c)

## E.2 — Agent Workspace (the key differentiator)

- **T111** — **`TurAgentWorkspace` service ✅ shipped** — per-conversation key-value-blob store backed by `TurStorageService` (already pluggable: NoOp / filesystem / MinIO). (§IX.3.a)
- **T112** — **`workspace` helper for Custom Tool Groovy ✅ shipped** — new Groovy binding [`TurCustomToolWorkspaceHelper`](../turing-app/src/main/java/com/viglet/turing/genai/tool/TurCustomToolWorkspaceHelper.java) bound as `workspace` by [`TurCustomToo… (§IX.3.b)
- **T113** — **Workspace SSE bus ✅ shipped** — live "files this agent built for you" stream. (§IX.3.c)
- **T114** — **Auto-offload large tool results ✅ shipped** — `TurToolCallbackPipeline` wraps each tool with `TurToolResultOffloadCallback`: a result over `turing.genai.tool-result-offload.inline-max-chars` (default 4096) is written to the agent workspace at `tool-results/{toolName}-{ts}.json` and replaced inline with a `workspace://` reference; the always-present `workspace_read` tool pulls it back on demand. Active only when storage is enabled, so `turing.storage.type=none` deployments are unchanged. (§IX.3.d)
- **T115** — **Memory compression to workspace ✅ shipped** — `TurChatMemoryCompressionService` (wired into `TurChatPromptAssembler`): when an agent opts in (`chatMemoryCompressionEnabled`) and the older pool (turns past the T30 recent-N window) exceeds `chatMemoryCompressionThresholdTokens` (default 8192), those turns are summarized in one cheap LLM call (`chatMemoryCompressionLlmId` or the Global Settings default), stored at `memory/summary-1-{to}.md` in the conversation workspace, and replaced by a compact summary block in the prompt. Throttled to once per `chatMemoryCompressionInterval` (default PT1H) per conversation; composes with T30 relevance retrieval. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.19`; round-trips on agent export/import + agent settings UI. (§IX.3.e)
- **T309** — **Async memory compression (off the hot path) ✅ shipped** — the T115 summary call no longer blocks a chat turn. `chatMemoryCompressionAsync` (default true) routes regeneration through `TurChatMemoryCompressionWorker` (`@Async` on a bounded `turChatMemoryCompressionExecutor` pool, deduped per conversation via an in-flight guard): the triggering turn proceeds uncompressed (reusing the last summary if present) and the next turn picks up the fresh one. Set false to keep the synchronous T115 behavior. Job logic extracted to `generateAndStore(CompressionJob)`; service↔worker cycle broken with `@Lazy`. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.20`; round-trips on export/import + settings UI.

## E.3 — Skills as first-class (differentiator)

- **T316** — **Scaffold native tools ✅ shipped** — `TurScaffoldToolService` (registered in `TurNativeToolService`) exposes three deterministic, dependency-free `@Tool`s: `scaffold_markdown` (beat-structured md skeleton), `scaffold_pick_quote` (longest-sentence-with-term heuristic, default min 40 chars), `scaffold_build_mind_map` (Mermaid mindmap from top-N stop-word-filtered terms) — the §VII.4 helpers, as real native tools (snake_case, with `prompts/tools/scaffold/*.md` descriptions). Generic and skill-independent (survived the T326 rollback). (§VII.4)
- **T326** — **Roll back the T116/T117/T118 skill entity ✅ shipped** — removed the decomposed `TurSkill` entity/manifest/catalog/API + `TurSkillOnStartup`, the marketplace "skill" kind, the `/skill` CRUD (list/form/detail) and its react query/service/model/i18n, and the three bundled `*.skill.manifest.json`; Liquibase `v2026.3.1.22` drops the `skill` table. Clean slate for the folder-in-storage model (T317+). T316's scaffold tools stay as generic native tools. (§IX.4.d)
- **T317** — **Skill = folder-in-storage + thin index ✅ shipped** — a skill is now an Anthropic-compatible folder (`SKILL.md` at root) in object storage; the recreated `skill` table (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.23`) is a thin catalog index (`name/description/version/author/path/enabled`) parsed from the `SKILL.md` YAML frontmatter. `TurSkillFrontmatterParser` (SnakeYAML, lenient — never throws) + `TurSkillCatalogService.reindex()` walk `turing.storage.skillsPath` (default `skills/`), upsert each folder (preserving `enabled`/id) and prune rows whose folder lost its `SKILL.md`. `TurSkillAPI` (`/api/skill`: list/get/skill-md/reindex/enabled) + `skillsEnabled` feature flag (= storage). Gated on storage. Foundation for T318–T325. (§IX.4.d)
- **T318** — **Skill folder editor (mini-VS-Code) ✅ shipped** — new `/admin/skill` route: the list shows only skill names; clicking one opens a file-tree + CodeMirror editor (`skill.page.tsx` + `skill-editor.page.tsx`). `TurSkillFileService` does file CRUD (list tree / read / write / createFolder / rename / delete) + `createSkill` (starter `SKILL.md`), all **scoped to the skill root** — caller-supplied paths are relative and run through `safeRelative()` (rejects `..`/absolute/back-slash), so the editor cannot navigate above the skill's folder. `SKILL.md` edits trigger a reindex. New endpoints on `TurSkillAPI` (`POST /skill`, `{id}/files`, `{id}/file` GET/PUT/DELETE, `{id}/folder`, `{id}/rename`). Gated on storage. (§IX.4.d)
- **T319** — **Import skill ZIP (one or many) ✅ shipped** — `POST /api/skill/import` (`TurSkillFileService.importZip`) unpacks each top-level folder containing a `SKILL.md` into `skills/` and reindexes; a single ZIP may carry many skills, and a root-level `SKILL.md` is treated as one skill (destination slug from frontmatter `name`, deduped). Skips `__MACOSX`/hidden entries. "Import ZIP" button on `/admin/skill`. (§IX.4.d)
- **T320** — **Export skill → Anthropic-compatible ZIP ✅ shipped** — `GET /api/skill/{id}/export` (`TurSkillFileService.exportZip`, in-memory) serializes a skill folder into a `.zip` with every file wrapped under one top-level `{slug}/` folder — uploads to Claude and round-trips back through `importZip` (verified by `exportThenImportRoundTrips`). Frontend `TurSkillService.download()` (blob → anchor); Export action on each `/admin/skill` card + in the editor header. Completes the §IX.4.d interop boundary (entry door T319 + exit door T320). (§IX.4.d)
- **T321** — **Anthropic-compatible Docker session (skill sandbox engine) ✅ shipped** — evolves the Code Interpreter from one-shot snippet execution to a persistent, interactive container **session** the model drives across turns. `TurSkillSandboxSessionManager` keys a session by `(agentId, conversationId, skillId)`, materializes the skill folder once into a stable host dir (mounted read-only at `/skill`) and keeps a persistent `workspace/` (mounted read-write at `/workspace`) that survives between turns. `TurSkillSandboxService.runBash` runs each command in a fresh **hardened** container (`--rm --network none --memory/--cpus/--pids-limit --cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges --read-only` + tmpfs `/tmp`, workspace the only writable mount) via `bash -lc`, returning a structured `TurSkillSandboxResult`. **Gated on `DOCKER` mode + storage** (no native fallback — that's the escape vector containerization closes). New `GLOBAL_CODE_INTERPRETER_SKILL_IMAGE` setting (python+node+bash, default `python:3.12-slim`) editable in Global Settings; `TurSkillSandboxCleanupTask` reaps idle sessions by mtime; `GET/POST /api/skill/{id}/sandbox/status|exec` exercises the engine. Engine for T322 (activation harness). (§IX.4.d)
- **T322** — **Skill activation harness (progressive disclosure) ✅ shipped** — `TurSkillActivationHarness` (`genai/skill/activation/`) is the seam that makes a skill *run*, in three cheap-then-deep levels mirroring Anthropic's disclosure model: (1) `buildSystemPromptBlock(skills)` injects each offered skill's `name`+`description` only — a few tokens each — with the activation protocol; (2) the `load_skill` tool (`TurLoadSkillToolCallback`) returns a chosen skill's full `SKILL.md` body on demand; (3) the `skill_bash` tool (`TurSkillBashToolCallback`) drives that skill's T321 sandbox session (`/skill` read-only, persistent `/workspace`) so the model reads `references/`, runs `scripts/`, and writes drafts. Both tools are constrained to the offered set, read agent/conversation scope from the Spring AI `ToolContext` (same keys as `workspace_read`), and return errors as text rather than throwing. `prompts/tools/skill/{load_skill,skill_bash}.md` descriptions. Gated on the sandbox being available (storage + `DOCKER`) — returns empty prompt block + no tools otherwise, so non-Docker prompts are unchanged. Generic seam wired into chat (T323) and semantic navigation (T324) next; callers decorate via `TurToolCallbackPipeline`. (§IX.4.d)
- **T323** — **Use skill in chat ✅ shipped** — per-agent opt-in `TurAIAgent.skillsEnabled` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.24`) offers every enabled, indexed skill to the agent's chat turns. **Design decision: a skill always runs on the Global Settings Default LLM, never the agent's own model** — so a portable skill behaves identically regardless of caller. Implemented as a *delegated sub-loop*: the parent turn (agent's model) sees only the cheap progressive-disclosure block (skill names+descriptions) + a single `run_skill(skill, task)` tool (`TurRunSkillToolCallback`); calling it routes to `TurSkillRunnerService`, which resolves the Default LLM, seeds a focused single-skill sub-conversation reusing the T322 harness (its block as the system message + `load_skill`/`skill_bash` as the toolset), drives `TurToolExecutionLoop` to completion, records token usage, and returns the result. Sandbox session keyed by the same `(agentId, conversationId, skillId)` so `/workspace` persists across turns. Wired into `TurChatPromptAssembler` (prompt block) + `TurChatToolResolver` (`run_skill` added + protected from the BM25 pre-filter). Gated on storage + `DOCKER` + a configured Default LLM (no-op otherwise → existing agents byte-for-byte unchanged). Frontend toggle in agent settings (gated on the `skillsEnabled` feature flag). Because the SN AI chat shares this executor, SN turns inherit skill activation automatically (T324 adds Turing-search-as-a-sandbox-tool). (§IX.4.d)
- **T325** — **Multi-skill ZIP as independent units ✅ shipped** — completes §IX.4.d. Two halves. (1) *Independent units*: already true via T319 import — `TurSkillFileService.importZip` splits every top-level folder carrying a `SKILL.md` into its own `skills/{slug}/` folder, each indexed as its own `TurSkill` and independently activatable via the T322 progressive-disclosure block. (2) *Selectable in chat as a distinct mode/"flow"*: a new optional `selectedSkillId` rides the chat request (mirroring `flowId`/`forcedVariant`) and threads through `TurAgentChatExecutor` → `TurChatPromptAssembler` + `TurChatToolResolver`. New `TurSkillRunnerService.offeredSkills(selectedSkillId)`: blank/null offers the full enabled set (legacy progressive disclosure); a pin (by id or case-insensitive name) narrows the turn's prompt block **and** `run_skill` tool to that one skill — an unknown/disabled pin yields empty (a pinned mode can't silently widen). The skill still runs on the Global Settings Default LLM (T323 rule). SDK: `selectedSkillId` on `PostAgentChatOptions` (both SDKs) + `UseTuringChatOptions`. Admin chat console gains a "Skill mode" selector beside the flow selector (shown only when the agent has `skillsEnabled` and the catalog has enabled skills). All paths default to null → byte-for-byte-unchanged legacy behaviour. (§IX.4.d)
- **T324** — **Use skill in semantic navigation ✅ shipped** — two parts. (1) *Activation in SN*: free — the SN AI chat shares `TurAgentChatExecutor`, so T323's per-agent `skillsEnabled` toggle already activates skills in SN mode (on the Default LLM, per the T323 rule). (2) *Turing search as a skill tool*: `TurSkillRunnerService.runSkill` now adds the Semantic Navigation DSL toolset (`TurDslToolService` → `dsl_list_indices` / `dsl_get_mappings` / `dsl_search` / `dsl_get_document` / `dsl_suggest` / `dsl_get_shards`, the same surface `TurLLMSemanticChatAPI` exposes) to the skill's Default-LLM sub-loop, alongside `load_skill`/`skill_bash`, all decorated together through `TurToolCallbackPipeline`. This is the first place a skill leverages Turing's own indexed data ("integrate, via standard tools"): the tools run in-process (no container network needed) and the model bridges results into the sandbox `/workspace` via `skill_bash`. The sub-loop system prompt now tells the model the search tools exist. Gating unchanged (storage + `DOCKER` + Default LLM). (§IX.4.d)

## E.4 — Human-in-the-loop (parity, made graph-visible)

- **T119** — **`humanApproval` node type** — new chat-flow node that pauses the flow, fires a notification on a configured channel (`email`/`slack`/`webhook`), persists a pending-approval record, and returns a resume URL. New entity `TurChatHumanApproval` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.39`, status `PENDING|DECIDED|TIMED_OUT|CANCELLED`, unguessable `resumeToken`); `TurHumanApprovalService` (idempotent `ensurePending` + `recordDecision` + timeout resolution), `TurHumanApprovalNotifier` (email via `TurEmailService`, Slack/webhook via the shared `VigletWebhookDispatcher`/`TurChatWebhookService`), `TurHumanApprovalNodeExecutor` (engine seam). The engine's `walkTransparentNodes` parks on the node and advances once the operator's decision lands in the approval slot; `resumeSuspendedFlow` + the parked-conversations dashboard + `getConversationState` treat `humanApproval` as a parked node alongside `suspend`. Public token endpoints `GET/POST /api/genai/approval/{token}` (permitAll, token = capability) + authenticated `GET /api/genai/approvals`; `TurHumanApprovalTimeoutSweepJob` (`@SchedulerLock`) auto-resolves expired approvals per `timeoutBehavior` (`auto_reject` default). Flow editor: rose `humanApproval` node + palette entry + property panel (channel/target/template/slot/timeout). Config under `turing.genai.human-approval.*` (`base-url`, `sweep-cron`, `default-timeout-seconds`). (§IX.5.a)
- **T120** — **Spectator + Co-pilot mode** — the live, in-the-loop complement to T119's async approval. An operator opens `/console/conversation/{id}?spectate=true` and watches a conversation they did not initiate over three conversation-scoped SSE buses: a new `TurChatMessageEventBus` (the chat-transcript analogue of the slot/workspace buses — `TurChatStreamingDispatcher.recordTurnTelemetry` tees every completed turn onto it), plus the existing slot + workspace buses re-exposed conversation-scoped on `TurChatSessionAPI` (`GET /api/chat/sessions/{id}/spectate/{stream,slots/stream,workspace/stream}` + `/state`, admin-secured, 25s heartbeats, `TurChatSlotSseRegistry.Mode.SPECTATE`). Toggling co-pilot ("take the wheel") posts `POST /api/chat/sessions/{id}/manual-turn` → `TurCopilotService`: the LLM step is **skipped** and the operator's text becomes the assistant message, but the turn still runs the same pre-LLM flow-advance (`advance(..., assistantMessage=null)`) + chat-memory + analytics path a normal turn does, so slots/cursor/analytics stay consistent; the reply teed onto the message bus (flagged `manual=true`) reaches the visitor and any spectator. V1 is operator-initiated (does not preempt a visitor's in-flight LLM call). Frontend: lazy `/console/conversation/:id` spectator page (live transcript + slots + workspace panes + co-pilot composer) reached via a "Spectate" link on the parked-conversations dashboard. In-memory bus, single-node caveat shared with the slot/workspace buses; no schema change. (§IX.5.b)
- **T410** — **Chaotic chatflow HITL coverage** — added a first-class `humanApproval` node type to `@viglet/turing-flow-dsl` (`NodeType` + `HumanApprovalNode`/`HumanApprovalConfig` carrying `channel`/`target`/`template`/`approvalSlot`/`timeoutSeconds`/`timeoutBehavior` nested under `data.humanApproval`; transpile label + data whitelist) and wove a dedicated **Absurd Approval Bureau** sub-flow (`chaos-approval`, reachable from the department `subFlowSwitch`) into the chaotic stress fixture — so the engine's T119 parking path (`ensurePending` → notify → decision-slot → resume) is exercised deterministically alongside the generic `suspend` gate, sub-flows, conditions and switches. `TurChaoticChatFlowEngineIT` gains `approvalFlow_parksOnHumanApprovalNode_thenDecisionResumesPastIt` (parks on the node, then `resumeSuspendedFlow` writing the `operator_decision` slot advances past it). **Cache bug fixed en route:** `TurRagRerankCacheTest`'s nested `@Configuration` was leaking a fixed-name `ConcurrentMapCacheManager("turRagRerankScore")` into the `com.viglet.turing` component scan, replacing the auto-creating cache manager in *every* full-context `@SpringBootTest` and failing them all on startup with `Cannot find cache named 'turConfigVarfindById'`; moved it to `com.viglet.testsupport.cache.TurRagRerankCacheTestConfig` (outside the scan) and added an explicit `@Primary` auto-creating `TurCacheConfig` (Hazelcast-backed when present, else dynamic `ConcurrentMapCacheManager`). Also corrected `TurConfigVarOnStartupTest`'s stale default-config-var count (31 → 32).

## E.5 — Long-horizon task primitives

- **T121** — **`suspend` node + `POST /chat/resume`** — new chat-flow node type `suspend` parks the cursor indefinitely; the engine's `walkTransparentNodes` recognises the type and returns the state unchanged (cursor stays on the suspend node turn after… (§IX.6.a)
- **T122** — **Parked-conversations dashboard** — `TurParkedConversationService` scans flow graphs for `suspend` nodes (parsed once per flow) + loads only the matching `TurChatFlowState` rows via `findByFlow_IdAndCurrentNodeIdIn`; admin `GET /api/system/parked-conversations` + `POST /{id}/resume` (delegates to T121's `resumeSuspendedFlow`); React page at `/parked-conversations` (reason badge, waiting time, unblock action, sidebar entry, 15s poll). (§IX.6.b)

## E.6 — Context engineering enforcement

- **T123** — **Token-budget enforcement per turn (COMPACT mode completed)** — `overBudgetBehavior=COMPACT` now actually compacts instead of degrading to WARN. `TurTokenBudgetService.Decision` gains a `COMPACT` value; when the assembled prompt is over the agent's `maxPromptTokens`, the executor routes it through the new `TurPromptCompactor`, which keeps the leading system message(s) + the recent-N tail verbatim and folds the older middle turns into a single LLM summary via `TurChatMemoryCompressionService.summarizeText(...)` (reuses the T115 `prompts/chat-memory-compression.md` prompt + LLM-instance resolution), then proceeds best-effort even if still over budget. No-op (uncompacted, logged) when there are no older turns or the summarizer returns blank. WARN/ERROR paths unchanged; `maxPromptTokens=0` still disables the check. (§IX.7.a)
- **T124** — **Workspace + memory + tool-offload metrics** — extended `TurChatPipelineObservation` with the three context-engineering levers: `turing.chat.workspace.{puts,gets,bytes_stored}` (counters, wired in `TurAgentWorkspaceService.put/get`), `turing.chat.memory.compression_ratio` (distribution summary of compressed/original chars, recorded by both T115 compression and T123 compaction), and `turing.chat.tools.offloaded_bytes` (counter, wired in the T114 `TurToolResultOffloadCallback` via an optional observation threaded through `TurToolCallbackPipeline`). Metric names added to `TurMeterNames`. (§IX.7.b)

## E.7 — Developer experience (CLI + eval)

- **T125** — **`turing` CLI** — new `@viglet/turing-cli` package (the `turing` command). Zero runtime deps (Node 22+ built-ins), `tsc`-only build, mirroring `@viglet/turing-flow-dsl`. `init <name>` scaffolds an agent project (`agent.json`, `flows/`, `tools/`, `skills/`, `evals/`, `turing.config.json`, a minimal `docker-compose.dev.yml`); `dev` boots a local stack via Docker Compose (`--watch` redeploys on change); `deploy` pushes the agent (create-then-update, persisting the new id back to `turing.config.json`), chat-flows (atomic `chat-flow/import-bundle`), custom tools (`.groovy` + optional `.tool.json` sidecar, match-by-title PUT/POST), and skills (each folder zipped by a built-in store-method ZIP writer → `POST /api/skill/import`); `logs --conversation <id>` tails live chat events over the spectator SSE stream (`--slots` adds the slot stream). Shared `TuringClient` (fetch + CSRF/session, dev-token `Key:` header or HTTP Basic) and `turing.config.json` → env → flag connection resolution. (§IX.8.a)
- **T126** — **`turing eval` — YAML regression suite** — `*.eval.yaml` fixtures define N conversations with per-step assertions: `assistant.matches`/`not_matches`/`contains`/`not_contains` (over the streamed reply), `slot.<name>` (via `/chat-slots`), `tool_called`/`tool_not_called` (via the T60 slot-audit `TOOL` writes), `persona.forbidden`/`required`, and `node` (cursor via `/state`). Accepts both the flat dotted form and a nested `assert:` block. A dependency-free YAML reader (block maps/sequences, quoted scalars, inline flow lists, comments) keeps the package zero-dep; the runner is split into a pure scorer (unit-tested with a fake backend) + a `ClientEvalBackend` over the chat + session-inspection endpoints. `turing eval [path] [--watch]`; exit 1 on any failure. (§IX.8.b)
- **T127** — **`turing eval record`** — `turing eval record <conversationId>` pulls a conversation's full snapshot from `GET /api/chat/sessions/{id}/export` and freezes it as an `*.eval.yaml` fixture (visitor turns → `user:` steps, captured slots → a final `assert.slot.*` block) for the author to tighten by hand. (§IX.8.c)
- **T427** — **Per-conversation tool-call trace** — read-only `GET /api/system/chat-analytics/sessions/{id}/tool-calls` returning the tools that actually executed in a conversation (name, redacted arg digest, ok/err, `durationMs`), so `turing eval`'s `assert.tool_called` is exact rather than inferred from slot-audit `TOOL` writes — plus a per-conversation tool timeline for observability. Captured at the existing `TurLoggingToolCallback` seam into a per-turn `TurToolCallCollector` (published in the tool context, mirroring `TurRagSourceCollector`) and drained after the tool loop into a bounded process-local `TurToolCallTraceService` (access-ordered LRU over conversations + a ring per conversation). Shares the `TurChatToolCall` shape with the T436 live event so a live event and the read-only trace describe each call identically. (§IX.8.a)

## E.8 — Observability interop

- **T128** — **LangSmith run mirror** — opt-in `turing.observability.langsmith.*` exporter (`TurLangSmithExporter`) mirrors each completed chat turn as a run in a LangSmith project via the public ingest API (`POST {api-url}/runs/batch`, `x-api-key`). Wired at the single per-turn seam in `TurLLMTokenUsageService.recordUsage` (covers every chat path) and dispatched fire-and-forget through the shared, retrying `VigletWebhookDispatcher` so a slow/unreachable LangSmith never stalls or breaks a turn (fail-open). **Metadata-only by design** — provider, model, token counts, cost, agent and cost-stage — never message/reply content, keeping conversation PII out of a third-party trace store. Disabled (or a blank API key) is a pure no-op. (§IX.9.a)
- **T129** — **Chat-pipeline OpenTelemetry span export** — opt-in `turing.observability.spans.enabled` makes `TurChatPipelineObservation.record(...)` wrap each stage in a Micrometer `Observation` (mirroring `TurLlmObservation`), so every `STAGE_CHAT_*` stage becomes an OTel span via the existing micrometer-tracing OTel bridge, exported to any OTLP backend (Honeycomb, Datadog, Jaeger) when `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` is set. The timer metric is recorded identically either way (zero dashboard regression); the LLM call already has its own `turing.llm.calls` span. (§IX.9.b)

## E.9 — Tool-selection quality (MCP / native disambiguation)

- **T424** — **Trigger/namespace-aware tool prioritization** — two fixes to the §IV.2 pre-filter so a weak model (e.g. `gpt-4o-mini`) reliably reaches an attached MCP server's tools instead of a semantically-close native one. (1) **Anti-starvation quota floor** in `TurToolPreFilterService`: when the always-keep native set alone meets/exceeds `topK` (real incident: 24 natives, `topK=10` → quota `≤ 0`), the pre-filter no longer silently drops *every* operator-attached MCP/custom tool — it reserves up to `turing.genai.tool-prefilter.reserve-attached` slots for the top-scoring attached tools (only in that starved case; a positive quota still respects `topK`). (2) **Trigger boost** in `TurChatToolResolver`: when the user message matches an attached MCP server's `llmInstructions` trigger words, that server's tools are added to the always-keep set for the turn (and overlapping built-in `dsl_*`/native tools can be demoted) so list ordering — never a reliable LLM priority signal — isn't the lever. Also adds `TurConversationLock` to guard concurrent conversation-state mutation (lost-update prevention). (was §XXI)
- **T425** — **`mcp` binding for Custom Tools** — new `mcp` helper in the Groovy custom-tool sandbox (`TurCustomToolMcpHelper`, alongside `http`/`turingSearch`/`slots`/`workspace`/`code`/`agent`): `mcp.call("dspace_search_items", [query: term])` resolves the active agent's attached MCP servers and invokes the named tool, returning its raw (usually JSON) result. A Custom Tool now reuses the *one* MCP integration already configured on the agent instead of a parallel direct-REST call — no duplicated endpoint/auth and no JVM-side WAF fight (the Insper Akamai WAF 403s the JDK `Java/<v>` UA but allowlists the MCP client's `python-httpx`). No-op when there's no agent context / no attached servers.

- **T232** — **Admin smoke ITs (RTL)** — bootstrapped the **frontend's first test runner** (none existed in the monorepo): vitest 3.2.4 + @testing-library/react 16 + jsdom + fake-indexeddb, a standalone [vitest.config.ts](../frontend/apps/turing-app/vit… (§XII.2)

## Block I — Turing as an MCP Server (§XIII)

- **T245** — **MCP server transport (loopback first)** — added `spring-ai-starter-mcp-server-webmvc`; exposes the decorated Turing tool catalog at `/mcp` over Streamable HTTP via a `ToolCallbackProvider` built from `TurToolCallbackPipeline.decorate(...)` over the same `TurDslToolService` the internal Semantic-Navigation agent uses (one catalog, two consumers). Off by default (`spring.ai.mcp.server.enabled=false`); `TurMcpLoopbackFilter` enforces the loopback-first trust boundary (`turing.mcp-server.loopback-only=true`) until T246's OAuth 2.1 gate lands. IT (`TurMcpServerIT`) drives the real protocol end-to-end with the official MCP client; `TurMcpLoopbackFilterTest` covers the 403 path. (§XIII.5, §XIII.8)
- **T246** — **OAuth 2.1 resource-server auth + scoping** — opt-in `turing.mcp-server.require-auth=true` puts a dedicated high-priority `/mcp` `SecurityFilterChain` (`TurMcpSecurityConfig`) in front of the endpoint: validates Keycloak JWT bearer tokens, maps `realm_access.roles` + scopes to authorities (`TurMcpJwtAuthenticationConverter`), and keeps tenant binding via `TurTenantResolutionFilter` (T282). Fails closed (denies all) if no `JwtDecoder` is configured. Tool execution is scoped per-call by `TurMcpToolScopePolicy` + `TurMcpScopeToolCallback` (read-only by default; write tools require `SCOPE_mcp:write` — the write set is empty until T253). IT (`TurMcpAuthIT`, with a test `JwtDecoder`) asserts 401 without a token / accepted with a valid one; unit tests cover the policy + callback. (§XIII.6)
- **T247** — **Read search surface as MCP tools** — `TurMcpSearchToolService` publishes the spec-friendly read tools `list_sites`, `get_site_fields`, `search_site`, `get_document`, `similar_documents`, `facet_search`, `autocomplete` (curated `prompts/tools/mcp/*.md` descriptions). Each delegates to the production-tested `TurDslToolService` (full-text/facet/similarity tools build the ES-DSL body via a `Map` so user input is JSON-escaped, then route through `dsl_search` for multi-engine Solr/Lucene/Elasticsearch handling) — a clean public vocabulary, not a second search impl. This replaced the raw `dsl_*` names on the MCP surface (the internal agent still uses `dsl_*`). `TurMcpServerIT` now asserts these tools are listed + decorated over the real protocol; `TurMcpSearchToolServiceTest` covers delegation + query-body construction. (§XIII.3)
- **T248** — **`rag_answer` — one-call cited RAG** — `TurMcpRagToolService` exposes `rag_answer(site, locale, question, maxSources)`: retrieves via the same `TurDslSearchService` (multi-engine + per-site semantic ranking), grounds an LLM answer through `TurLlmSummaryService` (default-LLM resolution + token accounting + cache), and returns the answer plus a `[n] title — url` citation list built from the hits' own source fields. Degrades gracefully (no hits → says so; no default LLM → returns ranked sources). `TurMcpServerIT` asserts it's published; `TurMcpRagToolServiceTest` covers the retrieve→ground→cite paths + fallbacks. (§XIII.3)
- **T255** — **One-click client config (DX)** — `TurMcpClientConfigService` + `GET /api/v2/mcp/client-config` (admin-only) generate ready-to-paste `mcp.json` snippets for Claude Code / Cursor (native remote Streamable-HTTP by `url`) and Claude Desktop (`npx mcp-remote` bridge), resolving the endpoint from `turing.url` + `/mcp` and adding an `Authorization: Bearer` placeholder when `require-auth` is on. Reports `enabled` so the admin UI can prompt to turn the server on. `TurMcpClientConfigServiceTest` covers the auth/no-auth/disabled variants. (§XIII.7)
- **T253** — **Gated write / ingestion tools** — `TurMcpWriteToolService` exposes `index_document` (parse JSON doc → enqueue a `CREATE` job via the existing `TurSNImportAPI` pipeline), `deindex_document` (`DELETE` by id), and `reindex_site` (rebuild the site's RAG vector store on a virtual thread via `TurSNSiteContentExchangeService`). **Double-gated, read-only by default**: not registered unless `turing.mcp-server.write-enabled=true`, and even then each call requires the `SCOPE_mcp:write` authority (`TurMcpToolScopePolicy.DEFAULT_WRITE_TOOLS`). `TurMcpServerIT` asserts they're hidden by default; `TurMcpWriteToolsIT` asserts they appear when opted-in; `TurMcpWriteToolServiceTest` covers job construction + validation. (§XIII.3, §XIII.6)
- **T252** — **Analytics tools** — `TurMcpAnalyticsToolService` publishes `search_metrics` (top terms + counts + avg results over a period), `top_failed_searches` (queries that always returned zero results — new `TurSNSiteMetricAccessRepository.topZeroResultTerms` with `HAVING MAX(numFound)=0`), and `content_gaps` (high-demand, low-coverage queries: popular terms whose avg result count is below threshold). All site-scoped, backed by the existing search-access log (no Mongo/Redis needed). `TurMcpServerIT` asserts the three tools are published; `TurMcpAnalyticsToolServiceTest` covers period selection, zero-result, and the gap filter. (§XIII.4)
- **T251** — **MCP Prompts** — `TurMcpPromptConfig` publishes each enabled Persona as a reusable MCP prompt (`persona-<slug>`); one pick adopts the customer's brand voice + guardrails in any MCP client. The prompt body is produced by the same `TurPersonaPromptComposer` (deterministic static block) the chat runtime uses, so the exposed voice is byte-identical to the in-product one. `TurMcpServerIT` asserts the prompts capability is wired over the protocol; `TurMcpPromptConfigTest` covers the persona→prompt mapping (enabled-only, slug, composed voice). (§XIII.3)
- **T250** — **MCP Resources** — `TurMcpResourceConfig` publishes Turing content as browsable MCP resources so a client can attach a source/schema to its context, not only search it: a concrete `turing://sites` (the site catalog, read dynamically) plus templates `turing://site/{site}/schema` and `turing://site/{site}/doc/{docId}`. Read handlers delegate to `TurMcpSearchToolService` (same code as `list_sites`/`get_site_fields`/`get_document`), so resources and tools never drift; inherits the T246 endpoint gate. `TurMcpServerIT` asserts the resource + both templates are listed and readable over the real protocol. (§XIII.3)
- **T249** — **`invoke_agent` + `list_agents`** — `TurMcpAgentToolService` lets an external orchestrator discover the customer's enabled AI Agents and run one headlessly. `invoke_agent(agentId, message, conversationId?)` routes through the same `TurAgentChatExecutor` the public chat endpoint uses (resolves the agent's default LLM by min-id, collects the streamed `Flux` into one answer with a 120s cap, excludes `options` chips), then reads captured slots via `TurChatFlowEngineService.listSlotsForConversation(...)` and returns answer + slots + the conversation id to reuse for follow-ups. `TurMcpServerIT` asserts both tools are published; `TurMcpAgentToolServiceTest` covers list-filtering + the invoke collect/slot/validation paths. (§XIII.3)

## Block J — Multi-tenancy (§XIV)

> Shipped 2026-06-11 (T257–T284). One JVM, many isolated tenants, behind
> `turing.tenancy.enabled` (default off = legacy). See
> [MULTI_TENANCY.md](MULTI_TENANCY.md) for the operator + developer guide.

- **T257** — **`TurTenant` + `TurTenantMembership` model** — aggregate root (UUID PK, unique `slug`, `status`, `plan`, `isolationMode`) + membership join (role OWNER/ADMIN/MEMBER); cache-paired repos; Liquibase seeds the immutable `DEFAULT` tenant. (§XIV.2.1)
- **T258** — **`TurTenantContext`** — flag-aware thread-local current-tenant holder with `runAs`/`runAsSystem`; `resolveCurrentTenant()` short-circuits to `DEFAULT` when tenancy is off. (§XIV.2.2)
- **T259** — **`TurTenantResolutionFilter`** — resolves the tenant (JWT claim → session → subdomain → header), validates active membership (403), blocks suspended tenants, clears in `finally`; no-op when off. (§XIV.2.3)
- **T260** — **Hibernate `@TenantId` pilot on `TurSNSite`** — `CurrentTenantIdentifierResolver` → `TurTenantContext`; IT proves auto-stamp on write + auto-filter on read + B blind to A. (§XIV.2.4)
- **T261** — **Roll `@TenantId` across tenant-owned entities** — field + Liquibase column (nullable + DEFAULT backfill) on 15 aggregate roots (ai_agent, chat_flow, …). (§XIV.2.5)
- **T262** — **Backfill + retire legacy pseudo-tenant** — flip discriminator columns NOT NULL; delete the `turing.multi-tenant` / `findAllForListingByCreatedBy` branch. (§XIV.2.6)
- **T263** — **Keycloak tenant claim + membership authorities** — `TENANT_<id>` / `TENANT_<id>_<ROLE>` authorities in both OIDC and session paths. (§XIV.3.1)
- **T264** — **Self-service signup** — `POST /api/signup` creates tenant + OWNER membership; idempotent, slug + reserved-word validated. (§XIV.3.2)
- **T265** — **Current-tenant switch + my tenants** — `GET /api/tenants/mine`, `POST /api/tenants/{slug}/switch` (sets the session attribute). (§XIV.3.3)
- **T266** — **Platform-admin cross-tenant context** — `ROLE_PLATFORM_ADMIN` + audited `TurPlatformAdminService` (`runForTenant`/`runAsSystem`) — the only sanctioned bypass. (§XIV.3.4)
- **T267** — **Cache tenant-awareness** — `TurTenantCacheKeyGenerator` as the default key generator partitions every `@Cacheable` by tenant. (§XIV.4.1)
- **T268** — **Search-engine core tenant prefixing** — `TurTenantCoreNaming` `t<shortId>_` prefix on the name-keyed SN core + RAG core persistence. (§XIV.4.2)
- **T269** — **Storage path tenant scoping** — `TurTenantScopedStorageService` prepends `tenants/<id>/`, strips it from listings, guards traversal. (§XIV.4.3)
- **T270** — **Lucene vector store per-tenant base path** — `TurLuceneStoreProvider` resolves under `…/<tenantId>/`; cache key gains the prefix. (§XIV.4.4)
- **T271** — **Per-tenant secret key derivation** — `TurSecretCryptoService` derives `PBKDF2(master, tenantId)`; DEFAULT keeps the legacy key. (§XIV.4.5)
- **T272** — **JMS tenant propagation** — `turingTenantId` header on indexing/routine sends; listeners restore + clear via `TurJmsTenantPropagation`. (§XIV.4.6)
- **T273** — **Scheduled-job tenant fan-out** — `TurTenantScheduledFanOut` runs `@TenantId`-touching sweeps once per active tenant. (§XIV.4.7)
- **T274** — **Reactive + async context propagation** — Micrometer `ThreadLocalAccessor` + `Hooks.enableAutomaticContextPropagation()` + a `TaskDecorator`. (§XIV.4.8)
- **T275** — **Tenant-owned infra + shared pool** — nullable `tenantId` on 6 infra tables + `findVisibleToTenant` (`current ∪ NULL`). (§XIV.5.1)
- **T276** — **Per-tenant cost attribution** — `llm_token_usage.tenantId` + `findUsageByTenant` billing roll-up. (§XIV.5.2)
- **T277** — **Plan-based quotas** — `TurTenantQuotaService` (FREE bounded, paid unlimited); 402 at create, 429 at LLM admission. (§XIV.5.3)
- **T278** — **Frontend tenant context** — `X-Turing-Tenant` axios interceptor, tenant service/queries, org switcher + signup/settings page, cache re-scope on switch. (§XIV.6.1)
- **T279** — **Platform-admin tenant console** — `/api/platform/tenants` (list/suspend/activate/impersonate) + admin page. (§XIV.6.2)
- **T280** — **Cross-tenant isolation IT gate** — `TurCrossTenantIsolationIT` (secret crypto end-to-end) + `TurSNSiteTenantIsolationIT` + `TurTenantInfraVisibilityIT`. (§XIV.7.1)
- **T281** — **Tenant lifecycle teardown** — `TurTenantTeardownService` suspend + delete (storage prefix, Lucene dir, caches, registry rows); audited, idempotent, dry-run. (§XIV.7.2)
- **T282** — **MCP server multi-tenant scoping** — design-resolved: the future `/mcp` endpoint inherits tenant binding from `TurTenantResolutionFilter` (no separate code until Block I ships). (§XIV.7.3)
- **T283** — **`docs/MULTI_TENANCY.md`** — operator + developer guide: model, leak-surface checklist, new-entity recipe, teardown runbook. (§XIV.7.4)
- **T284** — **Hybrid escalation seam** — `TurTenant.isolationMode` (SHARED|SCHEMA|DB, default SHARED) reserves the path to schema/DB isolation; only SHARED implemented. (§XIV.7.5)
- **T333** — **Auto-provision personal tenant on first login** — opt-in `turing.tenancy.auto-provision` (default off): an authenticated principal that resolves to no explicit tenant gets a personal tenant + OWNER membership minted on first request (race-safe, slug derived from the username), and resolves to it via that membership thereafter. The "one environment per user" SaaS posture used by Viglet Cloud; legacy invite-based deployments leave it off. (§XIV.8)
- **T334** — **End-user tenant propagation on server-to-server calls** — a trusted internal caller (e.g. Dumont → `http://turing:2700` on behalf of an end user) can present `X-Turing-Internal-Token` matching `turing.tenancy.internal-token` and stamp `X-Turing-Tenant` to scope the call to the user's tenant — `TurTenantResolutionFilter` then honours the header **without** the per-user active-membership check (a suspended tenant still 403s; comparison is constant-time). Off until the token is set; the preferred path — forwarding the user's bearer token, where the JWT `tenant` claim + membership applies — works unchanged and needs no token. (§XIV.8.1)
- **T335** — **Per-tenant SN core auto-creation on standalone Solr** — the prefixed SN core (`t<shortId>_<core>`) is created at site/locale creation, but on a standalone Solr (`turing.solr.cloud=false`, the Cloud topology) a site whose core was never provisioned would `404` on its first index. `TurSNProcessQueue` now self-heals: before the first CREATE for a `(site, locale)` in a batch it verifies the core exists and provisions it on demand from the locale's configset (deduped per batch — one `indexExists` check per distinct core, not per document), reusing the import path's `indexExists`/`createIndex` pair. Operator requirement documented in `MULTI_TENANCY.md`: the standalone Solr must ship the `en`/`es`/`pt`/`ca` configsets (bare `solr-precreate turing` only has `_default`). (§XIV.8.2)
- **T336** — **De-provision on Keycloak user deletion** — closes the T333 lifecycle loop: a daily reconciliation sweep (`TurTenantDeprovisionService` + `TurTenantDeprovisionJob`, ShedLock cluster-wide-once) checks each non-DEFAULT tenant's members against Keycloak via a service-account (`client_credentials`) `TurKeycloakServiceAccountIdentityProvider`. A tenant whose **every** member is ABSENT/DISABLED is suspended (stamping a new `TurTenant.suspendedAt`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.35.yaml`); in opt-in `SUSPEND_THEN_DELETE` mode it is later torn down via the T281 service once it has stayed orphaned for `deleteAfterDays`. Fail-safe: one live member or any UNKNOWN (IdP unreachable) member spares the whole tenant; DEFAULT is never touched. The seam (`TurTenantOwnerIdentityProvider`) keeps the orchestration unit-testable. All off unless `turing.tenancy.deprovision.enabled=true` + service-account creds set. (§XIV.8.3)
- **T372** — **Platform-global BYO-infra is read-only in the CRUD** — with tenancy on, a GLOBAL (`tenantId IS NULL`) SE/LLM/Embedding/Store/Integration/MCP instance is writable only by a platform admin: one rule per layer — `TurInfraTenantScope.isPlatformGlobalReadOnly`/`assertWritable` (403, called at the top of each of the 6 controllers' update+delete on the persisted entity) + update-mappers `@Mapping(target="tenantId", ignore=true)` so an edit can't reassign ownership; `/features` exposes `tenancyEnabled`+`platformAdmin`, a `useInfraReadOnly(tenantId)` hook freezes Save/Delete and shows a "Global" badge in the 6 admin forms+lists. Tenancy-off installs unaffected. (§XIV.8)
- **T365** — **Scope BYO-infra access-by-id, not just listings** — the T275 fix scoped only *list*+*create* on the 6 plain-`tenantId` BYO-infra entities; `findById` was still an unscoped PK lookup, so a caller holding another tenant's id could read/mutate it via `GET/PUT/DELETE /{id}` (and the SE `/cores`·`/system-info`·`/select` and Store `/collections`·import/export sub-paths). New `TurInfraTenantScope.isVisibleToTenant` (own OR GLOBAL-`null` OR platform-admin; always true when tenancy off) applied as `findById(id).filter(scope::isVisibleToTenant)` on every by-id path in the 6 controllers — a non-visible id falls into the same not-found branch as a missing id; delete paths wrap the delete inside the filtered `ifPresent` so a non-visible id is never deleted. Entities using real `@TenantId` (`TurSNSite`, `TurAIAgent`) are already isolated by Hibernate and out of scope. (§XIV.8.4)
- **T366** — **Grant `ROLE_PLATFORM_ADMIN` from a Keycloak realm role** — the platform-admin authority (consumed by `TurPlatformAdminService`, the T279 cross-tenant console, and the GLOBAL-pool branch of `TurInfraTenantScope.stampOnCreate`) was never granted anywhere. `TurAuthorityResolver` now maps the `turing.tenancy.platform-admin-role` Keycloak **realm** role (default `platform-admin`, read from the token's `realm_access.roles` claim) → `ROLE_PLATFORM_ADMIN` on both the OIDC and OAuth2 paths via the new `extractRealmRoles`/3-arg `resolve`. The product decision is configurable: `turing.tenancy.admin-implies-platform-admin` (default **false** = platform-admin must be an explicit, separately-assigned realm role) optionally grants it implicitly to the bootstrap admin (`turing.keycloak-admin-id`). Default off + no such realm role = no principal gains the authority, so non-Cloud installs are unchanged. (§XIV.8.5)

## Block K — Agent CI (flow/prompt eval gate, §XV)
