Wired into `TurGoogleGenAiNativeService`: gated by the `contextCacheEnabled` provider option (+ optional `contextCacheTtlSeconds`, default 1 h), the system instruction is cached and threaded via `GenerateContentConfig.cachedContent(handle)` with `systemInstruction`/`tools` omitted from the request (they belong to the cache; bypassed tools are logged). Surfaced in the T435 Request Options picker as a `gemini-context-cache` REQUEST_OPTION (off by default). Unblocks T500 (full-context answering) + T502 (Anthropic `cache_control` on the same seam). Covered by `TurContextCacheSeamTest` (no-op, factory routing, Gemini gating + fail-open) + `TurGoogleGenAiNativeServiceTest` (cachedContent wiring + systemInstruction/tools omission + opt-in gate). (§X.20)
- **T498** — **Gemini thinking budget + thought summaries** — the Gemini leg of the cross-vendor F.12 "reasoning budget + Why-this-answer" contract. `TurGoogleGenAiNativeService.buildConfig` now emits a `thinkingConfig` when the instance sets `thinkingBudget` (caps reasoning tokens; 0 disables, -1 dynamic) and/or `includeThoughts`; when thoughts are surfaced, the stream splits thought parts (`Part.thought()==true`) from answer parts (`response.text()` would conflate them) and emits the accumulated thought summary as the shared **`reasoning` SSE event** — so it lands in the existing T178 "Why this answer" panel with zero frontend change. Default (neither option set) → unchanged request + fast aggregate streaming path. Covered by `TurGoogleGenAiNativeServiceTest` (thinkingConfig resolution + surfacing gate). (§X.19)
- **T497** — **Gemini Files bridge** — folds Gemini into `TurFilesApiBridge` (T175): a binary now mirrors into the **Gemini Files API** (`client.files.upload(bytes, …)`) and the cache stores the file **URI** (what a `fileData` part references for native PDF/video grounding), falling back to the resource name. Reuses the existing content-addressed, account-scoped dedup (T177) so a binary uploads once per vendor account. Distinctive twist: Gemini files are retained ~48 h, so the cache row gets an `expiresAt = now + 48h` (via the new `expiryFor(pluginType)`) and the existing `notExpired` filter re-uploads after expiry — OpenAI/Anthropic stay non-expiring. Fail-open unchanged (non-native vendor / no key / upload error → empty → Tika-text fallback). The native grounding *consumer* that builds the `fileData` part rides T501. Covered by `TurFilesApiBridgeTest` (Gemini cache-hit + native recognition; upload path is integration-only — the Gemini `Client.files` field isn't mockable). (§X.19)
- **T496** — **Gemini Batch provider (chat + embeddings)** — closes the T156 "Gemini → no provider" gap on both legs: `TurGeminiBatchProvider` (auto-registered in `TurBatchProviderFactory` by plugin type `gemini`) now implements **both** `TurBatchProvider` and `TurBatchEmbeddingProvider`, so T157 nightly summarization + T159 LLM-Judge eval (chat) **and** T158 in-place re-embedding (`TurBatchReEmbeddingService`) all run at the Gemini Batch ~50% discount instead of falling back to synchronous full price. Submission mirrors the OpenAI Files-then-Batch dance: the requests serialize to **keyed** JSONL (the `key` round-trips Turing's `customId` — the reason the file path is used over inlined requests, which carry no key), upload via the Files API, and are referenced as a `BatchJobSource` (chat, `TurGeminiBatchJsonl`) or `EmbeddingsBatchJobSource` on `client.batches.createEmbeddings` (embeddings, `TurGeminiEmbeddingBatchJsonl`); `status` maps `JobState` → `TurBatchState`; `results`/`embeddingResults` download + parse the destination file symmetrically. The embeddings input emits one `embedContent` request per line with `taskType: RETRIEVAL_DOCUMENT` (matching `TurGeminiEmbeddingModel`'s index-side path, since re-embedding is document-side), the model fixed once at create time; the output parser is lenient on both the `embedding.values` and `embeddings[0].values` shapes so a minor server shape change degrades to a failed line, not a failed batch. The drift-prone JSONL build/parse is unit-tested offline (`TurGeminiBatchJsonlTest` + `TurGeminiEmbeddingBatchJsonlTest`); bean wiring + vendor-routing matrix (Gemini/OpenAI support embeddings batch, Anthropic does not) is covered by `TurGeminiEmbeddingBatchProviderIT`. The mldev camelCase field names follow the documented contract and should still be confirmed against the live Batch API. (§X.19)
- **T495** — **Gemini embeddings + task-type optimization** — replaces the native Gemini provider's embedding `UnsupportedOperationException` with `TurGeminiEmbeddingModel` (a Spring AI `EmbeddingModel` over the GenAI SDK's `embedContent`, `gemini-embedding-001`). The differentiator: **asymmetric task types**, applied via the standard VectorStore calling convention with zero caller changes — `embed(Document)` / `embed(List<Document>,…)` (index time) use `RETRIEVAL_DOCUMENT`, while `embed(String)` / `call(EmbeddingRequest)` (query time) use `RETRIEVAL_QUERY` — a measured retrieval-quality win the OpenAI/Anthropic providers can't match. Optional Matryoshka `outputDimensionality` (provider option) trades a smaller vector for lower storage/IO; `dimensions()` reports it. `TurGeminiLlmProvider.createEmbeddingModel` builds it from the instance creds + `embeddingModel`/`outputDimensionality` options. Covered by `TurGeminiEmbeddingModelTest` (dims + response mapping) + updated `TurGeminiLlmProviderTest`. (§X.19)
- **T494** — **Gemini Computer Use (`gemini-2.5-computer-use`)** — the Gemini analog of OpenAI (T136) / Anthropic (T143) computer use. New owns-turn `GEMINI_COMPUTER_USE` (`gemini-computer-use`, function `computer-use`) capability + `TurGeminiComputerUseService`: the multi-turn screenshot/action loop driven via Gemini **function calling** (the model emits a `FunctionCall` UI action → `TurComputerUseDriver` performs it → a `FunctionResponse` returns the screenshot as inline image data, until a final text answer). Reuses the same vendor-neutral `TurComputerUseDriver` seam (T136) as the other two vendors; ships against the no-op driver, so with none configured the loop replies with guidance (errors-as-text) rather than failing. `translateAction` maps Gemini's predefined functions (`click_at`/`type_text_at`/`scroll_document`/`drag_and_drop`/…) to the neutral action. `TurNativeChatExecutor`'s Gemini branch routes it ahead of the base path (owns the turn). Auto-surfaces in the T432 picker with the "runs the turn alone" warning. Covered by `TurGeminiComputerUseServiceTest` (config/translate/guidance) + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest` routing. (§X.19)
- **T493** — **Gemini native image generation** — the Gemini analog of OpenAI `image_generation` (T137). New `GEMINI_IMAGE_GENERATION` (`gemini-image-generation`, function `image-gen`) capability. Unlike the other Gemini natives it is **not** a `tools` entry: `TurGoogleGenAiNativeService.buildConfig` sets the request's `responseModalities` to `["TEXT","IMAGE"]` when enabled, so an image-capable model (`gemini-2.5-flash-image` / Imagen) returns the generated image as an inline data part. That part flows through the same part-collection + `TurGeminiCodeExecutionDecoder` path (T492) and renders into the answer as a self-contained `data:` URI. Auto-surfaces in the T432 picker; opt-in (no modality requested by default → unchanged path). Covered by `TurGoogleGenAiNativeServiceTest`. (§X.19)
- **T492** — **Gemini code execution tool (`code_execution`)** — the Gemini analog of OpenAI `code_interpreter` (T135) / Anthropic `code_execution` (T141): a built-in Python sandbox. New `GEMINI_CODE_EXECUTION` (`gemini-code-execution`, function `code-exec`) capability; `TurGoogleGenAiNativeService.buildTools` adds the `code_execution` `Tool` and the streamed turn collects the response's code-execution parts. `TurGeminiCodeExecutionDecoder` renders them into trailing answer markdown — the generated Python as a ```python block, its output as a ```text block, and inline matplotlib images as self-contained `data:` URIs (the zero-dependency equivalent of the OpenAI/Anthropic `sandbox:` scheme for Gemini's inline image bytes; renders in the existing markdown surface with no new artifact endpoint). Auto-surfaces in the T432 picker; opt-in. Covered by `TurGeminiCodeExecutionDecoderTest` + `TurGoogleGenAiNativeServiceTest`. (§X.19)
- **T491** — **Gemini URL Context tool (`url_context`)** — the Gemini analog of Anthropic `web_fetch` (T140): the model pulls a URL/PDF named in the prompt directly in Google's infrastructure, no Turing crawler. New `GEMINI_URL_CONTEXT` (`gemini-url-context`, function `web-fetch`) capability; `TurGoogleGenAiNativeService.buildTools` adds the `url_context` `Tool` when enabled, coexisting with `google_search` in the same turn. Auto-surfaces in the T432 capability picker; opt-in via the two-level gate. Covered by `TurGoogleGenAiNativeServiceTest` (url_context-only + combined-with-google_search tool wiring). (§X.19)
- **T490** — **Gemini Google Search grounding (`google_search`)** — the Gemini analog of OpenAI/Anthropic `web_search`. New `GEMINI_GOOGLE_SEARCH` (`gemini-google-search`, function `web-search`) capability; `TurGoogleGenAiNativeService.buildConfig` adds the `google_search` `Tool` when enabled, and the streamed turn captures the candidate's `GroundingMetadata`. `TurGeminiGroundingDecoder` maps each `groundingSupport → supporting chunk` pair onto a `TurChatCitation` (cited answer span via a UTF-8 byte-offset→char-index conversion, web source link), reusing the shipped `type:"citations"` SSE + T154 citation-underline UI for free; the Google-mandated Search Suggestion chips ride a new `type:"searchSuggestions"` event (`renderedContent` HTML + `webSearchQueries`). Both SDKs parse the new event onto the response/message (`TurSearchSuggestions`); the admin chat renders the chips below the answer (rendered fragment verbatim, or query-chip fallback) with en/pt i18n. Strictly opt-in via the two-level gate. Covered by `TurGeminiGroundingDecoderTest`, `TurGoogleGenAiNativeServiceTest` (tool wiring) and `search-suggestions.test.tsx`. (§X.19)
- **T489** — **`TurGoogleGenAiNativeService` + Gemini executor branch** — the third native chat branch alongside OpenAI Responses (F.2) and Anthropic Messages (F.3). `TurNativeProviderClient` gains a `gemini()` accessor that builds a `com.google.genai.Client` from the instance credentials (api-key + optional base-URL override via `HttpOptions`), reusing the same `TurSecretCryptoService`/provider plumbing — no parallel security path. `TurGoogleGenAiNativeService` streams a turn through the SDK's `generateContentStream` (blocking call on `Schedulers.boundedElastic`, like the OpenAI path), taking the same `TurChatPromptAssembler`-fused system prompt + history the other two paths receive (system → `systemInstruction`, history → `Content` turns with the assistant→`model` role mapping) and mapping each chunk onto the shared `ChatResponse` SSE contract. `TurNativeChatExecutor` recognises the `gemini` plugin type and routes to it when the per-instance capability matrix is non-empty; **empty matrix = unchanged Spring AI `GoogleGenAiChatModel` path**, so every existing Gemini agent is byte-for-byte unchanged. `TurCapabilityProvider` gains a `GEMINI` constant. The enabled capabilities are threaded through `buildConfig` for the server-side built-in tools (T490+) to wire. Covered by `TurGoogleGenAiNativeServiceTest` (prompt/option/role mapping), `TurNativeProviderClientImplGeminiTest` (accessor) and new `TurNativeChatExecutorTest` routing cases. (§X.19)

## Block M — Verifiable answers (provider-agnostic provenance, §XVII)

- **T292** — **RAG attribution metadata end-to-end** — `search_knowledge_base` chunks' `(sourceId, title, url, chunkIndex, score)` flow through a per-turn `TurRagSourceCollector` (published in the tool context) and out as a structured `sources` SSE event from `TurChatStreamingDispatcher`; both SDKs parse it (`TurChatSource`) onto the assistant message. Provider-agnostic — Turing's own retrieval metadata, no vendor citation API. (§XVII.1)
- **T293** — **Source-chip UI + "why did you say this?" trace** — headless `TuringSourceChips` in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` (grouped chips, confidence cue from top score, one-click expansion to the cited passages); skinned into the admin chat list. (§XVII.2)
- **T327** — **Provenance in the public SN chat** — `TurSNGenAi` preserves per-chunk metadata through retrieval and emits the same `sources` SSE event on `/api/sn/{site}/chat/conversation`; the SN chat-memory buffer is now token-only so side-channel events don't pollute recorded answers. (§XVII.3)
- **T328** — **Relevance gate + optional reranker for SN RAG** — config-driven similarity floor on the filtered path (replaces the hard-coded `0.0`, default `0.0` = legacy) + opt-in fail-open LLM reranker (`TurRagReranker`) narrowing the candidate pool to a higher-precision top-k. Global Settings: `RAG_SN_SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD`, `RAG_SN_RERANK_ENABLED`, `RAG_SN_RERANK_TOP_N`. (§XVII.4)
- **T329** — **Deterministic "not in this site" refusal** — when post-gate retrieval is empty, the SN streaming path short-circuits to the canonical localized refusal (en/pt/es/ca) without an LLM call. (§XVII.5)
- **T330** — **Optional groundedness check for SN answers** — opt-in (`RAG_SN_GROUNDEDNESS_CHECK_ENABLED`, default off) post-generation faithfulness audit; on "unsupported" appends a localized low-confidence caveat to the answer. (§XVII.6)
- **T331** — **Grounded follow-up questions in AI mode** — opt-in (`RAG_SN_FOLLOWUPS_ENABLED`, default off) 2–3 suggestions generated from the retrieved chunks, emitted via the existing `options` chip contract and rendered as clickable chips in `ai-mode-chat.tsx`. (§XVII.7)
- **T332** — **Source chips on the public `ai-mode-chat.tsx`** — the T293 `TuringSourceChips` renderer wired into the public SN AI-mode chat, fed by the T327 `sources` event. (§XVII.8)

## Block Q — `viglet-core` shared backend platform (cross-product)

> Incubate the cross-product backend foundation as a Turing submodule, promote to a public shared lib (Shio + Dumont) when stable. Bet + phasing → [STRATEGY.md](STRATEGY.md) §XII.

- **T367** — **Phase 1 — `viglet-core` Turing submodule skeleton** — new multi-module Maven parent `viglet-core/` (groupId `com.viglet.core`, packaging `pom`, inherits the Turing parent only for version/plugin management while incubating) with its first leaf module `viglet-core-lang` (jar, **zero runtime dependencies**). Both day-1 hard rules in place so the Phase-2 promotion stays a "move + publish": neutral identity (package `com.viglet.core.*`, no `Tur*`) and a one-way dependency arrow (`viglet-core` never imports `turing-*`). Wiring proven by migrating one leaf concern — `TurUtils.getUrlTemplate` → `com.viglet.core.lang.UrlTemplate.of` (+ its 11-test suite) — with `turing-app` as the first consumer (declares `com.viglet.core:viglet-core-lang`, call sites updated, old `TurUtils`/test removed). Added to the root reactor's default profile + `dependencyManagement`. (§XII.3/§XII.4)
- **T368** — **`viglet-core` crypto + storage starters** — lifted the secret-crypto and object-storage abstractions into two Spring Boot starter modules: `viglet-core-crypto` (`VigletSecretCryptoService` — AES/GCM + per-tenant PBKDF2, where key material is a `Supplier` and the tenant is a `VigletTenantKeyResolver`; `VigletCryptoAutoConfiguration` wires it from `viglet.crypto.*`) and `viglet-core-storage` (neutral `VigletStorageService` SPI + filesystem/MinIO/no-op backends + `VigletStorageObject`/`Stat`/`ContentTypes`; `VigletStorageAutoConfiguration` selects by `viglet.storage.type`). Both beans are `@ConditionalOnMissingBean` so a product overrides them. `turing-app` is the first consumer via thin adapters that preserve its existing API and behaviour byte-for-byte: `TurSecretCryptoService` subclasses the core service (keeping `turing.ai.crypto.key`, the production fail-fast, the legacy salt prefix so old ciphertext still decrypts); `TurStorageServiceAdapter` maps the neutral types back to `TurAssetItem`/`TurStorageType`/`TurStorageObjectStat` and re-adds the multipart upload convenience, with `TurStorageContentTypes` now aliasing the core map. No `turing.storage.*` config or call-site changes. (§XII.2)
- **T370** — **`viglet-core` tenancy kernel** — lifted the proven, persistence-agnostic tenancy primitives into `viglet-core-tenancy` (Spring Boot starter), sequenced after Block J stabilized (T372/T365/T366): `VigletTenantContext` (thread-bound current tenant + system mode + `runAs`/`runAsSystem`), `VigletTenantOwnedInfra` (plain-column marker), `VigletInfraTenantScope` (stamp-on-create/import + scoped list + the T372 `assertWritable`/`isPlatformGlobalReadOnly` and T365 `isVisibleToTenant` rules), `VigletPlatformAdminService` (+ `ROLE_PLATFORM_ADMIN`), and the Hibernate `@TenantId` `CurrentTenantIdentifierResolver` wiring (`VigletTenancyAutoConfiguration`, nested config `@ConditionalOnClass` Hibernate, all beans `@ConditionalOnMissingBean`, `viglet.tenancy.*`). `turing-app` is the first consumer with behaviour byte-for-byte: `TurTenantContext`/`TurPlatformAdminService`/`TurInfraTenantScope` are now thin `Tur*` subclasses keeping `turing.tenancy.*` + `TurTenant.DEFAULT_TENANT_ID` (so they satisfy the core `@ConditionalOnMissingBean` and every call site + `TurPlatformAdminService.ROLE_PLATFORM_ADMIN` reference is unchanged), the six BYO-infra entities implement `VigletTenantOwnedInfra` directly, and `TurHibernateMultiTenancyConfig` is replaced by the core autoconfig. **Stayed per-product** (bound to Turing's user/membership model, per the security-model-alignment gate): `TurTenantResolutionFilter`, `TurTenantService`, the tenant-registry entities/tables and `TurTenancyProperty`'s product-specific fields. (§XII / §XIV.8.8)
- **T369** — **`viglet-core` security starter** — lifted the genuinely product-agnostic web-security primitives into `viglet-core-security` (Spring Boot starter): `VigletAuthenticationEntryPoint` (JSON `{"error":"Unauthorized"}` 401, with configurable `BasicRealmRule`s for paths that need a `WWW-Authenticate` challenge), `VigletCsrfCookieFilter` (renders the deferred CSRF token to the cookie + echoes/exposes the header), `VigletCsrfController` (CSRF-token endpoint, path via `viglet.security.csrf.endpoint`, default `/api/csrf`), and `VigletKeycloakClaims.extractRealmRoles` (parses the Keycloak `realm_access.roles` claim). `VigletSecurityAutoConfiguration` registers the entry-point + controller `@ConditionalOnMissingBean`; it never assembles a `SecurityFilterChain` (that wiring stays per-product). Scoped conservatively pending the security-model alignment audit — the OIDC/OAuth2 user services and DB-backed authority resolution stay in each product because they bind to its user/group/membership model. `turing-app` is the first consumer with behaviour preserved: `TurAuthenticationEntryPoint extends VigletAuthenticationEntryPoint` (passing the `/git/` → `"Turing Git"` rule), `TurCsrfController extends VigletCsrfController`, the security config uses the core `VigletCsrfCookieFilter`, and `TurAuthorityResolver.extractRealmRoles` delegates to the core util. (§XII.2)
- **T377** — **`viglet-core` messaging + persistent-logging modules** — lifted the embedded-Artemis tuning and the MongoDB/Redis Logback appenders re-implemented byte-for-byte in Turing + Dumont into two lean leaf modules (kept separate so an app pulls only the concern it needs, per §XII.2). `viglet-core-messaging` holds `VigletArtemisConfigurationCustomizer` (pins `globalMaxSize`/`maxDiskUsage`, switches the address-full policy to PAGE so producers spill to disk instead of hanging on credit; takes the product's queue-address name(s) as a constructor arg, applies to them + the catch-all `#`; Artemis stays a `provided` dep). `viglet-core-logging` holds the `VigletMongoDBAppender(Base)`/`VigletMongoDBIndexingAppender` + `VigletRedisAppender(Base)`/`VigletRedisIndexingAppender` families, the neutral `VigletLoggingEntry` general-log DTO, and `VigletIsoDateSerializer`. `turing-app` is the first consumer with behaviour byte-for-byte: `TurArtemisConfig` is now a thin `@Component` subclass passing `TurSNConstants.INDEXING_QUEUE`, and the `logback-spring-{mongo,redis}.xml` appender FQCNs point at the `com.viglet.core.logging.*` classes (the persisted document shape is unchanged). The product-specific `TurLoggingIndexing` DTO stays in `turing-commons` (it references the indexing-status enums) and now reuses the core serializer; the eight moved classes + their five tests were deleted from `turing-commons` (the tests were ported to `viglet-core-logging`). Shio has neither concern, so extraction was confirmed at ≥2 products. (§XII.2)
- **T378** — **`viglet-core` outbound-webhook + HMAC signing** — lifted the outbound-webhook dispatch pattern re-implemented in two products (Turing's `TurChatWebhookService` HTTP send — custom method/headers/auth, **no** signing or retry — and Shio's `ShWebhookSigner`/`ShWebhookDispatcher` — HMAC + retry) into the new **zero-runtime-dependency** `viglet-core-webhook` jar. `VigletWebhookSigner` computes `HMAC-SHA256(secret, body)` as the de-facto `sha256=<hex>` header value (verified against the canonical known-answer vector); `VigletWebhookDispatcher` POSTs a `VigletWebhookRequest` (neutral of any entity — method, URL, opaque `byte[]` body, headers, an already-decrypted signing secret) over the JDK `HttpClient` with a bounded **linear retry/back-off** (`VigletWebhookRetryPolicy`) and reports a `VigletWebhookDeliveryResult` — `deliver()` is synchronous (callers that already run off a background pool use it directly), `dispatchAsync()` uses a lazily-created daemon pool, signing only bytes actually put on the wire. `turing-app` is the first consumer: `TurChatWebhookService.post` now serialises the envelope/template, builds the headers (custom + the `authHeader` fallback unchanged) and delivers through the shared dispatcher — **gaining retry/back-off** (3 attempts, 2s linear) for free — and gains **opt-in HMAC signing** via a new per-webhook encrypted `signingSecret` + `signatureHeader` (default `X-Turing-Signature`; blank = unsigned legacy behaviour), surfaced write-only through the DTO (`hasSigningSecret`) and the admin form exactly like `authHeader`. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.36`. 14 module unit tests (signer KAT + dispatcher wire-contract against a loopback `HttpServer`: happy path, signature header, custom method/headers, retry-until-2xx, exhaustion, async). Shio adoption is Phase-2 (gated, §XII.3). (§XII.2)
- **T376** — **`viglet-core` config-var / global-settings service** — lifted the runtime key-value settings store re-built three times (`TurGlobalSettingsService`, `ShConfigVarUtils`, `DumConnectorConfigVarService`) into the new `viglet-core-settings` Spring Boot starter, split along the persistence seam so core stays JPA-free: `VigletConfigVarStore` (the port a product implements over its own `*ConfigVar` entity + repository — those stay per-product) and `VigletConfigVarService` (the typed accessor layer that owns the boilerplate every product re-implemented by hand — null-coalesce + trim on write; parse-or-default for `boolean`/`int`/`long`/`double`, optional `[min,max]` clamp, and case-insensitive `valueOf`-style enum resolution that falls back rather than throwing on read). `VigletSettingsAutoConfiguration` wires the service from `viglet.settings.*` but only `@ConditionalOnBean(VigletConfigVarStore.class)`, so the module is inert until a product supplies its adapter. `turing-app` is the first consumer with behaviour byte-for-byte: a thin `TurConfigVarStore` adapts the cached `TurConfigVarRepository` (preserving the find-or-create-then-stamp-path upsert + cache eviction), and `TurGlobalSettingsService` keeps every public signature while delegating all parse/trim/clamp/enum work to the core service — the Turing-only concerns (secret encryption for the Cohere key, the URL-signing-secret minting, the custom `TurRagRerankStrategyType`/`TurCodeInterpreterExecutionMode` parsers) stay in the Turing layer. Existing `TurGlobalSettingsServiceTest` (66 tests) + API/Bean tests pass unchanged, proving the lift preserves behaviour; 16 new tests cover the core service. (§XII.2)
- **T375** — **`viglet-core` commons module** — lifted the small commons the three products hand-synced identically into the new `viglet-core-commons` jar (pure utility, no Spring beans, like `viglet-core-lang`/`-content`): base exceptions `VigletException` (checked) / `VigletRuntimeException` (unchecked), the `VigletFileSize` (byte→KB→MB, two-decimal rounding) + `VigletFileAttributes` DTOs, `VigletStoreDirectory` (the `user.dir`-relative `store/`/`tmp` helpers), `VigletZipUtils` (commons-compress write + zip4j extract), and `VigletJson` (`isValidJson` via org.json + `asJsonString` via Jackson 3). `turing-commons` is the first consumer with behaviour preserved: `TurException`/`TurRuntimeException` are now thin subclasses of the neutral bases (so every `throws`/`catch` site is unchanged), `TurFileSize` delegates its conversion to `VigletFileSize` (keeping its builder + all-args constructor), and `TurCommonsUtils`' `addFilesToZip`/`unZipIt`/`getStoreDir`/`addSubDirToStoreDir`/`getTempDirectory`/`isValidJson`/`asJsonString` delegate to the neutral utils — existing `TurFileSizeTest`/`TurFileAttributesTest`/`TurException*Test` + `TurFileUtilsTest`/`TurOcrAPITest` pass unchanged. The `TurFileAttributes` DTO (bound to the published `turing-java-sdk` API via `@Builder`/`@AllArgsConstructor`) stays per-product; its `VigletFileAttributes` neutral twin is the Phase-2 unification target. (§XII.2)
- **T374** — **`viglet-core` content-extraction module** — lifted the document-extraction stack hand-synced identically across the three products into the new `viglet-core-content` jar (pure utility, no Spring beans, like `viglet-core-lang`): the Apache Tika pipeline (`tika-core` + pdf/text/ocr/microsoft parser modules + the jbig2/jai-imageio readers its PDF/TIFF path needs, versions pinned by the parent) and jsoup HTML→text. Neutral API: `VigletContentExtractor` (the `AutoDetectParser` + PDF inline-image + Tesseract-OCR + recursive embedded-document setup → `VigletExtractedContent` record of file/text/Tika-`Metadata`) and `VigletHtmlText` (`toPlainText`, structure-aware `stripHtml`, `normalizeWhitespace`). `turing-app` is the first consumer with behaviour preserved: `TurFileUtils` keeps its URL-fetch/SSRF/multipart/DTO logic but delegates all Tika parsing to `VigletContentExtractor` (adapting `VigletExtractedContent` → `TurTikaFileAttributes`), and `TurRagUtils.stripHtml`/`normalizeWhitespace` delegate to `VigletHtmlText` — existing `TurFileUtilsTest`/`TurRagUtilsTest` (116 tests) pass unchanged. (§XII.2)
- **T371** — **`viglet-core` promoted to a standalone published library + adopted across all three products** — moved the incubated `viglet-core/` submodule out of the Turing monorepo into its own project, [`openviglet/viglet-core`](https://github.com/openviglet/viglet-core), published to **GitHub Packages**. The new repo has a **self-contained parent** (imports the Spring Boot 4.1.0 dependencies BOM and pins the versions/plugins it used to inherit from the Turing parent — Tika, MinIO, httpclient5, jbig2/jai-imageio, MongoDB, Jedis, jsoup, zip4j, org.json, jetbrains-annotations; source/javadoc attach + compiler 3.15.0), a new **`viglet-core-bom`** module pinning all 13 module versions, Apache-2.0 license, and GitHub Actions for build + publish (`GITHUB_TOKEN`, no extra secrets). Versioned `2026.3.4`, **independent from Turing** going forward. All three SB-4.1.0 products now consume it as an **external dependency** via the BOM + the GitHub Packages `<repository>` (auth required even to read — devs/CI need a `read:packages` PAT in `settings.xml`; local builds resolve from `~/.m2`): **Turing** imports `viglet-core-bom` (replacing the 12 inline `dependencyManagement` entries), drops `viglet-core` from the reactor and deletes the in-repo copy; **Shio** adopts `viglet-core-webhook` (signer + dispatcher delegate) + `viglet-core-tenancy`; **Dumont** adopts `viglet-core-tenancy`. Cross-product tenancy parity on the one shared kernel is locked by the conformance test (T394–T399). Verified: standalone repo builds + installs clean (13 modules + BOM); `turing-commons` compiles and `turing-app` resolves all 12 core modules offline from `~/.m2`. Broader per-module adoption (crypto/storage/settings/http/content/commons/logging/messaging) in Shio/Dumont stays opportunistic/gated per §XII.3. (§XII.3/§XII.4)
- **T379** — **`viglet-core` HTTP/RestClient builder** — lifted the outbound-HTTP-client wiring re-implemented in two products (Turing's `TurHttpClientConfiguration` — a shared JDK `HttpClient` on a bounded executor + a pooled httpclient5 `CloseableHttpClient`; Dumont's `DumRestClientConfig` — a Spring `RestClient` on a pooled httpclient5 factory with an API-key default header + a trace request/response interceptor) into the new `viglet-core-http` jar. Neutral API: `VigletHttpClientSettings` (pool sizing + connect/response timeouts, with the hand-tuned 100/20/5s/30s defaults), `VigletHttpClients` (`pooledClient`/`pooledConnectionManager` for httpclient5 + `sharedJdkClient` for the bounded JDK client), `VigletRestClientTracingInterceptor` (the TRACE request/response logger, guarded so it costs nil when off), and `VigletRestClientBuilder` (assembles a `RestClient.Builder` on the pooled factory + optional API-key header + optional tracing-with-buffering). `spring-web` is `provided` so a product that only wants the raw clients pulls nothing extra. `turing-app` is the first consumer with behaviour byte-for-byte: `TurHttpClientConfiguration`'s two beans now delegate to `VigletHttpClients` (the pool/timeout values stay as Turing config) — existing `TurHttpClientConfigurationTest` passes unchanged. 13 module unit tests (settings flooring/defaults, pooled-manager sizing, JDK connect timeout, interceptor pass-through/trace, builder smoke). Dumont's `RestClient` adoption is Phase-2 (gated, §XII.3). (§XII.2)

## Multi-tenancy parity (cross-product) — Turing / Shio / Dumont on one `viglet-core-tenancy`

> Make tenancy 100% identical across the three products on one shared implementation. Staged audit → expand kernel → align each → conformance test. Design → [IMPROVEMENTS.md](IMPROVEMENTS.md) §XIV.9.

- **T399** — **Cross-product tenancy conformance test** — locked the parity promise with a product-neutral behaviour contract (`TurTenancyConformanceTest`) that drives the shared `com.viglet.core.tenancy.*` classes directly and asserts the same inputs → the same isolation / visibility / scoping / 402 / 403 / suspend outcomes, parameterized over three SPI **product profiles** (Turing `DEFAULT` / Shio `shio` / Dumont `DEFAULT`, with distinct reserved-slug sets and resource keys) so a profile is just swappable wiring and the contract is the artifact. Eight behaviour groups × 3 profiles = **24 cases**: context flag-aware resolve + `runAs`/system-mode restore; the `ROLE_PLATFORM_ADMIN` gate (`SecurityException` without it); BYO-infra stamp-on-create, read-only-GLOBAL 403, by-id visibility (own + GLOBAL, never another tenant's), and the tenancy-off no-op path; FREE-bounded quota → 402 at limit (paid unlimited, default un-throttled); idempotent signup + 409 conflict + 400 reserved/malformed slug + personal-tenant resolution; the resolution-filter gate matrix (suspended→403, non-member→403, platform-admin/trusted bypass, always-clear-in-finally, off no-op); and teardown orchestration (admin-gated, default-tenant guarded, dry-run vs purge, suspend/activate). Imports only `com.viglet.core.tenancy.*` + in-memory SPI fakes, so the same file can run against Shio's and Dumont's real beans. (§XIV.9)
- **T398** — **Dumont: adopted the shared tenancy (greenfield)** — wired Dumont (which had no `viglet-core` dependency and only a `dumont.multi-tenant:false` flag) onto the T395 surface, reaching Turing/Shio parity; the full connector-app suite stays green (117 tests). **Build:** added the `viglet-core-bom` import + the GitHub-Packages repository to the root pom and the `viglet-core-tenancy` dependency to `connector-app`. **Registry (greenfield):** new `DumTenant` / `DumTenantMembership` JPA entities (+ `DumTenantStatus`/`DumTenantRole`/`DumTenantMembershipStatus` enums + repositories) following the Shio model — the tenant **name** is the `@TenantId` discriminator (surrogate UUID PK), so `VigletTenantRef.id`=`name`; `DumTenantOnStartup` idempotently seeds the reserved default tenant `dumont`. **Three SPI beans** (`com.viglet.dumont.connector.tenant`): `DumVigletTenantStore` (the `VigletTenantStore` bridge mapping `Dum*`↔`Viglet*` enums), `DumVigletTenantResolver` (resolution sources: `DUM_TENANT` session attr → `X-Dumont-Tenant` header → OIDC `tenant`/`workspace_id` claim; drop-to-default when unresolved), `DumVigletResourceCounter` (`indexings` count + FREE 10k, else unlimited). **Platform-admin:** `DumPlatformAdminService extends VigletPlatformAdminService`, overriding `isPlatformAdmin()` to honour Dumont's `ROLE_ADMIN` (granted to local admins **and** to Turing→Dumont machine calls via `DumApiKeyFilter`) — so those callers bypass the membership gate. **Filter:** `DumTenantFilter extends VigletTenantResolutionFilter`, registered after `BasicAuthenticationFilter` in `DumWebConfiguration` (a complete no-op when `dumont.multi-tenant=false`). **`@TenantId` entity set:** the four connector-data entities (`DumConnectorIndexingModel`, `DumConnectorDependencyModel`, `DumConnectorIndexingStatsModel`, `DumConnectorIndexingRuleModel`); auth entities + the tenant registry stay global, and `DumConnectorConfigVarModel` is deliberately **excluded** (its non-generated string PK would collide across tenants — config stays installation-global). `DumTenancyConfiguration` supplies the `VigletTenantContext` (from `dumont.multi-tenant` + default `dumont`) and the slug validator's `dumont` reserved name; the no-op `VigletTenantProvisioner`/`VigletTenantPurger` defaults are kept (no extra bytes to seed; torn-down `@TenantId` rows go invisible once the catalog row is gone). Schema is created by Hibernate `ddl-auto` (Dumont has no Liquibase). (§XIV.9.4)
- **T397** — **Shio: adopted the full shared tenancy** — re-homed Shio's SH22 tenancy onto the T395 surface, mirroring T396, with the Shio tenancy suite green (`ShTenantMembershipIT`/`ShTenantQuotaIT`/`ShTenantCdaDeliveryIT`/`ShTenantIsolationIT`/`ShStaticFileTenantIsolationIT`/`ShTenantProvisioningIT`/`ShH2SchemaIT` + `ShTenantSuspendedAccessTest`/`ShTenantTeardownServiceTest`/`ShTenantAPITest`). **Five SPI beans** (`com.viglet.shio.tenant`): `ShVigletTenantStore` (the `VigletTenantStore` bridge over `ShTenant`/`ShTenantMembership` — Shio's discriminator is the tenant **name**, so `VigletTenantRef.id`=`name` and the kernel canonicalizes/binds/scopes by name), `ShVigletTenantResolver` (resolution sources: CDA-token request attr → `trusted`, `SH_TENANT` session attr, `workspace_id` claim), `ShVigletResourceCounter` (`sites`/`posts` counts + FREE 2/200, else unlimited), `ShVigletTenantPurger` (`file_source` + image-cache wipe), and `ShPlatformAdminService` — the adopted `VigletPlatformAdminService`, overriding `isPlatformAdmin()` to honour Shio's `ROLE_ADMIN` as platform-admin, closing the gap §XIV.9 flagged (Shio previously had only the `ROLE_ADMIN` filter bypass and no audited cross-tenant primitives). **Services re-homed to thin adapters/subclasses:** `ShTenantFilter` now `extends VigletTenantResolutionFilter` (keeps the `TENANT_REQUEST_ATTR`/`TENANT_SESSION_ATTR` constants; inherits the twice-written gate); `ShTenantMembershipService` delegates signup/slug to `VigletTenantMembershipService`+`VigletTenantSlugValidator` (Shio extras `shio`/`store`/`file_source`); `ShTenantQuotaService`→`VigletQuotaService` (maps back to `ShTenantPlanLimits`); `ShTenantTeardownService`→`VigletTenantTeardownService` (resolving Shio's surrogate-UUID API to the tenant name, keeping the 409 default-guard + its report shape). `ShTenancyConfiguration` wires the `VigletTenantContext` bean from `shio.multi-tenant` (its thread-locals are shared with the static `ShTenantContext` facade) + the best-effort post-signup provisioner bridge; `VigletInfraTenantScope` is now available as a bean (no Shio BYO-infra entity adopts it yet). **Behaviour change adopted (Turing parity):** the legacy username-as-implicit-tenant deny is replaced by drop-to-default — an authenticated request that resolves no explicit tenant now operates in the default `shio` tenant rather than being denied; the re-homed teardown additionally self-gates on platform-admin (defense-in-depth atop the `ROLE_ADMIN`-gated endpoints). (§XIV.9.4)
- **T396** — **Turing: aligned to the expanded shared tenancy kernel** — re-homed Turing's Block J tenancy onto the T395 surface so it consumes it identically, behaviour byte-for-byte (gated by the Block J ITs: `TurTenantInfraVisibilityIT` / `TurCrossTenantIsolationIT` / `TurSNSiteTenantIsolationIT` / `TurSNSiteImportTenantScopingIT`, all green). **Four SPI implementations** (`com.viglet.turing.tenant`): `TurVigletTenantStore` (the `VigletTenantStore` registry bridge over `TurTenant`/`TurTenantMembership` via the neutral `VigletTenantRef`, mapping `Tur*` ↔ `Viglet*` enums and stamping `suspendedAt` on the ACTIVE→SUSPENDED edge per the store contract), `TurVigletTenantResolver` (the `VigletTenantResolver` carrying only Turing's resolution sources — claim/session/subdomain/header + T334 trusted-internal token + T333 personal-tenant auto-provision), `TurVigletResourceCounter` (the `VigletResourceCounter`: `agents`/`sites`/`monthlyTokens` counts + the FREE-plan limits 3/2/100k, else unlimited), `TurVigletTenantPurger` (the `VigletTenantPurger`: storage-prefix + Lucene-dir + cache wipe). **Four services re-homed to thin adapters/subclasses over the lifted base:** `TurTenantResolutionFilter` now `extends VigletTenantResolutionFilter` (keeps the named bean + resolution-source constants for the security configs / switch APIs; the twice-written gate is inherited), `TurTenantService` delegates signup/personal-tenant/slug/list to `VigletTenantMembershipService` + `VigletTenantSlugValidator` (with Turing's extra reserved `turing` slug, wired in `TurTenancyConfiguration`) and maps refs back to `TurTenant`, `TurTenantQuotaService` delegates the per-resource 402/429 checks to `VigletQuotaService`, `TurTenantTeardownService` delegates suspend/delete to `VigletTenantTeardownService` (mapping its report to Turing's). `TurTenantPlanLimits` deleted (subsumed by `VigletPlanLimits` + the counter). The shared membership/quota/teardown/resolution-filter beans auto-activate now that Turing supplies the SPI beans. 55 tenancy unit tests + 10 tenancy IT cases green. (§XIV.9.4)
- **T395** — **Expanded `viglet-core-tenancy` to the full shared feature set** — beyond the four-piece kernel (context / platform-admin / infra-scope / `@TenantId` resolver), lifted the surface the T394 audit found duplicated across Turing + Shio into neutral, SPI-driven classes (all in `com.viglet.core.tenancy`, jar `viglet-core-tenancy`). **Value types/enums:** `VigletTenantRole` (OWNER/ADMIN/MEMBER), `VigletTenantStatus` (ACTIVE/SUSPENDED), `VigletTenantMembershipStatus` (ACTIVE/INVITED/SUSPENDED), `VigletPlanLimits` (map-keyed per-plan limits; `-1`/missing = unlimited), `VigletTenantRef` (neutral read-only registry projection so the kernel stays JPA-free). **Pure logic:** `VigletTenantSlugValidator` (the `^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]{0,61}[a-z0-9])?$` rule + neutral reserved-name set + product extras + `basePersonalSlug`). **Shared services:** `VigletTenantMembershipService` (idempotent signup, personal-tenant resolve/provision, `tenantsOf`/`isActiveMember`/`setStatus`), `VigletQuotaService` (keyed plan-limit enforcement, 402 by default / caller status), `VigletTenantResolutionFilter` (the twice-written **gate** — suspended→403, non-member→403, platform-admin bypass, trusted-internal skip, always-clear-in-finally), `VigletTenantTeardownService` (platform-admin gate + default-tenant guard + dry-run + audit + registry delete). **SPI seams** (per-product adapters from §XIV.9.3): `VigletTenantStore` (registry bridge — the membership-model abstraction realized as a store interface, not a `@MappedSuperclass`, so each product keeps its own entity/id-gen/cache annotations), `VigletTenantResolver` (resolution sources + `Resolution(rawTenant, trusted)`), `VigletTenantProvisioner` / `VigletTenantPurger` (no-op defaults), `VigletResourceCounter` (counts + `limitsFor(plan)`). Auto-config wires every new bean **dormant** — `@ConditionalOnBean` on the product's SPI(s) + `@ConditionalOnMissingBean`, the filter further gated on a servlet stack — so nothing activates until a product adopts it (T396–T398). 39 new unit tests (slug, plan-limits, signup idempotency/conflict/personal-tenant, quota off/default/over/custom-status, teardown gate/guard/dry-run/purge, filter gate matrix); module green at 71 tests. (§XIV.9.2/§XIV.9.3)
- **T394** — **Audit + design the canonical shared tenancy surface** — class-by-class audit of `viglet-core-tenancy` + the three SB-4.0.6 products, producing the design that makes all three behave identically (IMPROVEMENTS §XIV.9.1–§XIV.9.4). Findings: today's kernel ships four pieces (`VigletTenantContext`, `VigletPlatformAdminService`, `VigletInfraTenantScope`/`VigletTenantOwnedInfra`, `VigletTenancyAutoConfiguration` + the conditional Hibernate resolver); **Turing and Shio independently converged on the same membership/quota/signup/suspend/teardown shapes** (hand-synced ports of one design — the cross-tenant-leak risk §XIV.9 names); **Dumont is greenfield** (a `dumont.multi-tenant:false` flag + hidden app switcher, no `viglet-core` dependency). The design (a) classifies each capability **agnostic-mechanism → lifts to core** (membership-model base, signup+slug validation, quota/plan-limits, the **resolution-filter base owning the twice-written gating half**, suspended-access gating, teardown orchestration) vs **product-specific → stays behind an SPI** (the `@TenantId` entity set, the concrete resolution sources, provisioning/purge bodies, infra plumbing, authority mapping), and (b) defines the **per-product adapter contract** — `TenantResolver` / `TenantProvisioner` / `TenantPurger` / `ResourceCounter` SPIs + the `VigletTenantOwnedInfra` registry, each product keeping its `@ConditionalOnMissingBean` override path and per-product default-tenant id. Sets up T395 (expand the kernel to that surface), T396–T398 (align Turing/Shio/Dumont) and T399 (shared conformance test). (§XIV.9)

## Block N — Pluggable reranker (cross-encoder / managed)

> Makes the SN RAG rerank stage (T328) a selectable strategy. Opt-in, **default `LLM`** → existing rerank-on deployments unchanged.

- **T337** — **Reranker strategy seam** — new `genai/rag/rerank/` package: `TurRagRerankStrategy` interface + `TurRagRerankStrategyType` enum (`LLM` | `CROSS_ENCODER` | `COHERE`, lenient `fromValue`) + `TurRagRerankRequest` record (chatModel rides in the record) + `TurRagRerankStrategyFactory` (routes by type, falls back to `LLM`). The old LLM logic moved verbatim into `TurLlmRerankStrategy`; `TurRagReranker` became the fail-open facade (resolves strategy from Global Settings, returns retrieval order on any exception/empty). Caller `TurSNGenAi` unchanged. `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_STRATEGY` (default `LLM`).
- **T338** — **Cross-encoder strategy (self-hosted)** — `TurCrossEncoderRerankStrategy` + shared `TurHttpRerankClient` (JDK HttpClient + Jackson 3, 2s timeout, no retry; tolerates TEI bare-array and Cohere `{results:[]}` shapes) against a TEI/Infinity/Jina-compatible `/rerank`. `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_ENDPOINT` / `_MODEL`.
- **T339** — **Cohere Rerank strategy (managed)** — `TurCohereRerankStrategy` (fixed `/v2/rerank`, default `rerank-v3.5`); API key encrypted via `TurSecretCryptoService` (`GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_API_KEY`), write-only in the bean/API.
- **T340** — **Global Settings UI** — rerank section in the admin Global Settings page: enable switch, strategy `Select`, top-K, and conditional endpoint/model/apiKey fields; api-key `password`/write-only with a configured-status hint. Bean fields exposed via `/api/system/global-settings`; en/pt i18n.
- **T341** — **Rerank-score cache (opt-in)** — `TurRagRerankCache` wraps the strategy call in `@Cacheable("turRagRerankScore")` keyed by `(strategy, model, query, SHA-256(candidate ids+text), topK)`; the `TurRagReranker` facade caches the strategy's **ordered doc-id list** (serialization-safe across Hazelcast), then re-maps ids → live `Document`s. Gated by `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_CACHE_ENABLED` (default off) — when off, the pre-T341 path is byte-identical. Paired `@CacheEvict(allEntries)` on the rerank-config setters (strategy/model/endpoint/api-key/cache-enabled); content edits self-invalidate via the candidate hash. Settings bean/API + en/pt UI toggle.

- **T342** — **Docusaurus blog on docs.viglet.org** — `plugin-content-blog` enabled in the classic preset (RSS + Atom feeds, reading time, `authors.yml`/`tags.yml`, navbar + footer Blog link); blog posts emit `BlogPosting` JSON-LD. Live at `https://docs.viglet.org/blog`. (`vigletdocs.github.io`) (§X.2)

(§X.2)
- **T344** — **Enrich `llms.txt`** — docs `llms-txt.ts` plugin enumerates blog guides/comparisons; viglet.org `vite-plugin-llmtxt.ts` adds a "Guides & Comparisons" section pointing at the docs blog. Both live (`docs.viglet.org/llms.txt`, `www.viglet.org/llms.txt`). (§X.2)

All live. (§X.1)
- **T346** — **GitHub README + topics keyword pass** — `README.adoc`/`README.ce.adoc` lead with "enterprise search for Adobe AEM / WordPress" + Algolia/Coveo/Lucidworks-alternative framing; `openviglet/turing-ce` tagged with 9 discovery topics (`enterprise-search`, `aem`, `wordpress`, `algolia-alternative`, `coveo-alternative`, `rag`, `semantic-search`, `solr`, `elasticsearch`). (§X.1)

Cross-links to Dumont/Turing docs all resolve. Medium `canonical` re-point deferred to T354. (§X.3)

Live; Turing ES claims grounded in the same connector facts verified for T347. (§X.3)

## Block P — Open-core source split (`turing` → `turing-ce`)

> Publish the runtime as a public community edition while keeping strategy docs, history, and the (future) paid add-on private. Boundary + license → [STRATEGY.md](STRATEGY.md) §XI; publish mechanics → [IMPROVEMENTS.md](IMPROVEMENTS.md) §XIX.

- **T356** — **`ce-exclude.txt` boundary manifest** — checked-in gitignore-style denylist at the repo root that is the single source of the public/private rule: default-deny `docs/**` design content (re-includes only CHANGELOG / RAG_HYBRID_CONFIG / MULTI_TENANCY / chat-flow samples), explicit strategy/roadmap/improvements + `docs/products/**` deny, internal AI/contributor instructions (`agents.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude-instructions`, copilot-instructions), internal publish workflows + GitHub Pages site, secrets/`.env.local`/keystores, scratch dirs, the internal publish tooling, and the internal `README.adoc`/`COMMERCIAL-LICENSE.md`. Consumed by the T357 publish script; T358 secret-scan is the backstop. (§XIX.2)
- **T357** — **One-way publish script** — `scripts/publish-ce.sh`: assembles a clean tree at a release ref via `git archive`, applies the **versioned** `ce-exclude.txt` (extracted from the same ref) through git's own ignore engine (so `!` re-includes are honoured), promotes `README.ce.adoc → README.adoc`, runs the fail-closed secret-scan hook (T358 plug-in point; refuses `--push` when no scanner is installed unless `--allow-no-scan`), then appends **one squashed `Release vX.Y.Z` commit** to `turing-ce`'s default branch — no private history mirrored. Default is a dry run; `--push` publishes. Verified end-to-end on `HEAD` (denied paths stripped, code/user-docs kept). (§XIX.3)
- **T361** — **Contribution inflow + DCO** — `CONTRIBUTING.md` (ways to contribute, dev setup, PR guidelines, and the snapshot/backport flow from the contributor's POV), the verbatim `DCO` 1.1 text, and a `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` checklist — all kept (not denylisted) so they ship into the `turing-ce` snapshot. Requires a **DCO sign-off** (`git commit -s`) per commit instead of a CLA, which keeps contributions under Apache 2.0 and preserves the single-owner relicensing option; the rationale stays private (only the requirement is public). (§XIX.5)
- **T360** — **CI publish workflow** — `.github/workflows/publish-ce.yml` (itself denylisted), the source-tree counterpart to `publish-docker.yml`: installs gitleaks (so T358's scan is real) and runs `scripts/publish-ce.sh`. **`workflow_dispatch`** with a `push` boolean (default **false** = dry run); the snapshot lands on a `turing-ce` branch whose name **mirrors the branch the run was dispatched from** (run on `2026.3` → publishes to a `2026.3` branch on turing-ce, **created as an orphan** if absent so it inherits no other branch's history). No on-tag trigger on the private repo — release **tags/Releases are created on turing-ce by `publish.yml`** (`gh release create --repo openviglet/turing-ce`), so this workflow is the manual/dry-run entry point and the building block the release pipeline invokes. Push auth reuses the existing **`GH_PKG_TOKEN`** (already pushes git content to `turing-ce`, so it has the needed `contents: write`) injected via git's `insteadOf` (token never logged); user-controlled inputs are bound to env vars to avoid run-block injection. (§XIX.6)
- **T359** — **Squash-snapshot history model + public README** — the append-one-commit-per-release model is implemented in the T357 publish script; this task adds the curated `README.ce.adoc` (titled "Community Edition", `turing-ce` clone URLs, accurate frontend monorepo structure, badges trimmed to license/docker/docs/download, and a note explaining the one-commit-per-release snapshot + contribution flow). The publish script promotes it to `README.adoc` in the public tree; the internal `README.adoc` stays denylisted. (§XIX.3)
- **T358** — **Fail-closed secret-scan gate** — two layers over the *assembled* tree: (a) an **always-on built-in pattern scan** driven by `scripts/ce-deny-patterns.txt` (itself denylisted) catching internal hostnames / customer names / high-signal secret markers (PEM blocks, `AKIA…`, `ghp_…`, Slack/Google keys) — patterns verified zero-match against the current tree, with the false-positive-prone ones (private-IP URLs in test fixtures, the `vilt-group.com` support contact) shipped commented; (b) **gitleaks/trufflehog** for entropy/known formats, a real run of which is now mandatory before `--push`. Any hit aborts; matches are **redacted** in output so secrets don't re-leak through CI logs. (§XIX.4)

## Block R — Structured-source indexing & the cursarei reference (with Dumont)

> Conservative grounding (absent ≠ empty) + objective ranking for self-hosted structured-catalog search. Rationale → [STRATEGY.md](STRATEGY.md) §XIII; designs → [IMPROVEMENTS.md](IMPROVEMENTS.md) §XX.

- **T381** — **Conservative-grounding indexing contract** — pinned and documented the absent-vs-empty rule for `TurSNJobItem.attributes`: an absent field is a missing key (never `null`/`""`/empty collection), and the indexing boundary (`TurSNProcessQueue.getConsolidateResults` / `removeDuplicateTerms`, via the shared `isGroundedValue` predicate) drops `null`, blank-string, and empty-collection values so they are never materialized as an empty value in Solr/ES — keeping facet counts and `exists`-negation filters correct. Documented as the indexing-side half of Dumont's grounding rule (D03); covered by `TurSNProcessQueueTest` (predicate + dedup + end-to-end CREATE). (§XX.1)
- **T382** — **Field-manifest site provisioning** — one idempotent `POST /api/sn/manifest` (`TurSNSiteManifestService`) that creates-or-converges an SN site **and** its field schema from a single declarative `TurSNSiteManifest` (site + locale codes + ordered `TurSNJobAttributeSpec` field specs: type, multi-valued, facet, mandatory, localized facet labels). Re-posting the same manifest is a no-op; adding a field/locale is additive. Field creation was extracted from `TurSNProcessQueue.createMissingFields` into the shared `TurSNFieldProvisioner`, so the indexing-time auto-create path and the manifest path converge on one implementation (the "builds on `TurSNJobAttributeSpec`" intent). Foundation for schema-as-code (T386) + LLM-assisted derivation (T387). Covered by `TurSNFieldProvisionerTest` + `TurSNSiteManifestServiceTest`. (§XX.2)
- **T383** — **RRF hybrid ranking for public SN search** — opt-in `TurSNRankingMode` (`LEGACY` default / `HYBRID_RRF`) on `TurSNSiteGenAi`, distinct from the RAG flags. On a `HYBRID_RRF` site, `TurSNHybridRankingService` side-writes each document's embedding into a per-site embedded vector collection (`sn_<siteId>`) as it is indexed/deindexed (reusing `TurRagContextBuilder.buildFromGlobalSettings` + the default embedding model/store), and at query time — when the sort is relevance, the query isn't `*`, and results aren't grouped — runs a vector pass and fuses the BM25 page with it via the shared `TurRagRrf` (k=60), reordering the page while leaving facet counts and pagination BM25-derived. Fully fail-open: no default embedding model/store, an embedding error, or a store outage all degrade to the legacy lexical order; legacy sites never touch the service. Admin "Ranking Híbrido (Busca Pública)" card on the SN GenAI page; Liquibase `v2026.3.1.37`. Covered by `TurSNHybridRankingServiceTest` + round-trip in `TurSNSiteAPITest`. Generalized into a pluggable `retrieve → fuse → rerank` pipeline + explainability by T389. (§XX.3)
- **T384** — **Public "similar documents" endpoint** — first-class `GET /api/sn/{siteName}/search/similar?id=&rows=&locale=&mode=` (`TurSNSimilarDocumentsService`) returning `TurSESimilarResult`s most like a given document, so a catalog renders "similar courses" live from the index instead of shipping a pre-baked neighbors file. Two strategies (`TurSNSimilarMode`): **VECTOR** — semantic neighbors over the T383 per-site `sn_<siteId>` collection via the new `TurSNHybridRankingService.findNeighbors`, hydrated back into renderable results; **MLT** — lexical MoreLikeThis over the live index via the new `TurSearchEnginePlugin.getSimilarDocuments` (Solr `/mlt` keyed by `id:` with a BM25-over-seed-text fallback; Elasticsearch `more_like_this`) plus `getDocumentsByIds`. Auto-selects VECTOR on `HYBRID_RRF` sites and MLT otherwise; the VECTOR path fails open to MLT when there are no neighbors or no seed. Similarity is content-based only — never a commercial signal (Block R objective-ranking invariant). The vector-neighbor primitive is the basis for cross-source dedup (T390). Covered by `TurSNSimilarDocumentsServiceTest` + `findNeighbors` cases in `TurSNHybridRankingServiceTest`. (§XX.4)
- **T385** — **NL→facet query-parsing eval pack for faceted catalogs** — a self-hosted regression harness for the prose→filters/ranges/sort path that replaces a catalog's hand-written `nlFilters`. A `TurNLFacetParser` seam (production `TurLlmNLFacetParser` drives the default LLM with a constrained prompt; tests inject a deterministic fake) turns prose into a `TurDslQueryRequest` over a declared field schema; a pure `TurNLFacetEvalScorer` checks each golden expected filter/range/sort is present (tolerant: `lt`≈`lte`, `terms` multi-value, nested `bool`) and hard-fails any field outside the schema — the objective-grounding invariant made testable. `TurNLFacetEvalService` runs a `TurNLFacetEvalPack` (self-contained schema + cases, or schema resolved from the live site like `dsl_get_mappings`) and aggregates a `TurNLFacetEvalReport`; `TurNLFacetEvalGenerator` auto-derives a candidate pack from a T382 manifest (caller supplies facet samples / range thresholds — never fabricated). Runtime `POST /api/sn/nl-facet-eval` (+ `/available`) is the agent-CI-gate analog for the NL→facet path; feeds the catalog copilot (T392). Covered by `TurNLFacetEvalScorerTest` + `TurNLFacetEvalServiceTest` (catalog fixture, golden/hallucinating/throwing parsers). (§XX.5)
- **T386** — **Manifest as versioned schema-as-code (diff + additive migration)** — promoted the T382 manifest to a versioned artifact (`TurSNSiteManifest.schemaVersion` + `migrations`) and made provisioning a *diff-and-converge* op. `TurSNManifestPlanner` diffs a manifest against the live site into `fieldsToAdd` (additive — applied silently), `breakingChanges` (a `type`/`multiValued` change on an existing field), `fieldsUnchanged`, and `localesToAdd`. `TurSNSiteManifestService.provision` rejects any *undeclared* breaking change with `TurSNManifestMigrationRequiredException` (HTTP 409, nothing mutated); a breaking change declared via a `TurSNFieldMigration` (`RECREATE`) is applied by the new `TurSNFieldProvisioner.recreateField` (drops the field's DB rows + SE schema across every locale core, then recreates from spec — destructive, must reindex). New dry-run `POST /api/sn/manifest/plan` returns the `TurSNManifestDiff` so a schema change is reviewable in a PR before apply. The "Liquibase for the SN field schema" contract the team already trusts. Facet/mandatory/description tweaks stay non-breaking. Covered by `TurSNManifestPlannerTest` + new cases in `TurSNSiteManifestServiceTest`. (§XX.6)
- **T387** — **LLM-assisted manifest derivation from sample documents** — `POST /api/sn/manifest/derive` takes a sample of a source's documents (parsed JSON objects) and returns a **draft** `TurSNSiteManifest` (per-field `TurSEFieldType`, facet/multi-valued/mandatory) for human review, so onboarding a structured source starts from a generated schema instead of hand-declaring every field. Split so the useful part runs without an LLM: the pure `TurSNManifestSampleAnalyzer` computes per-field statistics (coverage, observed scalar types, cardinality, multi-valued, max length) honoring the absent ≠ empty contract (a `null` value counts as *absent*, lowering coverage, never as a present empty value); `TurSNManifestHeuristics` maps those to a baseline draft (numeric/date/bool → facet, low-cardinality string → facet, `TEXT` never a facet, present-in-nearly-every-doc → mandatory, mixed numerics widen to `DOUBLE`). The `TurSNManifestDeriver` seam's production `TurLlmManifestDeriver` grounds the configured default LLM on those observations to *refine* the baseline (better type/facet calls + human descriptions), but it may only refine fields actually observed — an invented field name is dropped and an omitted field keeps its heuristic spec, so the draft never hallucinates or loses a field (conservative grounding); any LLM error falls back to the heuristic draft. Echoes the request's `name`/`seInstanceId`/`locales` into the draft so the reviewed manifest POSTs straight back to `/api/sn/manifest`. `GET /api/sn/manifest/derive/available` reports whether the LLM path is live. The draft is never auto-provisioned. Feeds the onboarding wizard (T391). Covered by `TurSNManifestSampleAnalyzerTest` + `TurSNManifestHeuristicsTest` + `TurLlmManifestDeriverTest` (deterministic, no LLM). (§XX.7)
- **T389** — **Pluggable ranking pipeline + ranking explainability** — generalized T383's `HYBRID_RRF` boolean into a configurable `retrieve → fuse → rerank` pipeline by adding a third `TurSNRankingMode`, `HYBRID_RRF_RERANK`, on `TurSNSiteGenAi`. After the BM25+vector RRF fusion, the fused page is re-ordered once more through the **Block N `TurRagReranker` strategy seam** (LLM | CROSS_ENCODER | COHERE, selected in Global Settings) via a new full-list `TurRagReranker.reorder` (keeps every result, no top-k truncation) — the SN and RAG ranking stacks now share one reranker abstraction instead of a parallel path. New `TurDefaultChatModelResolver` supplies the default chat model for the LLM strategy (best-effort; HTTP strategies skip it). Every hybrid result also carries an objective **ranking explanation** (`pipeline`, `lexicalRank`, `semanticRank`, `fusedRank`, `rrfScore`, `finalRank`, `rerankerDelta`, `rerankStrategy`) on `TurSEResult` → `TurSNSiteSearchDocumentBean.rankingExplanation` (omitted via `@JsonInclude(NON_EMPTY)` so the legacy response is byte-identical) — the audit surface for the objective-ranking claim; no commercial signal ever enters the pipeline. Fully fail-open and opt-in (default `LEGACY` untouched). Admin "Hybrid + Rerank" mode button (en/pt); column widened to `varchar(32)` via Liquibase `v2026.3.1.38`. Covered by new cases in `TurSNHybridRankingServiceTest` + `TurRagRerankerTest`. (§XX.9)
- **T390** — **Multi-source duplicate clustering** — reuses the T384 vector-neighbor machinery, tuned for identity rather than similarity, to collapse the same real-world entity arriving from many sources (the identical course listed by five portals) into one cluster while keeping every member's provenance. New `TurSNHybridRankingService.findScoredNeighbors(site, seed, topK, minSimilarity)` returns `TurSNScoredNeighbor`s (id + cosine score) and applies a minimum-similarity cut-off both as the store's `similarityThreshold` and defensively against `Document.getScore()` — a scoreless hit is conservatively dropped under any positive threshold so distinct documents are never wrongly merged (`findNeighbors` now delegates to it with threshold 0). `TurSNDuplicateClusteringService.findCluster` hydrates the high-similarity neighbors into `TurSNDuplicateCluster` (canonical = lexicographically smallest member id, so any cluster member resolves to the same representative; `source` provenance from `source_apps`); vector-only and fail-safe — non-hybrid sites, a missing seed, or no neighbors return a single-member, non-duplicate cluster. Exposed two ways: standalone via public `GET /api/sn/{siteName}/search/duplicates?id=&threshold=0.92&rows=&locale=` (`TurSNSiteSearchAPI`), and **inline on each search result** — `TurSNDuplicateClusteringService.attachClusters` enriches the result page (one vector pass per result, a single batched id-hydration for the whole page) so each `TurSNSiteSearchDocumentBean.duplicateCluster` carries its near-identical siblings. This revives the previously-dead, Solr-only lexical `widget.similar`/MLT path: the inline clustering reuses the existing per-site **MoreLikeThis toggle** (`turSNSite.getMlt()`) as its opt-in and runs only on hybrid sites, so legacy responses are byte-identical (`duplicateCluster` omitted via `@JsonInclude(NON_NULL)`). The cluster DTOs moved to `turing-commons` (`TurSNDuplicateCluster` / `TurSNDuplicateClusterMember`) and are surfaced through both SDKs (`@viglet/turing-sdk` + `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`) as `TurDocument.duplicateCluster`. Content-based only — the canonical never reflects a commercial signal (Block R objective-ranking invariant). Covered by `TurSNDuplicateClusteringServiceTest` (standalone + inline `attachClusters`) + `findScoredNeighbors` cases in `TurSNHybridRankingServiceTest`. (§XX.10)
- **T388** — **Per-field coverage / completeness observability** — turns the T381 absent ≠ empty contract into a data-quality signal. New `TurSearchEnginePlugin.getDocumentCountWithField(locale, field)` field-presence primitive (Solr `field:[* TO *]`, Lucene `FieldExistsQuery`, ES `exists`; default `-1` = unsupported so the UI degrades to "unknown" instead of a misleading 0%); `TurSNFieldCoverageService` sums presence/total across all locales for every enabled field and reports `% populated` ordered worst-first. Exposed via `GET /api/sn/{id}/field-coverage` (`TurSNSiteFieldCoverageAPI` → `TurSNFieldCoverageReport`); admin "Field Coverage" page (own nav item) with per-field bars (red/amber/green) and a summary card. Pure count over the index — no new indexing behaviour. Covered by `TurSNFieldCoverageServiceTest`. (§XX.8)
- **T408** — **Admin wizard: generate SN fields from sample documents** — the frontend surface for T387. A "Generate from sample" action on the SN site **Fields** page (`sn.site.field.list.page.tsx`) opens a 2-step `SNFieldWizardDialog`: (1) paste or upload a sample of the source's documents (JSON array, single object, or NDJSON — parsed client-side); (2) review the **draft** schema in a table (name, `BadgeFieldType`, facet/multi/required flags) and pick which fields to add. Step 1 calls `POST /api/sn/manifest/derive` (via new `TurSNManifestService` + `useDeriveManifest`); applying converges only the chosen fields into the live site through the idempotent `POST /api/sn/manifest` (`useProvisionManifest`), so existing fields are shown but pre-excluded and re-applying is a no-op. Surfaces the heuristic-only note when no LLM is configured (`GET /api/sn/manifest/derive/available`); resolves the site name + SE instance via a new `useSnSite(id)` detail query. Nothing is created until apply, and the result toast reports created/skipped counts. New `sn-manifest.model.ts` + `sn-manifest.queries.ts`; i18n under `sn.fields.wizard.*` (en + pt). (§XX.7)
- **T391** — **Structured-source onboarding wizard** — the capstone flow that chains the block's indexing capabilities into one guided, minutes-long operation a non-engineer can run, surfaced as a standalone page at `/admin/sn/onboarding` (route `SN_ONBOARDING`; "Onboard a catalog" action on the SN site-list `GridList`). Four steps over the existing endpoints, orchestrated client-side (matching the `SNFieldWizardDialog` precedent — no new backend, no IT revalidation): (1) **Sample & identity** — name the catalog, pick the search-engine instance + optional locales, paste/upload a JSON/NDJSON document sample → `POST /api/sn/manifest/derive` (T387); (2) **Review schema** — select which derived fields to keep in a `BadgeFieldType` table; (3) **Create catalog** — converge the chosen fields via the idempotent `POST /api/sn/manifest` (T382/T386), invalidating the SN-site cache; (4) **Validate** — run an NL→facet eval pack against the live schema via `POST /api/sn/nl-facet-eval` (T385), pre-filled with a runnable starter pack derived from the provisioned fields (a facetable-string term case + a numeric-range case), then **go live** with quick links to configure / open search / add fields. The eval step degrades gracefully when no default LLM is configured (`GET /api/sn/nl-facet-eval/available`). T385 gained its first frontend surface here: new `sn-nl-facet-eval.model.ts` + `sn.nl-facet-eval.service.ts` + `sn-nl-facet-eval.queries.ts` (`useNLFacetEvalAvailable` / `useRunNLFacetEval`), covered by `sn.nl-facet-eval.service.test.ts`. i18n under `sn.onboarding.*` (en + pt). (§XX.11)
- **T392** — **Grounded conversational catalog copilot** — the GenAI value layer that composes the block's three primitives into a "talk to the catalog" assistant. `TurCatalogCopilotService.answer(site, locale, messages)` parses the latest user turn into a grounded `TurDslQueryRequest` over the site's **declared** field schema via the T385 `TurNLFacetParser` (free-text fallback when no LLM / parse fails), executes it against the live index through `TurDslSearchService`, reorders the result page through the T383 `TurSNHybridRankingService` when the site runs a hybrid mode (objective signals only — RRF/rerank, never a commercial boost; carries the ranking explanation through), then grounds the default LLM on those objectively-ranked, cited results so the answer is built **strictly** from the index and cites each claim as `[n]`. Both Block-R invariants preserved (conservative grounding + objective ranking) and the whole path is fail-open (parse → free-text, hybrid → lexical, LLM failure → still returns the matched `TurCatalogCitation`s so the client renders results without a synthesized answer). Two surfaces share one retrieval step: the dedicated `POST /api/sn/{siteName}/copilot` (multi-turn, `TurSNCatalogCopilotAPI` → `TurCatalogCopilotResult`; `GET …/copilot/available` gates on a default LLM) and a new `catalog_search` `@Tool` (`TurCatalogCopilotToolService`, description in `prompts/tools/catalog/catalog_search.md`) registered into the semantic-navigation agent so the chat agent can talk to a catalog in prose without hand-writing DSL. Covered by `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` (deterministic — fake parser + stubbed search + canned LLM, no live index). (§XX.12)
- **T393** — **Lifted the manifest + grounding contract into `viglet-core`** — promoted the product-neutral half of the Block-R schema-as-code work into a new **`viglet-core-manifest`** module (`com.viglet.core.manifest`, viglet-core `2026.3.5`) so Shio (CMS post-types) and Dumont (structured connectors) can declare and ground a structured-source schema the same way. Lifted: the conservative-grounding contract (`VigletGrounding.isGroundedValue` — the absent ≠ empty predicate from T381), the neutral field model (`VigletFieldType` + `VigletFieldSpec`), the manifest records (`VigletFieldManifest`, `VigletFieldMigration`, `VigletManifestMigrationAction`, `VigletManifestDiff`, `VigletManifestFieldChange`, `VigletManifestResult`, `VigletManifestFieldObservation`), and the sample-driven derivation kernel (`VigletManifestSampleAnalyzer` + `VigletManifestHeuristics` + the `VigletManifestDeriver`/`VigletManifestDeriveRequest` seam). What stays in Turing is everything bound to a search engine / persistence / LLM: `TurSNFieldProvisioner`, `TurSNSiteManifestService`, `TurSNManifestPlanner`, the REST controller, and `TurLlmManifestDeriver` (now `implements VigletManifestDeriver`), each mapping `VigletFieldSpec` ↔ Turing's published `TurSNJobAttributeSpec`/`TurSEFieldType` at the boundary via the new `TurSNManifestMapper` (lossless name-keyed enum translation). `TurSNProcessQueue.isGroundedValue` and `TurNLFacetEvalGenerator` delegate to the kernel. The `/api/sn/manifest` JSON contract is byte-identical (record component names match the old Lombok properties; `-parameters` retained), so the T408 wizard / T391 onboarding flow and all callers are unchanged. Honors both viglet-core day-1 rules (neutral identity, one-way arrow). Kernel covered by `VigletGroundingTest` + `VigletManifestSampleAnalyzerTest` + `VigletManifestHeuristicsTest` in viglet-core; Turing's adapter behavior stays green in the existing manifest/queue/eval tests. (was §XX.13)
- **T407** — **End-to-end NL→facet eval IT (real LLM + live index)** — the missing integration test that exercises the whole NL→facet path the T385 unit tests only cover with a fake parser. `TurNLFacetEvalIT` (extends `AbstractTuringSpringIT`) creates a Lucene SE instance against a throwaway temp dir, provisions a small catalog site + field schema through the **T382 manifest** (`TurSNSiteManifestService`), indexes a handful of fixture courses, then asserts four things: (1) the manifest-provisioned schema + indexed docs are queryable by field via hand-built DSL term/`bool` queries (no LLM); (2) the real `TurLlmNLFacetParser` parses every prose case grounded (no field outside the declared schema — the Block R invariant) and scored; (3) each parsed `TurDslQueryRequest` is *executable* against the live index via `TurDslSearchService` (catches DSL that scores well but fails to run); (4) the parsed "online graduate" query returns **only** online courses, proving the parsed filter hit the live index. Gated on `OPENAI_API_KEY` (`@EnabledIfEnvironmentVariable`) and excluded from the default Failsafe profile (`*NLFacetEvalIT.java`); run via `mvn verify -Pnl-facet-eval`, mirroring `-Pagent-eval`. PR/CI runs stay zero-cost. (was §XX.14)
- **T409** — **Lucene DSL executor: type-aware numeric/currency range + sort** — fixed two correctness bugs in `TurDslLuceneExecutor` that T407 surfaced. Numeric/currency/date fields are indexed as Lucene `*Point` + `NumericDocValues` (never string terms), but the executor translated every `range` as a lexicographic `TermRangeQuery` (matched **nothing** on those fields) and every `sort` as a `SortedSetSortField` (**threw** on numeric doc-values → the whole query returned empty). The executor now resolves the site's `field → TurSEFieldType` map (`TurSNSiteRepository` + `TurSNSiteFieldService`) and translates type-aware: `range` → `IntPoint`/`LongPoint`/`FloatPoint`/`DoublePoint.newRangeQuery` (with `gt`/`lt` exclusivity via the next representable value; `CURRENCY` strips the `,ISO` suffix like indexing does) for numeric/date and `TermRangeQuery` for string/unknown; `sort` → a typed `SortField` (`INT`/`LONG`/`FLOAT`/`DOUBLE`, `DATE`→`LONG`) for numeric and `SortedSetSortField` for string, with field-type context threaded through the whole `bool`/`nested`/`span` recursion. Sorting is also **fail-open**: an unsortable field degrades to relevance order instead of dropping the result set. The interface entry points (`translateQuery`/`translateSort`) keep the string-fallback behavior, so Solr/ES executors and existing callers/tests are untouched. This makes numeric/currency range filtering and sorting actually work for catalogs on the embedded Lucene engine (the cursarei/T385 NL→facet path). Covered by `TurDslLuceneExecutorTest` (deterministic translation unit test) + the now-green T407 IT.

## Block S — SDK / client parity (vanilla + React)

> Close the known gaps where a public REST/SSE endpoint shipped without a JS client wrapper, and add a guard so it stops happening. Both SDKs stay in lockstep. Designs → [IMPROVEMENTS.md](IMPROVEMENTS.md) §XXI.

- **T404** — **SDK semantic-chat client** — wrapped `POST /api/v2/llm/{id}/semantic-chat` (an LLM running the Semantic Navigation tool set — list_sites / get_site_fields / search_site / catalog_search + any MCP tools, over SSE) in both SDKs so a widget can "talk to the index" through tool calls without configuring a full AI Agent. Vanilla `@viglet/turing-sdk`: `postSemanticChat(client, llmInstanceId, messages, { onToken, signal })` + a bound `createTuringApi().postSemanticChat`. React `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`: `postSemanticChat(llmInstanceId, messages, opts)` (axios-baseURL + CSRF-primed `fetch`, like `postLlmChat`) + a `useTuringSemanticChat({ llmInstanceId })` hook mirroring `useTuringLlmChat` (same `send`/`stop`/`reset`/streaming shape). JSON-only — unlike `postLlmChat` it drops the multipart `files` path because the model reaches content via tool calls, not uploads; reuses the existing `consumeAssistantStream` SSE envelope unchanged. New shared `PostSemanticChatOptions` type owned by the vanilla SDK and re-exported by the React SDK. (§XXI.5)
- **T400** — **SDK "similar documents" client** — wrapped T384's `GET /sn/{site}/search/similar` in both SDKs: vanilla `fetchSimilar` + an observable `createSimilarController`; React `fetchSimilar` + a `useTuringSimilar` hook (auto-loads on `documentId`, exposes imperative `load`). Surfaces the `mode` (VECTOR|MLT) + `rows` knobs (both optional → server auto-selects). Renders a "related / you may also like" rail with one hook. (was §XXI.1)
- **T401** — **SDK multimodal slot-upload + flow resume** — wrapped T64's `POST /sn/{site}/chat/slot-upload` (IMAGE/AUDIO/FILE + optional vision-LLM extraction) and `POST /sn/{site}/chat/resume` (resume a conversation parked at a suspend node, optionally applying slot updates) in both SDKs: vanilla `postSiteSlotUpload` / `postSiteChatResume`; React the same fns + `useTuringSlotUpload` / `useTuringResume` (session-aware, status-tracked). Completes the chat-capture surface the SDKs already half-covered. (was §XXI.2)
- **T402** — **SDK spell-check client** — wrapped `GET /sn/{site}/{locale}/spell-check` as `fetchSpellCheck` (both SDKs, reuses the existing `TurSpellCheck` shape) + React `useTuringSpellCheck` (exposes a convenience `suggestion` string), so a search box renders "did you mean" without parsing it out of the search bean. (was §XXI.3)
- **T403** — **SDK DSL search client** — wrapped the Elasticsearch-compatible `POST /sn/{site}/_search` (typed `dslSearch` → `TurDslSearchResponse`) and the raw Solr-style `POST /sn/{site}/query` (`dslQuery` → parsed JSON) in both SDKs + React `useTuringDslSearch`, for power users building bool/filter/aggs queries from JS without the facet-param layer. New `TurDslSearchRequest` / `TurDslSearchResponse` / `TurDslSearchHit` types owned by the vanilla SDK. (was §XXI.4)
- **T405** — **SDK platform discovery helpers** — thin typed getters for `GET /discovery`, `/features`, `/locale`, `/llm/vendor` plus `POST /v2/summary` in both SDKs (`fetchDiscovery` / `fetchFeatures` / `fetchSystemLocales` / `fetchLlmVendors` / `postSummary`) + React `useTuringPlatformInfo` (bundles discovery + features with optional locales/vendors), so a generic embed self-configures at runtime instead of hard-coding which sites/features/locales/vendors exist. New `TurDiscoveryInfo` / `TurFeaturesInfo` / `TurSystemLocale` / `TurSummaryResult` types. (was §XXI.6)
- **T406** — **SDK parity guard (contract test)** — `frontend/packages/sdk/src/__tests__/sdk-parity.test.ts` enumerates the public client-facing endpoints (`PUBLIC_ENDPOINTS`) and asserts each has a wrapper in **both** SDKs — the vanilla checked at runtime (named export resolves to a function), the React checked by source text (barrel re-export + `core/api.ts` definition) so the test stays a zero-dependency Node test (no axios / React / jsdom). Adding a public endpoint to the registry now fails CI until both wrappers exist — "ship a public endpoint" includes "ship its SDK wrapper" by construction. Fixes the root cause behind this block. (was §XXI.7)
