## Block W — Agent↔UI transparency & client tools (AG-UI parity, §XXII)

- **T436** — **Live tool-call events on the chat SSE** — opt-in per-agent (`toolCallEventsEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.53`, default off → existing streams byte-for-byte identical). On a CALL-mode turn the per-turn `TurToolCallCollector` gets a live listener attached, so each server-side tool invocation streams a `tool_call` SSE event (`start` with a redacted arg digest, then `end` with status + `durationMs`) merged into the turn ahead of the answer token — the UI can show "calling `search_knowledge_base`…" while the tool loop runs. Args are redacted/truncated by `TurToolArgsSummary` (never raw secrets). Reuses the T427 `TurChatToolCall` shape so a live event and the read-only trace agree; the agent settings form gains a "Live Tool Activity" switch (en/pt). (§XXII.1)
- **T437** — **SDK + headless UI consume tool-call events** — both SDKs parse the `tool_call` event: `createChatController` (vanilla) and `useTuringChat` (React) expose a `toolCalls: TurChatToolCall[]` array on the in-flight assistant message (merged by `callId`, live `onToolCall` callback + final value). New headless **`TuringToolActivity`** atom in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` (zero-dep, same `classNames`/`labels`/`icons` skinning contract as `TuringThinkingDots`/`TuringSourceChips`) renders the running/finished tool list with `data-status`. The admin chat skins it above the answer bubble (en/pt) — viglet.com adopts the published atom the same way. Covered by an `AgentChatTab` SSE test. (§XXII.2)
- **T438** — **Client-tool protocol (backend round-trip)** — opt-in per-agent (`clientToolsEnabled` + `clientToolsJson` declarations `[{name,description,schema}]`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.54`). Declared frontend tools are advertised to the model (`TurClientToolService` → `TurClientToolCallback`); when the model calls one, `TurToolExecutionLoop.callWithClientTools` throws `TurClientToolParkException` and the dispatcher **parks** the turn (keyed by `(conversationId, callId)`) and emits a `client_tool_call` SSE event `{callId,name,args}` instead of an answer. The browser runs the tool and POSTs to **`POST /api/v2/chat/client-tool-result`** `{conversationId, callId, result|error}`; the dispatcher appends the tool response and re-dispatches via `dispatchCall`, streaming the continuation (which may answer or park again on another client tool). Only declared names are callable; unknown/expired park → 404. Park store is in-memory (`TurClientToolParkRegistry`, single-node MVP + TTL sweep; a persisted/cluster-safe park is a follow-up). SDK `registerClientTool` is T439. (§XXII.3)
- **T439** — **SDK client-tool registration** — both SDKs consume the `client_tool_call` event and drive the round-trip. Vanilla `createChatController` gains `registerClientTool(name, handler)` + a `clientTools` option; React `useTuringChat` gains a `clientTools` option + `registerClientTool`/`unregisterClientTool`. On a `client_tool_call` the SDK runs the handler with the parsed args, POSTs `{conversationId, callId, result|error}` to `/v2/chat/client-tool-result` (`postClientToolResult`), and consumes the continuation into the same assistant message — looping for chained calls (hop cap 10), accumulating text + tool activity across legs. A missing/throwing handler is reported to the agent as a tool error (it recovers), never thrown into the UI; mirrors `useCopilotAction` for a thin CopilotKit migration. Covered by controller round-trip tests. (§XXII.4)
- **T440** — **Generative UI via client tools** — builds on T438/T439, no new backend. New React hook **`useGenerativeUI(registry)`** turns a name→component registry into client-tool handlers: when the agent calls a tool named after a registered component, the handler renders it (adds a pending item) and parks the turn on a promise resolved when the component calls `respond` (immediately for display-only, or on user interaction for a picker/configurator) — the value flows back as the tool result and the turn continues. New headless **`TuringGenerativeContent`** atom in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` dispatches each item to its component by name (the same dispatch model as `TuringRichContent`, keyed by name instead of segment type), zero-dep + skinnable. Component names map 1:1 to declared client-tool names. Covered by RTL + renderHook tests. (§XXII.5)

## Block X — Creative experiences at the search × agent intersection (§XXIII)

- **T450** — **Embeddable action widget (Turing as an action layer)** — opt-in per-agent (`actionWidgetEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.61`, default off). Built-in host-action client tools (`navigate` / `fill_form` / `click_element` / `add_to_cart`) folded into the advertised set via `TurActionWidgetClientTools` (the `TurBuiltInClientToolProvider` seam) + injected `prompts/action-widget.md`, so the agent can ACT on the page the widget is embedded in, not just talk. Frontend: a zero-dep vanilla-SDK **`createHostActions(options)`** returning `{ clientTools }` with safe DOM-driven defaults (navigation gated behind `allowNavigate`/`onNavigate`; `add_to_cart` requires a host `onAddToCart` hook; `fill_form`/`click_element` resolve by CSS selector or input name and fail safe without a DOM) — spread into `createChatController({ clientTools })`. Turing stops being "a search box you embed" and becomes an action layer over any website, no browser extension. Covered by `TurClientToolServiceTest` + `host-actions.test.ts`. (§XXIII.9)
- **T449** — **Skills that ship their own UI** — a skill folder can embed a `ui/components.json` (`[{name, description, schema}]`); `TurSkillUiService` reads it (cached per skill) and exposes each component as a generative-UI client tool under a skill-qualified `toolName` (`<skill>__<component>`, collision-safe). A new **dynamic** `TurSkillUiClientToolProvider` (the provider seam widened to `declarations(TurAIAgent)` — transparent to the static answer-as-app/co-browse/user-memory providers) advertises the available skills' UI tools when the agent has `skillsEnabled` and the skill layer is available, so the agent renders a skill's own UI through the same T440 surface. Discovery via **`GET /api/skill/{id}/ui-components`**; frontend `TurSkillUiComponent` model + `TurSkillService.uiComponents` + `useSkillUiComponents` hook let the host register a renderer per `toolName` into `useGenerativeUI`. Skills graduate from prompt+scripts to vertical apps. Covered by `TurSkillUiServiceTest` + `TurSkillUiClientToolProviderTest`. (§XXIII.8)
- **T448** — **Agent-to-agent handoff (a visible team)** — opt-in per-agent (`agentHandoffEnabled` + `specialistAgentIds` CSV allowlist, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.60`, default off). A router agent gets a `delegate_to_agent` tool (`TurDelegateAgentToolCallback` + `TurAgentHandoffService`, registered in `TurChatToolResolver` like the skill/client tools and kept past the BM25 pre-filter): the model calls it with a specialist name + a self-contained task, and the specialist runs one-shot via the existing `TurCustomToolAgentHelper` (same cross-agent `agent.invoke` path — so it inherits the shared `parentDepth+1` recursion cap, bounding A→B→A loops). The allowlist resolves by id or case-insensitive title, skips self/disabled/missing specialists, and errors return as text (the router recovers). The handoff is **visible for free**: `delegate_to_agent` is a normal decorated tool, so the T436 tool-call events fire and the headless `TuringToolActivity` (T437) renders the pass-off — the Turing analog of AG-UI subgraphs. Covered by `TurAgentHandoffServiceTest` + `TurDelegateAgentToolCallbackTest`. (§XXIII.7)
- **T447** — **Self-tuning agent (eval as continuous improvement)** — opt-in per-agent (`selfTuningEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.59`, default off). A nightly cluster-once job (`TurSelfTuningJob`, `@Scheduled` + `@SchedulerLock`) runs `TurSelfTuningService.runCycle` per opted-in agent: **mine** recent failed conversations (`TurSelfTuningMiner` over the chat-analytics store — ABANDONED/ERROR/HANDOFF outcome, NEGATIVE/FRUSTRATED sentiment, or tool errors; fail-open on NoOp store) → **draft** a revised system prompt (`TurSelfTuningDrafter`, one LLM call) → **score** current vs proposed against the agent's Block K golden sets and open a PR-style suggestion ONLY if the proposal wins by ≥0.01. Scoring reuses a new **non-persisting** `TurAgentEvalRunnerService.scoreWithSystemPrompt(agentId, override)` that detaches the agent before an in-memory prompt swap, so a trial never writes a report or flips the green baseline. New `TurAgentSuggestion` entity (tenant-scoped, status PENDING/APPROVED/REJECTED) + repo + `TurAgentSuggestionAPI` (list / approve = apply the prompt to the agent / reject / delete) — never auto-applied. Frontend admin data layer: `agent-suggestion` model + service + React Query hooks (`useAgentSuggestions`/approve/reject/delete). The highest leverage-to-cost bet, almost pure composition. Covered by `TurSelfTuningServiceTest`. (§XXIII.6)
- **T446** — **Cross-conversation personal memory** — opt-in per-agent (`userMemoryEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.58`, default off). New `TurUserMemory` entity (tenant-scoped via `@TenantId`, keyed `userId+agentId+memoryKey`, upsert-on-key) + repository + `TurUserMemoryService` (remember/list/delete/clear/recall) + a visitor-facing `/sn/{site}/user-memory` REST controller (GET list, POST upsert, DELETE one, DELETE clear; agent resolved from the site so the host only supplies userId). Persistence survives across sessions, unlike the per-conversation T163 memory. Exposed to the agent as two built-in client tools (`recall_user_memory` / `remember_fact`, via the `TurBuiltInClientToolProvider` seam) + injected `prompts/user-memory.md` — the host SDK holds the stable `userId` and answers both tools through the REST endpoints, so the server never threads userId through the chat path. Frontend: a React SDK **`useTuringUserMemory({userId})`** hook wiring both handlers and exposing `{ memories, refresh, remove, clear }` for a "what the assistant remembers about me" panel (GDPR delete as a feature). Covered by `TurUserMemoryServiceTest` + `TurClientToolServiceTest`. (§XXIII.5)
- **T445** — **Ambient / proactive copilot** — opt-in per-agent (`proactiveEnabled` + `proactiveThresholdSignals`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.57`, default off / 3). The host writes ambient interaction signals as ordinary slots named `signal.<kind>` (an interaction count, e.g. `signal.return_policy="5"` from clicks/dwell via `useTuringClickTracking`); a new SSE endpoint `GET /sn/{site}/chat/proactive/stream` subscribes to the conversation's slot bus (T63) and runs each post-write map through `TurProactiveCopilotService` — a pure, unit-tested evaluator that emits ONE offer when a fresh signal crosses the agent's threshold, suppresses repeats per signal kind, and rate-limits the conversation to one offer per 30s window. The stream is empty unless the agent opted in (gated at subscribe). Frontend: a React SDK **`useTuringProactiveCopilot()`** hook subscribing to the stream, returning `{ suggestion, dismiss }` (`suggestion.suggestedPrompt` to send on accept). "A proactive agent that interrupts constantly is worse than none" — hence opt-in + threshold + throttle. Covered by `TurProactiveCopilotServiceTest`. (§XXIII.4)
- **T444** — **Conversational glass-box ranking** — a new `explain_ranking` agent tool (`TurRankingExplainToolService`) lets the model answer "why is this result #1?" with the REAL signals instead of a hand-wave. It re-runs the same grounded retrieval the catalog copilot uses (`TurCatalogCopilotService.retrieve`, which attaches the T389 objective-ranking breakdown), finds the asked-about document (by id, or the top result when omitted) and narrates its keyword (BM25) rank, semantic (vector) rank, the T383 HYBRID_RRF fused rank + score, the final position, and the Block N reranker's up/down delta. Signals are objective-only (ranks + RRF score + rerank delta — never commercial weight), and a legacy lexical-only site is reported as such. Registered (unflagged, like `catalog_search`) in the semantic-navigation chat (`TurLLMSemanticChatAPI`), description in `prompts/tools/ranking/explain_ranking.md`. A trust feature for regulated/enterprise procurement. Covered by `TurRankingExplainToolServiceTest`. (§XXIII.3)
- **T443** — **Co-browse the search UI** — opt-in per-agent (`coBrowseEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.56`, default off). Built-in co-browse client tools (`set_search_query` / `toggle_facet` / `clear_facets` / `set_sort` / `set_page`) are folded into the agent's advertised set via a new generalized `TurBuiltInClientToolProvider` seam (`TurAnswerAsAppClientTools` + `TurCoBrowseClientTools` both implement it; `TurClientToolService` iterates all providers, each gated by its own flag, de-duped by name) and the system prompt gains `prompts/co-browse.md`. Frontend: a React SDK **`useCoBrowseSearch(store)`** hook that maps each tool to the real `useTuringUrlSearch` search store — query/sort/page via `updateParams`/`setSort`, facets by matching group-name + visible value label to the server-supplied toggle/clear `link` (same path as `useTuringFacets`, so the backend's filter-query encoding stays authoritative). The chat becomes a remote control for the page the user is already looking at, not a parallel box. Covered by `TurClientToolServiceTest` (compose-independently + co-browse cases). (§XXIII.2)
- **T442** — **Answer-as-an-app (search-grounded generative UI)** — opt-in per-agent (`answerAsAppEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.55`, default off). When on, three built-in generative-UI client tools (`comparison_table` / `spec_card` / `configurator`) are folded into the agent's advertised client-tool set by `TurClientToolService` (independent of `clientToolsEnabled`; custom declarations of the same name win) — so they park + resume through the very same T438 protocol — and the system prompt gains `prompts/answer-as-app.md` (search-first, manifest-type-driven workflow: CURRENCY→slider/currency, DATE→date, faceted→select, …). New `TurAnswerAsAppClientTools` carries the declarations + JSON schemas. Frontend: headless **`TuringComparisonTable` / `TuringSpecCard` / `TuringConfigurator`** in `@viglet/turing-react-ui` (type-aware `formatTypedValue`, zero-dep, `respond()` on row-select/action/submit) + `ANSWER_AS_APP_COMPONENTS` registry, and a React SDK **`useAnswerAsApp()`** hook pre-wiring them onto `useGenerativeUI` (T440). The agent renders a live mini-app from a real typed SN search instead of prose — impossible in a pure-agent framework with no typed index. Covered by `TurClientToolServiceTest` + `TuringAnswerAsApp.test.tsx`. (§XXIII.1)

## Block Y — Reference showcase app ("Atlas Store", §XXIV)

> **Block complete (2026-06-29).** All seven tasks shipped (T451–T457) — the
> `frontend/apps/showcase` "Atlas Store" reference app exercising the maximum
> platform surface end to end (typed catalog, faceted + hybrid search, RAG chat,
> agent power, creative bets, voice/memory/ops, MCP), an in-app guided tour, the
> `TurShowcaseSmokeIT` e2e contract test, a `docs-turing` page, and the §XXIV.1
> coverage audit (`COVERAGE.md`). The smoke test uncovered + fixed a T448
> handoff-service circular dependency that blocked full app-context startup.

- **T457** — **Guided tour, tests & docs (+ coverage audit)** — an in-app `GuidedTour` walkthrough narrating each capability (re-openable, with "try it" deep-links into the copilot); the **e2e smoke test** `TurShowcaseSmokeIT` (SDK-contract pattern: boots the backend on a random port, imports the showcase ZIP into an embedded Lucene index, asserts the 200-product wildcard count, typed default fields, category/brand facets, keyword search, sort options and `chat/enabled → NO_GENAI`) — which **surfaced and fixed** a circular-reference regression (`TurAgentChatExecutor → TurChatToolResolver → TurAgentHandoffService → TurAgentChatExecutor`, T448) that made the full Spring context fail to start, by marking the handoff service's executor injection `@Lazy`; a `docs-turing/showcase.md` page + sidebar entry; and `COVERAGE.md` reconciling the app against the §XXIV.1 matrix (✅ live / 🔌 backend-gated / 📄 documented, gaps listed). `tsc -b` + `vite build` + the IT (7/7) green. (§XXIV.2)
- **T456** — **Voice, memory & ops tour** — **Voice agent** (T147): a mic button in the chat composer (`useTuringVoice`, Web Speech STT) dictates the question and auto-sends on a final utterance. **Search by image**: a hero affordance hands off to the copilot's multimodal upload (vision → "find similar products"). **Cross-conversation memory** (T446): a "brain" toggle opens a panel of what Atlas remembers about the visitor (`useTuringUserMemory`, keyed on a stable localStorage visitor id), with `remember_fact`/`recall_user_memory` client tools merged into the chat and a "Forget me" GDPR delete. **Ops tour** (`/ops`): a guided map of chat analytics (sentiment trajectory T87, tool-latency p95 T88, SSE channels T90), agent eval + golden sets (Block K), self-tuning suggestions (T447), and cost governance (Block L) — admin endpoints documented (not faked) per surface. **MCP exposure** (Block I): `mcp/claude-desktop.json` bridges Claude Desktop to Turing's MCP server (`/mcp`, Streamable HTTP) via `mcp-remote`, so the Atlas catalog is drivable from Claude Desktop. `tsc -b` + `vite build` green. (§XXIV.2)
- **T455** — **Creative-bets wiring** — the thin compositions that only pay off because Turing owns search **and** agents. **Co-browse** (T443): `useCoBrowseSearch(store)` folds `set_search_query`/`toggle_facet`/`clear_facets`/`set_sort`/`set_page` into the chat's client tools, so the agent drives the real storefront the shopper sees. **Glass-box "Why #1?"** (T444): a results button asks the agent to explain the top result's ranking via the `explain_ranking` tool (BM25/vector/RRF/rerank signals). **Ambient proactive copilot** (T445): a `ProactiveToast` driven by `useTuringProactiveCopilot` offers a prompt when an interaction signal crosses the agent threshold. **Agent→agent handoff** (T448): the `delegate_to_agent` call shows up for free in the inline tool-call activity (T436). **Embeddable action widget** (T450): a new `/embed-demo` route is a **mock third-party site** embedding the Atlas agent via the zero-dep vanilla `@viglet/turing-sdk` `createHostActions({ onAddToCart })` — the agent adds to *that site's* cart, Turing as an action layer over any website. A small `ask-atlas` pub/sub bridge lets the Why-#1 button and the proactive toast open the copilot with a preset prompt. `tsc -b` + `vite build` green. (§XXIV.2)
- **T454** — **Agent power** — lights up the Block W + skills agent surface in the copilot. **Client tools** (T438/T439): `add_to_cart`/`get_cart`/`clear_cart` (`src/lib/cart-tools.ts`) run against a real `CartProvider` context (localStorage-persisted), shared by the header cart drawer and the product-page "Add to cart" — the agent acts on the shopper's live basket. **Code interpreter** (T80): exposing `get_cart` lets the agent run Python to "compute my cart total / chart price history" over real data. **Live tool-call activity** (T436): `TuringToolActivity` renders each `tool_call` SSE event inline (`message.toolCalls`). **Answer-as-an-app** (T442): `useAnswerAsApp` + `TuringGenerativeContent` render the built-in `comparison_table`/`spec_card`/`configurator` widgets, parked/resumed over the client-tool protocol. **Bundled returns/RMA skill** (T323): `skills/returns-rma/` is a self-contained Anthropic skill folder (`SKILL.md` + `references/return-policy.md` + a pure-stdlib `scripts/rma.py` that computes eligibility from purchase date/category and mints an RMA id) to drop into the backend `skillsPath`. All agent-side features are gated server-side (`clientToolsEnabled`/`answerAsAppEnabled`/`toolCallEventsEnabled`/`skillsEnabled`); the client wiring is always present. `tsc -b` + `vite build` green. (§XXIV.2)
- **T453** — **Chat surface** — a floating "Ask Atlas" slide-over copilot exercises the RAG chat half end to end via `useTuringChat`: token **streaming** with a thinking indicator, **RAG sources** as `TuringSourceChips` (T152/T292), **option chips** for the next turn, **native multi-field forms** (T107 — `activeForm` → `submitForm`, with text/email/tel/number/date/textarea/select widgets in a dedicated `NativeForm`), a **slots "Collected" strip** (T63, `useTuringSlots`), **multimodal image upload** with vision (T401, `useTuringSlotUpload` paperclip), a **workspace panel** of agent artifacts (T113, `useTuringWorkspace` → `TuringWorkspacePanelView`), and **rich content** dispatch — markdown / sandboxed `html` / `d2` diagrams via `TuringRichContent` (T295–T298), the `@terrastruct/d2` engine **lazy-imported / code-split** (the 8 MB wasm chunk is only fetched when an answer contains a diagram; main bundle stays ~253 KB gzip). Honest "chat lights up when an AI agent + LLM is configured" notice on the offline Lucene seed (search-only). `tsc -b` + `vite build` green. (§XXIV.2)
- **T452** — **Search surface** — the Atlas Store storefront lights up the SDK "search half" end to end: faceted + URL-synced search (`TuringProvider` urlSync + `useTuringFacets`), autocomplete dropdown, a spell-check "did you mean?" banner (`useTuringSpellCheck`), **category tabs** (`useTuringTabs` on `category`, with the category facet hidden from the sidebar so they don't conflict — T247), a **sort dropdown** wired to the site's 5 custom sorts (`useTuringSortOptions` → `store.setSort`: price ↑/↓, top-rated, most-reviewed, newest), a **"you may also like"** similar rail on the product page (`useTuringSimilar`, T400 vector/MLT), and click-tracking/CTR on every result open (`useTuringClickTracking`). HYBRID_RRF (T383) + the pluggable reranker (Block N) are documented as a **server site-config toggle** (`TurSNSiteGenAi.snRankingMode` = `HYBRID_RRF`/`HYBRID_RRF_RERANK`) — off in the offline Lucene seed (no embeddings), activated when imported into a backend with an embedding LLM; the UI is rank-mode agnostic. Refactored a shared `Stars` component out of the card/detail duplication. `tsc -b` + `vite build` green. (§XXIV.2)
- **T451** — **Scaffold + seed** — new `frontend/apps/showcase` Turborepo app (`turing-showcase`), the "Atlas Store" commerce reference that stresses the maximum platform surface. A deterministic catalog generator (`gen-catalog.mjs`, seeded LCG → byte-stable) synthesizes a **200-product typed catalog** across 5 categories: price/list_price as `CURRENCY` (`amount,ISO4217`) with `price_amount`/`list_price_amount` `FLOAT` mirrors for cross-engine sort/range, `rating` `FLOAT`, `review_count` `INT`, `on_sale` `BOOL`, `release_date` `DATE`, and multi-valued `color`/`material`/`tags` `STRING` — exactly the surface the manifest + hybrid ranking + facets exercise. Hand-authored `export/export.json` provisions the SN site (typed fields + 9 facets incl. year-range + multi-valued OR, 5 custom sorts, a spotlight, spell-check, MLT, highlighting). The **import-bundle path** is complete: `gen-manifest.mjs` + a self-contained `zip-dist.mjs` produce `atlas-store.zip` (export.json + content + compiled SPA under `app/`) for marketplace/Pages import + the `turing init`/`dev` clone-to-running flow. Skinned with **`@viglet/turing-react-ui`** token theming (`src/lib/brand.ts`, T305) on a distinct Atlas palette (indigo + amber-sale accent). Working anchor: a faceted storefront (`@viglet/turing-react-sdk` search hooks + click tracking) + product detail page. Registered in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` + `dev:showcase`/`build:showcase` root scripts; `tsc -b` + `vite build` green. (§XXIV.2)

## Block Z — Client-side conversion analytics & the Google Analytics bridge (§XXV)

- **T458** — **SDK canonical analytics event bus** — zero-dep `createTuringAnalytics({ sinks, context })` + a fixed vendor-neutral event taxonomy (`turing_chat_*` / `turing_ab_variant_assigned` / `turing_search*`). `createChatController` gains an `analytics` option and emits `turing_chat_start` (once) + `turing_chat_message_sent` (per turn), keeping the bus context (`conversationId`/`sessionId`) in sync. No-op when absent → existing embeds byte-for-byte unchanged. Covered by `analytics.test.ts`.
- **T459** — **Pluggable sinks + GA4/GTM adapter** — `googleAnalyticsSink({ measurementId?, transport? })` maps canonical events to GA4 `gtag('event', …)` AND a GTM `dataLayer.push`, **auto-detecting** `window.gtag`/`window.dataLayer` (EDS zero-config); plus `onEventSink(fn)` (Segment/Matomo/Plausible) and `debugSink()`. Identity fields snake_cased to GA convention, reserved `session_id` namespaced to `turing_session_id`. Covered by `analytics-sinks.test.ts`.
- **T460** — **Client-side abandonment & engagement detection** — `createAbandonmentWatcher` (visibilitychange→hidden / pagehide / idle timer); the chat controller fires a single `turing_chat_abandoned` (with `turns`/`last_step`/`elapsed_ms`) for a started, unconverted conversation — the drop-off signal the server only infers via TTL. GA4 sink flags the event `transport_type: 'beacon'` so it survives unload. Default on with `analytics`, tunable/`false`. Covered by `analytics-abandonment.test.ts`.
- **T461** — **Conversion, funnel-step & A/B attribution** — backend exposes `personaId` on `GET /chat/state` (the `__activePersonaId` flow var). The chat controller emits `turing_chat_step` (cursor advance), `turing_ab_variant_assigned` once (then stamps `experiment_key`/`variant_label`/`persona_id` on **every** event), and `turing_chat_lead_captured` on a configurable goal slot write or a native form (T107) submit — so GA4 answers "which persona variation converted" natively. Covered by `analytics-conversion.test.ts`.
- **T462** — **Search / semantic-navigation conversion events** — `createSearchController` emits `turing_search` (+ total hit count), `turing_search_no_results` (content-gap signal, feeds T252), `turing_search_refined`, and `trackResultClick()` (one call → server CTR `postClick` + `turing_search_result_click`). Cross-surface `search → chat → lead` stitching via the shared `TUR_SESSION` cookie. Covered by `analytics-search.test.ts`.
- **T463** — **React hook + react-ui passthrough + EDS wiring + docs** — `useTuringAnalytics()` in `@viglet/turing-react-sdk` (memoized bus + GA4 sink auto-attached, `site` seeded from context); `useTuringChat` (lifecycle + abandonment + form-lead) and `useTuringSearch` (full search events + `trackResultClick`) gain the `analytics` option; `TuringResultList` gains an `onResultClick` analytics passthrough (zero-dep). New `turing-analytics` EDS example block (GA4 auto-detect) + SDK README "Analytics" section.

## Block AA — Synthetic personas: audience-fit content evaluation & persona synthesis from audio (§XXVI)

- **T464** — **Audience-profile facet on `TurPersona`** — opt-in `personaKind` discriminator (`SPEAKER` | `AUDIENCE` | `BOTH`, default `SPEAKER`) + a cohesive `TurPersonaAudience` `@Embeddable` (`readingLevel` band → FK-grade ceiling, `domainExpertise`, `vocabularyCeiling`, comprehension + accessibility notes, `primaryLanguage`). Default `SPEAKER` keeps every existing voice persona, agent attachment, and prompt-composition path byte-for-byte unchanged. `TurAgentPersonaResolver` now guards that an `AUDIENCE`-only persona never resolves as an agent's voice (`isUsableAsSpeaker()`/`isUsableAsAudience()`). Idempotent Liquibase `v2026.3.1.62` (snake_case columns matching `ai_persona`). Admin persona form gains a "Persona kind" selector + an "Audience" section shown only for `AUDIENCE`/`BOTH` (en/pt). (§XXVI.1)
- **T465** — **Persona "notebook" source set** — new `TurPersonaSource` child of `TurPersona` (`type` SN_DOC \| ASSET \| URL, `ref`, optional `siteName`, cached extracted text + extraction status). Three already-present ingestion paths: SN_DOC text via a new `TurPersonaSnDocResolver` (per-locale `id:"…"` query through `TurSNSearchProcess`, field-flattened), ASSET via `TurFileUtils.documentToText`, URL via `urlContentToText` behind the existing SSRF guard (`isAllowedRemoteUrlString`). Extraction is fail-soft (`PENDING`/`EXTRACTED`/`FAILED` + error, retryable). Nested CRUD `/api/persona/{id}/source` (+ `/upload` multipart, `/{id}/extract`) mirroring `TurAIAgentSlotAPI`; cached `findAll`/`findById` with eviction. Idempotent Liquibase `v2026.3.1.63` (`ai_persona_source`, FK cascade-delete). Admin persona form gains a self-contained "Evaluation notebook" section (add URL / SN doc / file upload, red/amber/green status, text preview, re-extract, delete) shown for `AUDIENCE`/`BOTH` personas (en/pt). (§XXVI.2)
- **T466** — **Pure multilingual readability/complexity scorer** — `TurReadabilityScorer` (+ `TurReadabilityMetrics`/`TurReadabilityResult` records): deterministic, no-LLM, no-IO (the T385 pure-scorer discipline). Computes language-adapted Flesch Reading Ease / Flesch–Kincaid grade (EN classic, PT Martins, ES Fernández-Huerta), avg sentence length + syllables/word (vowel-group counter with accents + EN silent-e), a complex-word ratio (3+ syllables, minus the persona's `vocabularyCeiling` allow-list), and a per-language passive-voice heuristic — fused into a 0–100 fit score against the reader's `TurPersonaReadingLevel` grade ceiling via a smooth asymptotic penalty (strictly monotonic in the grade gap, so two hard texts still rank by reader level). 6 deterministic unit tests. (§XXVI.3)
- **T467** — **Content-fit evaluator** — `TurPersonaContentFitEvaluator` (+ `TurContentFitResult`/`TurContentFitMisfit` records): the LLM role-plays the persona *as a reader* and, grounded strictly in the source text, returns a structured verdict (fit %, condiz `fits[]`, não-condiz `misfits[{span,reason,suggestion}]`). Delegates the LLM call + default-LLM resolution + cache (keyed `persona-fit:{personaId}:{sourceId}:{hash}`) + token accounting to `TurLlmSummaryService`; the JSON contract is baked into the system prompt (portable provider-neutral recipe, lenient brace-extract parse). Grounding hard-fail (à la T385) drops any flagged span not present verbatim in the source. Final score **fuses** the deterministic T466 readability sub-score (0.5) with the LLM's grounded fit (0.5); degrades gracefully to readability-only when the LLM is unavailable or unparseable. 5 unit tests (LLM mocked). (§XXVI.4)
- **T468** — **Persona-fit report API + admin page** — `POST /api/persona/{id}/content-fit` (`TurPersonaContentFitAPI`): evaluates one source (`?sourceId`) or the whole notebook and returns an aggregated `TurPersonaFitReport` (notebook-wide average over sources with extracted text + per-source verdicts + `llmAvailable`/`canRegenerate`). Admin persona form gains an "Audience-fit report" section (shown for `AUDIENCE`/`BOTH`): an "Evaluate" / "Regenerate" action rendering an overall + per-source red/amber/green fit bar (à la T388, green ≥70 / amber ≥40 / red <40), the condiz bullets, and the não-condiz flagged spans with reason chips + rewrite suggestions; degrades to a readability-only note when no default LLM is set. New persona-fit model + service; en/pt i18n. (§XXVI.5)
- **T469** — **Transcription seam `TurTranscriptionProvider`** — pluggable speech-to-text interface (+ `TurTranscriptionResult`), the one genuinely new piece of infra (Spring AI 2.0.0-M2 ships no transcription module). Default `TurOpenAiTranscriptionProvider` does a provider-neutral multipart `RestClient` POST to the configured default `TurLLMInstance`'s OpenAI-compatible `{baseUrl}/audio/transcriptions` (model `whisper-1`, MIME→extension mapping, fail-soft result); air-gapped installs override the bean with a local STT (`@Primary`). Partially unblocks F.5 (voice agents). Live AUDIO-slot auto-transcribe wiring deferred to avoid touching the T64 hot path. (§XXVI.6)
- **T470** — **Persona-from-audio authoring** — `POST /api/persona/derive-from-audio` (`TurPersonaAudioAPI` multipart): transcribe (T469) → `TurPersonaAudioDeriveService` runs an LLM behavioral analysis (JSON contract via `TurLlmSummaryService`) extracting BOTH voice traits (tone/verbosity/languageStyle/vocabulary) AND audience descriptors (reading level/expertise/language) into a **draft** `TurPersonaDto` — never auto-saved (`personaKind=BOTH` default, safe enum parsing, id left null). Frontend: a "Derive from audio" FAB on the persona list uploads the recording and hands the draft to the new-persona form via router state for human review + save (the "derive, never auto-apply" discipline of T387). 3 unit tests (transcription + LLM mocked). (§XXVI.7)
- **T471** — **Auto-suggest the best persona for content** — `TurPersonaSuggestionService` + `POST /api/persona/suggest` (`TurPersonaSuggestAPI`): batches the T467 content-fit evaluator across the audience personas and returns them ranked best-fit first (`TurPersonaSuggestionReport` with `bestPersonaId`/`bestScore` head fields + per-persona `TurPersonaSuggestion{rank,fitScore,result}`). Candidates are the `AUDIENCE`/`BOTH` personas (speaker-only skipped — nothing to grade against); default = every enabled audience persona, or an explicit `personaIds` subset (which may pin disabled ones). Ties broken by name for a stable order; degrades with the evaluator to readability-only when no LLM is set. Gives editorial routing + Block Z A/B selection (T69) a content-side companion: "which persona is this content even *for*". Frontend model + service client. 4 unit tests (evaluator mocked). (§XXVI.8)
- **T472** — **Content-fit as an index-time SN signal** — `TurSNContentFitIndexer` scores each document's readability against a per-site target-audience persona **at index time** (deterministic `TurReadabilityScorer` only — no per-doc LLM call, no credits) and stores the 0–100 result as the `content_fit_score` SN field, provisioned on demand via `TurSNFieldProvisioner`. Opt-in per site on `TurSNSiteGenAi` (`contentFitIndexingEnabled` + `contentFitPersonaId`; default off ⇒ indexing path byte-for-byte unchanged). New `getDocumentCountWithFieldBelow` SE primitive (Solr `[* TO n}` / ES `range lt`; Lucene/default −1 = "unknown") powers `TurSNContentFitCoverageService` → `GET /api/sn/{id}/content-fit` (`TurSNContentFitReport`), which buckets indexed docs into too-complex/borderline/good and reports "% too complex for its audience". Surfaced as a red/amber/green card on the T388 Field Coverage page; configured from a new section on the GenAI settings form. Blank-text docs left unscored (T381 grounding). 9 unit tests. (§XXVI.9)

## Block AB — Public site & top-of-funnel conversion (`turing.viglet.org`, §XXVII)

- **T473** — **Marketing site app `frontend/apps/site`** — standalone React 19 + Vite + Tailwind v4 + shadcn site (no SDK/backend dep), modeled on the marketplace apps; content centralized in `src/lib/site-content.ts`, sections in `src/components/sections/`. Registered in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` + `dev:site`/`build:site` scripts; `publish-github-pages.yml` builds it and deploys `dist/*` to the Pages root (Storybook `/react-sdk/` + marketplace `/marketplace/` layered on top). Migrated `robots.txt`/`sitemap.xml`/`llms.txt`/favicons into `public/`; **removed** the superseded static `.github/pages/`.
- **T474** — **Repositioning + structure** — analogy hero ("the open-source, self-hosted alternative to Algolia — with AI agents built in", §XIV.2), four outcome pillars **Search it → Ask it → Automate it → Run it** (replacing a flat feature grid), a defensible "Why Turing" comparison table (vs hosted SaaS vs DIY, dated + caveated), and an in-page **interactive search demo** (client-side sample corpus, Meilisearch movie-demo pattern; goes live via T477/T478).
- **T475** — **Near-free trust & quickstart wins** — live GitHub-stars badge (`api.github.com`, graceful fallback), copy-paste quickstart (`npx @viglet/turing-cli init`), and public **Roadmap/Changelog** links to the canonical GitHub-rendered markdown (not duplicated into the site).
- **T476** — **CLI showcase + README** — dedicated `turing-cli` site section (terminal walk-through `init`/`dev`/`deploy`/`eval`/`logs`) + ecosystem card, and the published `@viglet/turing-cli` README (install, project layout, command reference, eval YAML, config/auth) written from the real `src/commands/*` + `scaffold.ts`.
- **T523** — **Per-feature deep-dive pages (Phase 1)** — the four outcome pillars now each have a real page at `/features/{search,ask,automate,run}` (`FeaturePage` + a `FEATURE_DETAILS` content model in `site-content.ts`: lede, capability sections, a code snippet, docs/demo CTAs, cross-links). Pillar cards + the native-tools teaser link out; the full native-provider-tools grid moved onto the Automate page. Statically **prerendered** via a custom `react-dom/server` step (`scripts/prerender.mjs` + `src/entry-server.tsx`; a trivial `path`-based `App` router and `hydrateRoot`) instead of an SSG framework — `vite-react-ssg` peers cap at Vite 7 and require react-router 6, so the site (Vite 8) would have forced `--legacy-peer-deps`. The build now runs `vite build` → `vite build --ssr` → prerender, emitting real `/features/<slug>/index.html` with per-page `<title>`/description/canonical/OG (patched from the client template) + sitemap entries; an inline anti-FOUC theme script and an SSR-safe `ThemeProvider` keep hydration clean. deps: T474. (§XXVII.3)

## Block AC — JPA caching strategy: decouple cache from entities

- **T486** — **Strip repository-level `@Cacheable`/`@CacheEvict` across the board** — removed every cache annotation (and the dead per-finder cache-name constants) from all ~40 repositories so the environment runs cache-free at the repository layer, killing the cached-entity ↔ lazy-proxy `LazyInitializationException` class. Pruned the now-dead controller-level `@CacheEvict` that only evicted those repo caches (ranking-expression, facet ordering, spotlight). Flipped `TurCacheAnnotationConventionsTest` from "every `@Cacheable` must be evicted" to **"no cache annotations on repositories"**. Service-level DTO/read-model caches (`turRagRerankScore`, `turLlmPrice`, persona/flow static prompts, `spotlight_term`, search-API & snapshot) are untouched — they cache immutable values, not entities.
- **T487** — **Dedicated cached read-model layer for the hot path (chat + semantic navigation)** — added two service-level caches over immutable, `Serializable` read-models (never JPA entities): `TurChatFlowGraphCache` memoizes the parsed `ChatFlowGraph` by flow id (collapsing the ~dozens of per-turn re-parses + lazy-proxy hydrations to one), evicted via the existing `evictFlowDerivedCaches()` flow-write callback; and `TurSNSiteConfigCache` memoizes the SN site + enabled-field config (`TurSNSiteFieldConfigModel`) the `dsl_get_mappings` tool reads, evicted via the existing `TurSNSiteSnapshotEvictionListener`. Made the `ChatFlowGraph` record tree `Serializable` for clustered Hazelcast `IMap`s. Both follow the proxy-routed separate-bean + `Supplier` pattern (`TurRagRerankCache`).
- **T488** — **Admin/CRUD = uncached + transactional graph loads** — retired the `TurSNSiteRepository.findByIdNoCache` band-aid (now redundant since T486 made `findById` uncached): all 11 callers switched to plain `findById`, and the load-mutate-map / load-prompt paths now read inside a transaction. Added a `findByIdWithGenAi` `LEFT JOIN FETCH` finder (the project convention — **not** `@EntityGraph`, which logs warnings) that fetches the site's to-one GenAI graph (`turSEInstance`, `turSNSiteGenAi` → `turAIAgent` + `ragSeInstance`) in one query for the DTO/insights paths (`turSNSiteGet`, `turSNSiteUpdate`, `TurSNSiteSummaryAPI`, manifest converge, nightly batch). Made the previously non-transactional read endpoints transactional (`@Transactional(readOnly=true)` on summary/content-fit/field-coverage) and wrapped the nightly batch's per-site prompt build in a read-only `TransactionTemplate`, so lazy associations resolve through an open session now that `hibernate.enable_lazy_load_no_trans` is off. Standing policy (already linted by `TurCacheAnnotationConventionsTest`): cache lives in the hot-path read-model service only; repositories and admin are uncached; never cache an entity with a lazy association.

## Block AD — Provider coverage + cross-vendor capability exploitation (§XXVIII)

- **T522** — **Multi-provider "second opinion" answer cross-check** — for high-stakes answers, a cheap model from a **different vendor** critiques the primary RAG answer (a cross-vendor LlmJudge) and the agreement is surfaced beside it as a confidence signal — it never alters the answer. New `genai/secondopinion/`: `TurSecondOpinion` (verdict: agree + optional confidence + rationale + critic model/vendor) and `TurSecondOpinionService`, which resolves the configured critic instance, **enforces the cross-vendor constraint** (skips when the critic's plugin type equals the answerer's — a same-family second view shares blind spots), builds its resilience-wrapped `ChatModel`, runs a strict fact-checker judge prompt over the question + retrieved context + answer, and parses an `AGREE`/`DISAGREE` verdict (+ optional `Confidence: N%`, reusing the T180 yes/no normalization idea; OpenAI critics can later supply calibrated `top_logprobs` confidence). Wired into the CALL branch of `TurChatStreamingDispatcher` beside the T516 guardrail (uses the final text + drained RAG context, passes the answering `llmInstance` for the vendor check) and emitted as a new `secondOpinion` SSE event. Opt-in via `GLOBAL_SECOND_OPINION_ENABLED` + `GLOBAL_SECOND_OPINION_LLM` (admin GET/PUT); both SDKs parse the event onto `TurChatSecondOpinion` (`onSecondOpinion` + message field), and the admin chat renders an agrees/disagrees confidence badge (en/pt). Fail-open everywhere; disabled = byte-for-byte legacy path. **Completes Block AD's cross-vendor bridges.** (§XXVIII.18)
- **T521** — **Managed reranker strategies (AWS Bedrock Rerank + Google Vertex AI Ranking)** — two additional Block N `TurRagRerankStrategy` backends for cloud-native deployments that don't want to self-host a cross-encoder, both fail-open like the rest of Block N (any error → empty → `TurRagReranker` keeps retrieval order). `TurBedrockRerankStrategy` (`BEDROCK`) calls the AWS Bedrock `Rerank` API (reusing the T520 `bedrockagentruntime` client; IAM creds; region `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_REGION`; model is the `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_MODEL` ARN or a bare id like `amazon.rerank-v1:0` expanded to the region's foundation-model ARN). `TurVertexAiRerankStrategy` (`VERTEX_AI`) calls the Vertex AI Ranking (Discovery Engine `rankingConfigs:rank`) REST endpoint with a `GoogleCredentials` bearer (service-account JSON in the encrypted rerank-key field, else Application Default Credentials), project/location from `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_VERTEX_PROJECT`/`_LOCATION`, model default `semantic-ranker-default-004`. Both reorder via the shared `TurRerankIndexMapper` (the pure response→index mapping is unit-tested). Added to `TurRagRerankStrategyType` + the strategy enum/selector; admin Global Settings gains the two options + region / project / location / service-account fields (en/pt). Selecting either is the only change — every existing strategy path is untouched. (§XXVIII.17)
- **T520** — **Managed-RAG retrieval backend seam (`TurRetrievalBackend`) + Bedrock Knowledge Bases** — an opt-in <em>alternative</em> to the built-in vector/hybrid index for tenants standardized on a cloud RAG stack, without rewriting the retrieval core. New `genai/rag/backend/`: `TurRetrievalBackend` (returns Spring AI `Document`s exactly like the built-in path, so retrieved passages flow through reranking / `sources[]` / native-citations verbatim), `TurRetrievalBackendType` (`BUILT_IN` default | `BEDROCK_KB`), `TurRetrievalRequest`, and `TurRetrievalBackendResolver` — the single seam the core consults: it returns an override <em>only</em> for an explicitly configured **and** available managed backend, else `Optional.empty()` so `TurRagSearchToolService.retrieveDocuments` runs the built-in path byte-for-byte unchanged (one early, fail-safe override at the top; a backend error degrades to built-in). `TurBedrockKnowledgeBaseBackend` implements the AWS Bedrock KB `Retrieve` API (new `software.amazon.awssdk:bedrockagentruntime` dep, pinned to the spring-ai-bom AWS SDK version; IAM creds mirror T506), mapping each `KnowledgeBaseRetrievalResult` to a `Document` carrying `source_id`/`url`/`title` — so a Bedrock-KB passage becomes a **citation source through the existing `TurCitationDocument` path** (this delivers the Bedrock half of T519's native-citation bridge — no separate decoder). Config is deploy-time infra (`turing.retrieval.*`, `TurRetrievalProperty`, like `turing.storage.*`): default `BUILT_IN` = unchanged. Vertex AI Search / RAG Engine are future adapters on the same seam. (§XXVIII.16)
- **T519** — **Native-citation RAG across vendors (Cohere `Command`)** — decodes Cohere's native citation-mode citations onto the existing T152–T155 `TurChatCitation` / `citations` SSE contract, so a Cohere-grounded answer renders through the exact same chip/popover UI as the already-wired Anthropic/Gemini native citations — no new UI. New `genai/nativeapi/cohere/`: `TurCohereCitationSupport` (pure, unit-tested — `documentsArray` encodes the retrieved `TurCitationDocument`s as the Cohere v2 `documents` array with stable `doc-N` ids; `decode` maps the response `message.citations[]` `start`/`end`/`text` + `sources[].id` back onto `TurChatCitation`, resolving `doc-N` → sourceId/title/url, `answerStart`/`answerEnd` from the span, `locationType="search_result"`) and `TurCohereMessagesService` (calls Cohere's native `POST /v2/chat` over `java.net.http` with the passages as documents, decodes, and emits the answer token + `citations` event in the same `Mono.fromCallable(runTurn)…flatMapMany(emitTurn)` shape as `TurAnthropicMessagesService`). Wired into `TurNativeChatExecutor`: a `cohere` branch taken **only** when the agent's `citations` Request Option is on (Cohere otherwise stays on the OpenAI-compatible Spring AI path, unchanged), reusing `retrievePassagesForCitations` + `TurCitationDriftService.tapForPersistence` like the Anthropic path. Opt-in + dependency-light (HTTP, no new SDK). **Bedrock Knowledge Base citations** (the other half of this bridge) ship with **T520**, where the `bedrockagentruntime` `RetrieveAndGenerate` dependency naturally lands — its KB response decodes onto this same contract. (§XXVIII.15)
- **T518** — **Cost-aware cross-provider fallback / meta-provider** — an OpenRouter-style layer that fails over to another vendor when a provider errors/rate-limits and (optionally) routes the cheapest-capable instance first. `TurFallbackChatModel` is a delegating `ChatModel` over an ordered candidate list (each already a `TurResilientChatModel`, so the meta layer only fails over once a candidate has exhausted *its own* retry/circuit-breaker pipeline); CALL fails over on a candidate exception, STREAM fails over **only before the first token** (mid-stream replay would duplicate SSE text), and it tracks `lastServedInstanceId()` so cost attributes to the vendor that actually served. `TurMetaChatModelResolver` builds the chain from a Global-Settings instance-id list (`GLOBAL_LLM_FALLBACK_CHAIN`) ordered by `TurLlmFallbackMode` — `PRIORITY` (configured order) or `CHEAPEST` (ascending `TurLLMPriceService` rates) — each candidate decrypted + resilience-wrapped via `TurLlmModelFactory`. Wired at the two single model-resolution points: the agent chat turn (`TurAgentChatExecutor`, after the T291 budget downgrade) and the resolver-based stages (`TurDefaultChatModelResolver` — rerank/judge/background/summary/catalog). Per-tenant cost attribution rides the existing `tenantContext` token-usage recording. Admin GET/PUT on `/api/system/global-settings` + a routing-mode select and per-instance chain toggles on the Global Settings LLM card (en/pt). Empty chain = byte-for-byte legacy single-model path; fail-safe everywhere (a broken chain reverts to the primary). Composes with — does not replace — the T291 monthly-budget downgrade. (§XXVIII.14)
- **T517** — **Cross-provider per-stage model "lanes"** — declaratively bind whole classes of LLM work to different vendors, monetizing the Block AD multi-provider breadth. New `system/lane/` seam: `TurModelLane` (`FAST` / `REASONING` / `CHEAP`) + `TurModelStage` (each pipeline call site — `AUTOCOMPLETE`/`SPELL`/`QUERY_REWRITE` → FAST, `ROUTER`/`RERANK`/`JUDGE` → REASONING, `BACKGROUND_SUMMARIZATION` → CHEAP — fixed-mapped to a lane in code, so the admin only configures three lanes). `TurModelLaneService.resolveInstanceId(stage)` applies the single rule **stage → lane → bound instance (present + enabled) → else default LLM**, fail-safe: a blank/missing/disabled binding silently reverts that stage to the legacy single-model path. `TurDefaultChatModelResolver` gains a lane-aware `resolve(TurModelStage)` overload (shared lookup-decrypt-build). Wired into two representative stages: the SN hybrid-ranking LLM reranker (`RERANK` → reasoning lane) and the nightly batch summarization job (`BACKGROUND_SUMMARIZATION` → cheap lane). Bindings live in Global Settings (`GLOBAL_MODEL_LANE_FAST`/`_REASONING`/`_CHEAP`, seeded blank) with admin GET/PUT on `/api/system/global-settings` + three lane selectors on the Global Settings LLM card (en/pt). Every lane blank = byte-for-byte legacy behaviour (all stages on the default LLM). (§XXVIII.13)
- **T516** — **Pluggable answer-grounding guardrail (`TurAnswerGuardrail` seam)** — validates that a streamed RAG answer is actually grounded in the retrieved context (and screens it for unsafe content / PII) at answer time — the managed complement to the next-day T155 citation-drift scan. New `genai/safety/guardrail/` seam mirroring the Block N reranker: `TurAnswerGuardrailStrategy` (type enum `NONE`/`BEDROCK`/`OPENAI_MODERATION`/`MISTRAL_MODERATION` + lenient `fromValue`), a by-type `TurAnswerGuardrailStrategyFactory` (falls back to `NONE`), and a `TurAnswerGuardrailService` facade that is the single fail-open + opt-in boundary. **Adapters:** `TurBedrockAnswerGuardrail` (the flagship — AWS Bedrock Guardrails `ApplyGuardrail` over the T506 dependency: contextual-grounding score + relevance + PII redaction in one managed call, decoded into a provider-agnostic `TurAnswerGuardrailVerdict`), `TurOpenAiModerationGuardrail` (reuses T182 `omni-moderation` via a new `moderateAlways`), `TurMistralModerationGuardrail` (GDPR-native `mistral-moderation-latest` over HTTP), and the no-op `NONE`. Wired into the CALL branch of `TurChatStreamingDispatcher` after the answer is assembled: the retrieved chunk **texts** are teed into the existing `TurRagSourceCollector` (server-side only — never on the `sources[]` event) and fed to the guardrail, which emits a new `grounding` SSE event with the verdict (and, when `block-on-violation` is set, replaces the answer with the redacted/blocked text). Both SDKs (`@viglet/turing-sdk` + `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`) parse the `grounding` event onto `TurChatGrounding` (`onGrounding` callback + message field); the admin chat renders a confidence badge beside the bubble (en/pt). Opt-in via `turing.safety.guardrail.*` (`TurGuardrailProperty`, default off → byte-for-byte legacy path); fail-open everywhere — a guardrail outage never blocks an answer. (§XXVIII.12)
- **T515** — **Mistral OCR / Document-AI extraction bridge** — augments Apache Tika on the indexer write path for scanned PDFs and image-only documents, which Tika reads poorly. New `TurMistralOcrService` over the Mistral `/v1/ocr` API (`mistral-ocr-latest`): PDFs via `document_url`, images via `image_url`, both as base64 data URIs (no upload step); the per-page `pages[].markdown` is concatenated into layout-aware text. Wired as an **augmentation** in `TurAssetTrainingService` — `augment()` only calls OCR when the file is OCR-eligible (image/PDF) AND Tika produced no usable text, so the common text-document path is untouched and spends no OCR credits. Opt-in via `turing.ocr.*` (`TurOcrProperty`: `enabled` + `mistral.apiKey`/`model`/`endpoint`/`timeoutSeconds`); disabled or keyless → inert, returns the Tika text. Fail-soft: any OCR error keeps the Tika text and never blocks indexing. The structured Markdown also feeds T387 manifest derivation downstream. (§XXVIII.11)
- **T514** — **Quantized / Matryoshka embeddings for the Lucene store** — opt-in scalar quantization on the embedded Lucene KNN store to shrink the index and speed search at a measured recall cost. New `TurLuceneQuantization` enum (`NONE`/`INT8`/`INT4`/`SEVEN_BIT`/`BINARY`) maps to a Lucene 10.4 `Lucene104HnswScalarQuantizedVectorsFormat` `ScalarEncoding`, built into a `FilterCodec` (per-field `PerFieldKnnVectorsFormat` over the `embedding` field) the writer applies on first open; the wrapper raises the format's advertised 1024-dim cap to 4096 so 1536/3072-dim models (OpenAI/Gemini) still index quantized. Selected per store via the `quantization` provider option on the store instance — `TurLuceneStoreProvider` resolves it once and threads it through every `openIndex` so the codec is consistent (one `IndexWriter` per directory). `NONE` (default) keeps `float32` — byte-for-byte legacy. Pairs with Matryoshka `outputDimensionality` truncation (already on the Voyage/Gemini embedding models) to compound the shrink. Changing the mode only affects new segments; Lucene records the codec per segment, so a re-index converts everything. (§XXVIII.10)
- **T513** — **Domain-specialized embedding model per SN site** — a per-site `embeddingModelId` override on `TurSNSiteGenAi` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.83`, nullable camelCase column) so a legal-docs site can pick `voyage-law`, a code-search site `voyage-code`, a finance site `voyage-finance` (or a Cohere domain model), while every other site stays on the platform default. `TurSNHybridRankingService` resolves the site override first (falling back to the Global Settings default) for **both** the index and query halves of the public hybrid-ranking path — they always use the same resolved model, so the per-site `sn_<siteId>` collection never mismatches within one config. Admin selector added to the SN GenAI "Hybrid Ranking" card (en/pt), populated from the embedding-model list; flows through the entity (no DTO). Null/blank = platform default → existing sites unchanged. Operational note: switching a site's model changes the vector space, so its collection must be cleared + re-indexed. (§XXVIII.9)
- **T512** — **Contextualized chunk embeddings (`voyage-context-3`)** — attacks the classic RAG failure (a chunk that's meaningless without its section) by embedding each chunk *with* its sibling chunks. New `TurContextualEmbeddingModel` capability mix-in + `TurVoyageContextualEmbeddingModel` over Voyage's `contextualizedembeddings` REST API (nested `inputs[doc][chunk]` request → `data[].data[].embedding` response; query/single-chunk calls degrade to one-doc-one-chunk with the asymmetric `query`/`document` input type; `output_dimension` Matryoshka). The `voyage` provider returns it for a `*context*` model variant or the `contextual` option (text-only stays default). `TurResilientEmbeddingModel` passes the contextual call through the resilience pipeline. `TurRagContextBuilder.reindexByMetadataContextual` embeds a document's chunks together and upserts the precomputed vectors via the store's `importChunks` seam, **fail-soft** back to per-chunk `reindexByMetadata` on a non-contextual model, a vector-count mismatch, or any error; the asset-training indexer calls it so it's wired end-to-end. Query time is unchanged. Opt-in = the embedding-model choice. (§XXVIII.8)
- **T511** — **Multimodal embeddings retrieval mode (search text → match an image)** — embeds images and text into *one* vector space so a plain-text query matches PDF page-images, diagrams and screenshots the text-only path can't. New `TurMultimodalEmbeddingModel` capability mix-in (alongside Spring AI `EmbeddingModel`) + `TurVoyageMultimodalEmbeddingModel` over Voyage's `multimodalembeddings` REST API (`voyage-multimodal-3`, base64 image parts, asymmetric `document`/`query` input types, `output_dimension` Matryoshka). The `voyage` provider returns it when the model is a `*multimodal*` variant or the `multimodal` provider option is set (text-only stays default — legacy configs byte-for-byte unchanged); `TurResilientEmbeddingModel` transparently passes the image side through the retry/CB/time-limiter pipeline so the capability survives the resilience wrap. `TurMultimodalRetrievalService` bridges the model + the embedded Lucene vector store + the indexer write path: `indexImage` embeds + upserts a precomputed vector tagged `_modality=image`; `searchImagesByText` embeds the query in the shared space and KNN-matches image vectors — both fail-soft (text-only model → no-op). REST surface `/api/v2/multimodal` (`available` / `image` upload / `search`). (§XXVIII.7)
- **T505** — **Generic OpenAI-compatible provider (`openai-compatible`)** — generalized the fixed-endpoint `gemini-openai` seam into a first-class, vendor-agnostic `TurOpenAiCompatibleLlmProvider`. The `baseUrl` is required per-instance config (from the `baseUrl` provider option or the instance URL — no default, since the URL *is* the vendor); the API key is sent as a Bearer token and is **optional** so a local vLLM/LM-Studio endpoint works with a blank key. Chat (buffered `OpenAiChatModel`), token-by-token streaming (`TurOpenAiStreamingChatModel`), and embeddings (`OpenAiEmbeddingModel`, where the endpoint exposes `/embeddings`) — one seam reaching **DeepSeek, xAI Grok, Groq, Cerebras, OpenRouter, Together, Fireworks, and local vLLM/LM-Studio**. Seeded `OPENAI_COMPAT` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.77` for existing installs), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN`, and a no-default-URL branch in the admin `applyVendorDefaults`. Capability matrix stays empty (maps to `ANY`) — no provider-native server tools assumed. (§XXVIII.1)
- **T510** — **Google Vertex AI provider (`vertex-ai`)** — the enterprise path for Gemini on GCP (`TurVertexAiLlmProvider`). The existing `gemini` provider hits the *public* `generativelanguage.googleapis.com`, which can't serve a GCP-regulated tenant; Vertex AI adds IAM, VPC-SC, CMEK, regional endpoints and provisioned throughput. It **reuses the native Gemini stack** — same `GoogleGenAiChatModel` and `TurGeminiEmbeddingModel` (so T489+ prompt-assembly and T495 asymmetric-task-type embeddings come for free) — and **differs only in transport + auth**: the `com.google.genai.Client` is built in Vertex mode (`vertexAI(true)`, `project` + `location` provider options) instead of with an API key. Auth mirrors the Bedrock pattern: a service-account JSON in the encrypted key field (or a `credentialsJson` option) → explicit `GoogleCredentials`, else Application Default Credentials (workload identity / gcloud / GCE metadata), scoped to cloud-platform. Seeded `VERTEX_AI` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.82`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN` + IAM-oriented `applyVendorDefaults`. (§XXVIII.6)
- **T509** — **Mistral AI provider (`mistral`)** — `TurMistralLlmProvider`, prioritized for the non-technical reason that a **European, GDPR-native** option matters to EU customers and Viglet's positioning. Mistral's API (`https://api.mistral.ai/v1`) is OpenAI-compatible, so chat (Mistral Large/Small, Codestral; default `mistral-large-latest`), token-by-token streaming, and embeddings (`mistral-embed`) reuse the proven OpenAI seam. Seeded `MISTRAL` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.81`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN` + `applyVendorDefaults`. **Reranker scope note:** Mistral exposes no managed rerank endpoint, so the planned `MISTRAL` reranker strategy was **not** added (it would have been a dead 404) — Mistral reranking is already available via the existing `LLM` rerank strategy pointed at a Mistral instance. (§XXVIII.5)
- **T508** — **Cohere full provider (`cohere`)** — promoted Cohere beyond the existing `COHERE` reranker strategy to a first-class chat + embeddings provider (`TurCohereLlmProvider`). Rides Cohere's **OpenAI-compatible** endpoint (`https://api.cohere.ai/compatibility/v1`), so `Command` chat (default `command-r-plus`), token-by-token streaming (`TurOpenAiStreamingChatModel`), and `Embed v4` embeddings (default `embed-v4.0`, multilingual/multimodal/Matryoshka) all reuse the proven OpenAI seam. Cohere's *native* built-in citation mode is deferred to the §XXVIII.15 cross-vendor bridge (T519). Seeded `COHERE` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.80`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN` + `applyVendorDefaults`. (§XXVIII.4)
- **T507** — **Voyage AI embeddings + `VOYAGE` reranker** — the retrieval specialist Anthropic recommends, in two halves. (1) A `voyage` embedding provider (`TurVoyageLlmProvider`, embedding-only — chat throws, mirroring the inverse Anthropic case) backed by `TurVoyageEmbeddingModel`, a custom Spring AI `EmbeddingModel` over the Voyage embeddings REST API (`voyage-3`/`voyage-3-large`) that applies the asymmetric `input_type=document|query` via the standard VectorStore calling convention, plus optional `output_dimension` Matryoshka truncation. (2) A `VOYAGE` Block N `TurRagReranker` strategy (`TurVoyageRerankStrategy`, `rerank-2.5`) reusing the shared `TurHttpRerankClient` — now generalized to take the result-count param name (Voyage uses `top_k`, not `top_n`) and to parse the `data` envelope (not just `results`). Seeded `VOYAGE` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.79`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN` + the reranker enum/selector (global settings model + page + en/pt i18n) + `applyVendorDefaults`. (§XXVIII.3)
- **T506** — **AWS Bedrock provider (`bedrock`)** — `TurBedrockLlmProvider` over Spring AI's Bedrock Converse API (`spring-ai-bedrock-converse`), the one provider authenticated by **IAM** (region + credentials chain) rather than an API key, so it acts as a gateway to Claude/Llama/Mistral/Nova/Titan on a single endpoint. Credentials resolve via `TurBedrockCredentials`: explicit static keys (`accessKeyId` provider option + the encrypted API-key field as the secret, optional `sessionToken`) or, when none are set, the AWS `DefaultCredentialsProvider` chain (env / instance profile / IAM role) — the data-residency path AWS-standardized enterprises require. Chat via `BedrockProxyChatModel` (native Converse streaming inherited); embeddings via a custom `TurBedrockEmbeddingModel` over `InvokeModel` (Titan `amazon.titan-embed-*` and Cohere `cohere.embed-*`, with asymmetric `search_document`/`search_query` input types) since the Converse module is chat-only. The model id is the Bedrock id or inference-profile ARN. Seeded `BEDROCK` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.78`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN`, IAM-oriented branch in `applyVendorDefaults`. (§XXVIII.2)

## Block AF — Domain layer: bounded completion, stop-criteria & enforcement (§XXX)

- **T540** — **Benchmarked the double-mapping cost on the hot path** — a gated (`-Dturing.bench=true`), warmed-up nanoTime harness (`TurDomainMappingBenchmark`, no JMH dep) measuring `entity → MapStruct → record` (`TurSNSiteDomainMapper.toDomain`) against direct-getter access on the exact `TurSNSite` aggregate behind the EAGER 200ms→5s incident. Result (JDK 21, warm, 5M ops): MapStruct hop ≈**48–53 ns/op** (≈40 ns/op over the getter baseline), ≈0.00005 ms — 4–5 orders of magnitude below a millisecond-scale search request, i.e. immeasurable on the hot path. The incident cost was the EAGER graph walk a by-ID record structurally cannot trigger, never the copy. **Decision fed to T541: keep ports on the hot read paths; a projection query is only warranted where the source entity is heavy to _load_ (many columns / large LOBs), not to _map_.** Numbers recorded in the benchmark Javadoc; not run in CI. (§XXX.1)
- **T541** — **ADR + stop-criteria for the domain layer** — wrote `docs/adr/0001-domain-layer-bounded-completion.md` (a new `docs/adr/` home), the first written rationale for the hexagonal pilot that previously lived only in class Javadoc + "scaffolding only" commit messages. Codifies the boundary: a `*Domain` record + port **only** for reads on a hot path (search/chat/RAG/autocomplete), a cached value, a cross-aggregate read (EAGER-cascade hazard), or a LazyInit-prone read (escapes its session) — records reference neighbours **by ID**; the JPA repository stays for admin/CRUD reads and **all** writes (`@Transactional` + `LEFT JOIN FETCH`, per T488). States the explicit **"not 100%"** decision, that **ports are read-only forever** (no `save`/`delete`), and the single criterion a *new* aggregate must meet to earn a record — partial coverage is the intended end state, not a debt. Mirrored the rule into `agents.md` ("Domain layer (ports & adapters)") and agent memory. (§XXX.2)
- **T542** — **Finished the valuable read-path slice** — audited the hot read paths (search / chat / RAG) against the T541 boundary and found the "keep" set already largely conformant: the LLM chat APIs (`TurLLMChatAPI`/`TurLLMSemanticChatAPI`) correctly split port (non-secret scalar read) vs JPA entity (decrypted `apiKey` + provider factory), `TurSpotlightService` reads the site via the port and stubs an entity only for the port-less spotlight query, `TurSNSearchProcess` operates on a snapshot-provided entity (not a per-request lazy proxy), and the genai secret/provider/collection reads legitimately need the managed entity. The one genuine gap — `TurDslToolService.loadFieldConfig`, which builds the **cached** `TurSNSiteFieldConfigModel` read-model but still sourced enabled fields as JPA `TurSNSiteFieldExt` entities — was migrated to `TurSNSiteFieldExtRepositoryPort.findBySnSiteIdAndEnabled(...)` (records), removing that JPA repo dependency from a chat-path service so a cached read-model no longer touches entities. No hot path is left half on entities where a record was warranted; remaining entity reads on those paths are intentional per the boundary, not gaps. (§XXX.3)
- **T543** — **Pruned the speculative scaffolding** — systematic pass against the T541 boundary. Deleted the orphan `TurPersonaDomain` record (a "scaffolding only" projection with **zero** consumers repo-wide — no port, no adapter, no mapper, never read) and its now-empty `domain/persona` package. The pass confirmed nothing else prunes: all **8** surviving `*RepositoryPort`s have a genuine hot/search consumer (agent/LLM/MCP/custom-tool ports feed chat; SN-site/field-ext/locale/metric ports feed search + the GraphQL/DSL search surface), so none is "admin-only ceremony", and `TurSNSiteCoreUsageDomain` — which looked port-adjacent — is live via `TurSEInstanceAPI.findCoreUsage`. The two-sources-of-truth surface is now exactly the keep set. (§XXX.4)
- **T544** — **Closed the write-path question** — rewrote the Javadoc on all **8** surviving `*RepositoryPort`s (agent / custom-tool / LLM / MCP / SN-site / field-ext / locale / metric-access) from the "read-only for the pilot / future slices will introduce `save`/`delete`" promise to the final decision: **read-only by design, permanently**. Writes stay on the JPA repository + `@Transactional` + `LEFT JOIN FETCH` (per T488) — a write gains nothing from an immutable read record and everything from a managed, dirty-checked entity. Each port now points to `docs/adr/0001-domain-layer-bounded-completion.md`. The layer no longer advertises itself as perpetually in-progress. (§XXX.5)
- **T545** — **`TurDomainLayerConventionsTest` enforcement lint** — build-failing convention test (mirrors `TurCacheAnnotationConventionsTest` / `TurLiquibaseChangelogAgnosticTest`) that keeps the domain layer inside the ADR-0001 boundary so it can't silently drift back toward 100%. Three checks: (1) every `record` under `com.viglet.turing.domain` carries **no** JPA / Hibernate / Jackson annotation (reflection over type + record components + fields + accessors); (2) the domain layer depends inward only — no `domain/` source imports a JPA repository or the `persistence.adapter` package — plus a curated set of hot-path consumers migrated to a port (`TurDslToolService`, `TurSpotlightService`, `TurSNSiteNamesAPI`) must not regress to the JPA repo they left; (3) every surviving `*RepositoryPort` has exactly one `*RepositoryAdapter` and one `*DomainMapper`. Green against the current 8 ports / 8 adapters / 8 mappers. (§XXX.6)
- **T546** — **Collapsed the one pure-ceremony port to a mapper-backed reader** — the ADR criterion is that a `*RepositoryPort` earns its interface only with a second implementation or a **mocking need**. Audit of all 8 ports found **7 are mocked in unit tests** (`TurAIAgent`/`TurLLMInstance`/`TurMcpServer`/`TurSNSite`/`TurSNSiteFieldExt`/`TurSNSiteLocale`/`TurSNSiteMetricAccess` — `@Mock` in their API/service unit tests) and stay as ports; only **`TurCustomToolRepositoryPort`** had no mock (its adapter had its own direct test), making the interface pure ceremony over a lone permanent JPA adapter. Collapsed it: deleted the port + `TurCustomToolRepositoryAdapter`, replaced by the concrete `TurCustomToolReader` (`@Component`, same mapper-backed `findById`/`findAll`/`findAllEnabled` returning immutable `TurCustomToolDomain` records — ~90% of the benefit without the interface), rewired the two consumers (`TurCustomToolAPI`, `TurCustomToolCallbackService`), renamed the adapter test → `TurCustomToolReaderTest`. `TurCustomToolDomain` record is unchanged. The layer is now **7 ports + 8 records + 1 reader**; `TurDomainLayerConventionsTest` stays green (it walks `*RepositoryPort`s, so the collapsed aggregate simply leaves the port set). No further collapse warranted — the mapping cost was already immeasurable (T540) and the remaining 7 have a live mocking need. (§XXX.7)

## Block AG — Bento UI: complete the interface migration (§XXXI)

- **T576** — **SN instance sub-sections → bento (completes the SN migration)** — `app/bento/sn/`. Migrated the deep Semantic Navigation sub-sections T557 deferred ("the rest later") into the bento shell with the full BentoHero rewrite: **locale** (multi-language, draggable list + detail), **behavior**, **facet** (draggable list + custom-facet + custom-facet-item + faceted-field), **custom-sort**, **search-rule**, **ai** (GenAI), **result-ranking**, **merge-providers**, **spotlight**, **top-terms**, and **insights**. Each reuses the existing console form/list components verbatim through a new **`baseRoute` prop** (default `ROUTES.SN_INSTANCE`; bento passes `ROUTES.BENTO_SN_INSTANCE`) so save/cancel/delete/row-edit navigation stays inside `/bento` — added to the custom-sort/search-rule/result-ranking/merge/spotlight/behavior/locale/custom-facet/faceted-field forms + the locale & facet draggable lists. The five GridList-style lists share a new `BentoSnSectionList` scaffold (BentoHero with a back-link eyebrow + "New" action over the reused `GridList`/`BlankSlate`); `top-terms` and the `custom-facet-item` editor reuse the console page via new `baseRoute`/`header` props (the `header` drops the sidebar-coupled `SubPageHeader`, mirroring T566). The SN instance config-sections panel now routes **every** section (incl. insights + top-terms) to its bento sub-route — no section links out to the console anymore. `tsc -b --noEmit` + 31 bento tests green. (§XXXI.6)

Emphasis replaces the binary `featured` flag on `BentoEntityTile` (three sizes + content density; the home/area mosaics keep the legacy `featured` boolean as a fallback). Save writes the user's protective override; admins also get **"Set as default for everyone"** (global template); customizers get **"Reset to default"**. The editable grid is **WYSIWYG** — the "New" tile stays in place (non-draggable) so edit and saved views share the exact same slots/packing. Pure `resolveBentoLayout` resolver (user entries → default), React-Query hooks (`useBentoLayout` + save/global/reset mutations) over `TurBentoLayoutService`, all 12 bento lists wired (`languageModel`/`aiAgent`/`searchEngine`/…), en+pt `bento.layout.*` i18n. 31 bento vitest green + `tsc -b --noEmit` clean. (§XXXI.11)
- **T574** — **Bento list layout persistence + cascade resolution (Enhancement)** — the persistence + resolver seam under the customizable mosaic. Today `BentoListPage` renders item `idx===0` as the 2×2 featured tile purely from array order; T574 makes **emphasis** (`SMALL|MEDIUM|LARGE`) and an explicit **displayOrder** persisted per list surface, decoupled from position. New `bento_layout` entity/repository (whole-layout JSON blob per `(tenantId, scope, ownerId, listId)`, `@TenantId`-isolated, unique index) + `TurBentoLayoutService` resolver implementing the **user override → admin global template → featured=idx0 default** cascade: a user override is protective (pins their layout) and clears via reset to re-inherit the template; an admin `GLOBAL` save (owner = `""` sentinel) is the template non-customizers follow. REST at `/api/v2/bento-layout/{listId}` — GET resolve (returns source USER/GLOBAL/DEFAULT + `canEditGlobal`), PUT user override, PUT `/global` (admin-`@Secured`), DELETE reset. Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson.*`) serialization; Liquibase `v2026.3.1.84`. 8 resolver-cascade unit tests green. T575 is the `@dnd-kit` reorder + emphasis UI on top. (§XXXI.11)

Kept the **T372 GLOBAL-is-read-only** behavior and the Solr-property catalog lock (`seInstanceReadOnly`): both drive a new `readOnly` mode threaded through the shared shell — `BentoEntityShell` (suppresses inline-edit/icon-picker/status-toggle/save-bar/delete, renders a `badge` + `notice` slot), `BentoInlineEdit` (plain text), `BentoHeroIconPicker` (static chip), and `BentoListPage` (`hideNew`). - **T573** — **Nav rail → hub-and-spoke (fixes sidebar overflow before fan-out)** — T549's rail listed migrated leaf surfaces grouped by section; with only 3 migrated it fit, but as all ~22 surfaces graduate it would overflow the fixed viewport height with **no scroll fallback** — the same "sidebar estoura a tela, não aparece tudo" problem the flat console `AppSidebar` has in icon-collapsed mode. Restructured the rail to list **Home + the three section hubs** (Generative AI · Enterprise Search · Management) and nothing else, so it stays a fixed handful of icons no matter how many surfaces migrate. Each hub is a new shared `BentoAreaPage` (`app/bento/area/`) that renders every surface of its section as a bento mosaic (migrated → in-shell, unmigrated → console via `bentoNavTarget`); leaves are reached from the hub or jumped to directly via `⌘K`. Collapsed the two divergent taxonomies into one source of truth: `bento-nav.config.ts` now carries per-item `descriptionKey`/`tone`/`span` + per-section `icon`/`descriptionKey`/`areaRoute` + a `useVisibleBentoSections` grouping helper, and the home page reads it (retiring its own drifted `SECTIONS` array — home mosaics now match the hubs exactly, each section heading links to its hub via a new `BentoSection.titleHref`). `resolveBentoParent` is now config-driven (home → hub → list → detail). Rail also got a defensive `overflow-y-auto`. New/updated vitest: `useVisibleBentoSections` + section helpers, `BentoNavRail` (4 links only, hub routes, active-state). `tsc -b --noEmit` + 24 bento tests green. (§XXXI)
- **T550** — **Bento conventions before fan-out (Phase 0 complete)** — locked the rules so ~20 Phase 1–4 screens don't drift.
