Added vitest + RTL coverage for every shared component under `components/bento/__tests__/` (16 tests): `useVisibleBentoNav` privilege gating + `bentoNavTarget`, `BentoListPage`/`BentoEntityTile` (New tile, featured-first, empty hint, status pill), `BentoCommandPalette` (filter, no-match, Enter/click navigation, console-fallback indicator), and `BentoEntityShell` (new-mode Save, dirty-reveals-Save, status pill). `tsc` + all 16 tests green. (§XXXI.4)
- **T549** — **Global Bento navigation + `⌘K` command palette** — the console has a sidebar; bento home was launcher-only, so deep areas cost too many clicks. Added a single source of truth, `components/bento/bento-nav.config.ts` (mirrors the console `AppSidebar` taxonomy — primary / generative-AI / enterprise-search / management sections, with a `bentoRoute` filled in only for migrated surfaces + a `consoleRoute` fallback + privilege gating via `useVisibleBentoNav`), consumed by two new components: **`BentoNavRail`** (a slim fixed frosted icon rail on the left, desktop-only, listing the migrated surfaces grouped by section with tooltip labels + `aria-current`; `md:pl-16` on the shell reserves its gutter) and **`BentoCommandPalette`** (a keyboard-first `⌘K` launcher built on the Radix `Dialog` primitive — **zero new deps, no `cmdk`** — with a filter box + arrow-key/Enter-navigable result list; migrated areas navigate inside the bento shell, unmigrated ones link out to the console marked with an external-link glyph so `⌘K` reaches everything during the migration). Wired into `BentoRootPage`: a global `⌘K`/`Ctrl+K` handler (ignored while typing in a field), a header search/`⌘K` trigger button (the mobile entry point where the rail is hidden), plus a new `bento.*` i18n namespace (en/pt). As each phase migrates a surface, setting its `bentoRoute` graduates it onto the rail automatically. `tsc -b --noEmit` green. (§XXXI.3)
- **T548** — **Shared Bento CRUD scaffold (the lever)** — extracted the hand-rolled ~360-line LLM detail `Shell` + duplicated list pages into three reusable components under `components/bento/`: **`BentoEntityShell`** (generic over the entity — owns the identity hero with inline-edit title/description + icon picker + optional Active/Idle pill, the immediate-save-vs-staged-create modes, the scroll-linked `--bento-fade` hero→save-bar morph rAF loop, and the delete flow; the form body is a render-prop child that keeps its own fixed `BentoSaveBar` + `BentoFormSection` fields unchanged), **`BentoListPage`** (the `bento-grid` mosaic + dashed New tile + featured-first tiling + empty-state hint, parameterized by a `renderTile`), and **`BentoEntityTile`** (the common icon-chip + status-pill + title + meta content tile). Proved by re-skinning the three existing surfaces onto it: `bento.llm.instance.page.tsx` dropped from ~360 lines to a thin config, and both `bento.llm.instance.list.page.tsx` / `bento.ai-agent.list.page.tsx` collapsed to a single `BentoListPage` call each. Each remaining Phase 1–4 screen is now a thin config + its field groups, not a bespoke page. `tsc -b --noEmit` green; behavior preserved line-for-line. (§XXXI.2)
- **T547** — **Migration architecture & cutover strategy (ADR)** — wrote `docs/adr/0002-bento-ui-migration-cutover.md`, the gate that fixes scope for the whole block. Decides the one fork that blocks everything: **Bento becomes _the_ interface** (console retired), not a permanent parallel alternative. Picks the cutover mechanism — **route swap + declarative `<Navigate>` redirects, not a runtime feature flag** (a flag would force dual-tree maintenance for the whole migration; the additive `/bento` tree already gives a safe half-migrated state since nothing is removed until Phase 5). Defines the safe rollout (Phases 1–4 add bento routes, console stays live/reachable → T569 cutover + console→bento redirects + drop Preview badge → T570 delete migrated console pages). Fixes scope as a closed **surface inventory** (~20 remaining surfaces mapped to their console `*.routes.tsx` files and phases) so Block AG has a definition of done and doesn't balloon; new features land in bento directly, never on this backlog. Mirrored into IMPROVEMENTS §XXXI + agent memory. (§XXXI.1)

## Block AH — LLM instance model picker

## Block AK — Live Preview as a per-turn prompt-diagnostics surface (§XXXIV)

- **T611** — **Node picker + simulated variables in the preview.** The preview only ever showed a flow's *entry* node with empty state — a per-*flow* view. Now `GET .../system-prompt/preview` takes optional `nodeId` + `vars` (a JSON object of collected slot values): `TurSystemPromptPreviewService` seeds the synthesized `TurChatFlowState` with the picked node id + those variables (invalid JSON → empty state), so the flow addendum's "Already collected: {…}" line and next-step hint reflect a real mid-conversation turn. Persona overrides upstream of the picked node are still captured (best-effort first-edge walk). The preview DTO exposes the flow's pickable nodes (`flowNodes`) + the rendered `selectedNodeId`; the Live Preview adds a node selector and an add/remove key-value "Simulated collected values" editor (remounted per flow). Makes it a per-*turn* inspector. en/pt i18n; preview-service tests + `tsc` green.
- **T610** — **Cross-segment redundancy & contradiction lint.** The existing conflict-check only looked <em>within</em> one fragment; this extends `TurSystemPromptValidatorService` to compare the assembled segments (persona / agent / MCP / active-flow addendum — read from the segments the preview already assembled, no re-parsing) against each other. **Redundancy** (`cross_segment_redundancy`, WARNING): a normalized rule-line repeated across ≥2 segments is flagged with the segments it duplicates across and the wasted token count (the "forbidden vocab in Persona + agent REGRAS DURAS" / "never ask CPF twice" waste, now a number). **Contradiction** (`cross_segment_contradiction`, ERROR): a directive reduced to its core (polarity markers + fillers stripped, EN/PT) that appears affirmatively in one segment and negated in another (e.g. agent "never answer general knowledge" vs a flow "always answer …") — opposite instructions on the same point. Deterministic + fast (runs on every page load); semantic contradictions beyond polarity stay the deep-LLM check's job. en/pt i18n; validator tests extended (18 green).
- **T609** — **"Inert this turn" detection.** When a chat flow governs a turn, its addendum orders the model to ignore the agent prompt's welcome / first-interaction / routing scaffolding — ~40–50% dead weight on an active-flow turn that the model still attends to. New `TurInertRegionDetector` scans the agent prompt by markdown heading section and flags the inert ones two ways: a **heuristic** (bilingual EN/PT welcome/greeting/first-interaction/menu/routing heading vocabulary, active only when a flow governs) and an **author marker** (`<!-- turing:concierge-only -->` = inert when a flow is active; `<!-- turing:flow-only -->` = inert on a no-flow turn) that takes precedence for precise attribution. Regions (label + char offsets + token cost + reason) ride on the AGENT `TurSystemPromptSegmentDto`; the Live Preview slices the segment at those offsets and renders the inert spans **dimmed** with an "inert this turn · ~N tok" tag so the dead weight is visible and quantified. Advisory/read-only — never rewrites the prompt. `TurInertRegionDetectorTest` (6) + `tsc` green.
- **T608** — **Per-segment token accounting + metrics in the preview.** The T613/T614 pipeline already emits a `chars/4` token estimate + STABLE/PER_TURN class per segment and the grand `totalTokens`/`stableTokens`/`cacheBreakpointIndex` on the preview DTO, but nothing surfaced them. The Live Preview now shows a **metrics strip** — grand total tokens, the **cacheable-prefix vs per-turn split** (what T614's STABLE-first ordering can reuse across turns), and per-segment **token count + share-of-total** in each passage's provenance label — reading the pipeline's numbers directly (no re-parsing of the assembled string). Added an **optional per-turn cost estimate**: `TurSystemPromptPreviewService` resolves the agent's first enabled LLM instance and prices the assembled system prompt's input tokens via `TurLLMPriceService` (0.0 for unpriced/local models, so the "$0 local" story stays honest); new `estimatedCostUsd` + `costModelName` on the DTO, rendered as `≈ $x/turn (model)` when priced. TS model + component + en/pt i18n updated; `tsc -b --noEmit` + backend tests green.
- **T607** — **Flow-aware persona in the preview + validator.** The preview and `TurSystemPromptValidatorService` both hardcoded `agent.getDefaultPersona()`, so a `persona`-type flow node that switches the active voice at runtime (via `__activePersonaId` → `TurAgentPersonaResolver`) was invisible — the preview showed the wrong persona and the validator missed the misconfiguration. `TurSystemPromptPreviewService` now walks the selected flow from its start node through the transparent nodes to the entry anchor (bounded, loop-guarded), records any `persona`-node override on that path, and reconciles it against the agent's catalog exactly as the resolver does (honour only an in-catalog, speaker-usable persona; otherwise silently fall back to the default). The resolved persona feeds the assembly context, and the PERSONA segment is labelled *"switched by the selected flow's persona node"* (or warns when the node points at a persona missing from the catalog). New `diagnoseFlowPersonas(agent)` walks every flow and the validator turns each unhonourable override into a `flow_persona_not_in_catalog` / `flow_persona_audience_only` WARNING (source `FLOW`, en/pt i18n). Read-only/opt-in; assembled prompt unchanged. Covered by `TurSystemPromptPreviewServiceFlowPersonaTest` + validator tests (19 green).

## Block AL — Prompt assembly as a composable, cache-friendly pipeline (§XXXV)

- **T613** — **`TurPromptContributor` SPI + single-pass assembler (single source of truth).** The turn's system message was built by string concatenation spread across `TurChatPromptAssembler` + `TurPersonaPromptComposer`, while the Live Preview *rebuilt a lookalike* separately — the root cause behind every Block AK symptom (the persona lie, the stray `===`, drift as the assembler grows). New `com.viglet.turing.genai.prompt` package: `TurPromptContributor` interface emitting `TurPromptSegment {origin, title, text, stability, tokens}`, a `TurPromptAssemblyContext`, and `TurPromptAssemblyPipeline` that runs the ordered contributor list **once** and joins the segments. Six built-in contributors (persona-head, agent-base, MCP, capability, skill, flow) each own their block at its source (the `render.md`/`answer-as-app.md`/… resources moved into `TurCapabilityPromptContributor`; the persona few-shot text is shared via a new `TurPersonaPromptComposer.fewShotBlock`). `TurChatPromptAssembler` now builds the system prompt through the pipeline by default (`turing.prompt.assembly.pipeline-enabled=true`), and `TurSystemPromptPreviewService` renders the **same** segments the runtime assembles — killing the reconstruction path. The legacy layout is reproduced **byte-for-byte** (a persona head that wraps the body with the `\n\n----\n` separator), proven by `TurPromptAssemblyPipelineTest`; `pipeline-enabled=false` restores the pre-pipeline path as an escape hatch. Per-segment token estimates + STABLE/PER_TURN class now flow to the preview DTO for free (`tokens`, `stability`, `totalTokens`, `stableTokens`). Backend `mvn test` green.
- **T614** — **Stability-classed ordering + provider cache breakpoint** ⭐. Confirmed cache-hostile ordering: the per-turn few-shot block sat *between* the stable persona and the stable agent-base/MCP/tool guidance, so a provider prefix cache could only cover up to the persona — the heavy stable prefix was re-billed every turn. Each `TurPromptSegment` now carries a `TurPromptStability`; opt-in `turing.prompt.assembly.stable-first-ordering=true` reorders every STABLE segment (persona-static, agent-base, MCP, capability, skill) contiguously before the PER_TURN tail (few-shot, flow addendum) and exposes `cacheBreakpointIndex` — the segment index at which the uncacheable tail begins — so the native cache providers (T499/T502) can set a prefix breakpoint there. Off by default (reordering changes what the model sees); the byte-identical legacy order stays the default and reports `cacheBreakpointIndex = -1`. Ordering + breakpoint covered by `TurPromptAssemblyPipelineTest`.

## Block AI — Persona as a first-class actionable surface (§XXXII)

- **T578** — **Persona chat backend** — `POST /api/v2/persona/{personaId}/chat` (SSE, JSON + multipart) lets you talk directly to a `TurPersona` to test its voice. New `TurPersonaChatAPI` drives the shared `TurAgentChatExecutor` with a **transient, never-persisted** `TurAIAgent` shell: `systemPrompt=null` (generic `DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` fallback), `nativeTools` = CSV of **all** discovered tool names (`TurNativeToolService.getAllTools()`), no MCP/custom-tool/skills/RAG/flow (fresh entity's empty `HashSet`s), and the loaded persona fused via `setDefaultPersona`. The turn is **stateless** (`conversationId=null`, `flowId=null`) so no flow-state/analytics/chat-memory rows are written. Enforces `persona.isUsableAsSpeaker()` (AUDIENCE-only → 400) and validates the LLM instance via `TurInfraTenantScope.isVisibleToTenant` (this path bypasses the agent allow-list). `@TenantId` auto-scopes the persona load. Backend `mvn compile` green.
- **T579** — **Persona chat SDK client + hook subject** — `postPersonaChat(personaId, llmInstanceId, messages, options)` mirrors `postAgentChat` (CSRF, timezone header, SSE consume, multipart) in both the React SDK (`react-sdk/core/api.ts`) and the vanilla `@viglet/turing-sdk` (`api.ts` + `createTuringApi` bound helper); only the URL + the leaner stateless payload differ. `useTuringChat` grows a `persona: { id, llmInstanceId }` subject as a peer of `agent` (persona wins if both set; stateless — flow/skill/variant ignored), and `createChatController` grows the same `persona` option. The transport is picked by a shared `dispatchSubjectTurn` (persona → agent → site). Extended the T406 SDK parity guard with the new endpoint. Both SDK typechecks + the app typecheck green.
- **T580** — **Deterministic, URL-addressable chat** — new bento routes `/bento/chat/agent/:agentId` and `/bento/chat/persona/:personaId` (kept `/bento/chat` = default agent). `ChatPage` now takes an `initialAgentId` prop resolved from the route instead of reading the **retired `CHAT_INITIAL_AGENT_KEY`** sessionStorage hand-off; the agent launch bar + home `ChatStarter` navigate to the deterministic URL (`ChatStarter` gained a `chatRoute` prop, defaulting to `/bento/chat`). The one-shot auto-send prompt hand-off (`CHAT_INITIAL_PROMPT_KEY`) stays. Back/forward + shareable links work; a `ChatSubject` type was added to `chat.types.ts`.
- **T581** — **Persona chat tab** — new `PersonaChatTab` + `PersonaChatPage` (persona-mode peer of `AgentChatTab`/`ChatPage`) reuse the presentational stack (`ChatInput`/`ChatMessageList`/`ChatEmptyState`/`ContextBar` + session sidebar + model-picker header) via the T579 `useTuringChat({ persona })` subject, dropping the agent-coupled surfaces (no flow/skill selectors, no per-agent pre-flight cost gate, no feedback/rephrase). `ChatEmptyState` gained a `persona` variant and `ChatInput` a `fuchsia` accent; any enabled LLM instance is offered (personas have no allow-list). Reached from `/bento/chat/persona/:personaId` under the frosted `BentoHero`. en/pt i18n added.
- **T582** — **Persona detail → bento dashboard** — the bento persona detail (`/bento/persona/instance/:id`) now reads like the AI-agent dashboard instead of reusing the pill-tab **form** as its landing (superseding T556 for the landing only). `bento.persona.page` became a thin **loader** that fetches the persona once and threads it (`BentoPersonaContext`) to child routes; the **index** renders a new `bento.persona.dashboard` — a `BentoHero` with **inline-edit** name/description + immediate-persist status toggle + `⋮` delete (mirrors the agent dashboard), over a frosted `BentoTile` **mosaic** (2×2 system-instruction hero tile → style, general, guidelines, and — for AUDIENCE/BOTH personas — audience, notebook, fit-report), each tile a doorway into its section. The section sub-pages stay reachable, moved under a pathless `bento.persona.editor` route that renders the unchanged shared-form `PersonaInstanceFormLayout` (single `useForm` + pill tab-bar + save bar); its bento branch now wraps the section `<Outlet>` in `SectionCardChromeProvider chrome="bento"` so every section's `SectionCard` renders as a frosted **`BentoFormSection`** (fixes the T556 half-migration — flat console cards, no hover-lift), and the hero eyebrow links back to the dashboard. New personas redirect to the general editor (nothing to dashboard yet). en/pt i18n added; `tsc -b --noEmit` + bento vitest green.
- **T583** — **Persona Launchpad** — new `PersonaLaunchBar` on the persona dashboard, mirroring `AiAgentLaunchBar` but built as an **extensible** launch bar: **Open chat** is live (navigates to the deterministic `/bento/chat/persona/:id`, T580) while future launch actions — content-fit validation (T584) and persona↔persona dialogue (T585) — render as disabled "soon" affordances (tonal gradient chip + "Soon" badge) so the surface communicates the roadmap without shipping half-features. New actions dock by appending to one `actions` array. en/pt i18n added.
- **T584** — **Content-fit validation from the launchpad** — the **Validate content** launch action is now live for AUDIENCE/BOTH personas (disabled with an "audience personas only" tooltip for SPEAKER, mirroring the dashboard's fit tiles). It opens a new first-class, shareable launch surface — `/bento/persona/validate/:personaId` (`BentoPersonaValidatePage`), a peer of the persona chat page rather than a buried editor tab — that composes Block AA's stack verbatim into an "add sources → score" flow: `PersonaSourcesSection` (T465 notebook: add SN doc / URL / uploaded asset) above `PersonaFitReportSection` (T467 grounded evaluator → T468 red/amber/green report), both self-contained on the persona id and rendered as frosted bento cards via `SectionCardChromeProvider chrome="bento"`. New `usePersona(id)` React-Query hook feeds the `BentoHero`. No backend change (the evaluator/report/notebook APIs already exist). Because this surface is now the single home for the notebook + fit report, the bento persona dashboard tiles and bento editor pills for "Evaluation notebook" / "Audience-fit report" were removed (the legacy console editor keeps them inline). en/pt i18n added; `tsc -b --noEmit` green.
- **T606** — **Style→model calibration on the native provider paths** (follow-up to T605). T605 applied the persona-derived `temperature`/`maxTokens` only on the Spring-AI CALL path (`TurAgentChatExecutor`); native-capable agents (OpenAI Responses / Anthropic Messages / Gemini GenAI) bypassed it. Now `TurNativeChatExecutor` computes `TurPersonaModelCalibration.forPersona(activePersona)` once (right after resolving the persona) and threads the `Params` into all three native services: **Anthropic** `buildParamsFromConversation` (temperature override respects the extended-thinking `temp=1` lock — skipped under thinking — while maxTokens is always honored), **OpenAI Responses** `buildParamsFromItems` (adds `maxOutputTokens`, which the chat path never set before, + temperature override), **Gemini** `buildConfig` (calibration takes precedence over `providerOptionsJson` then the instance default). `Params.NONE` (persona not opted in) leaves every native request byte-for-byte unchanged; Cohere native is out of scope. Per-service builder tests added (calibration overrides + NONE keeps instance values + Anthropic thinking-lock). All native + executor + mapper tests green.
- **T605** — **Opt-in style→model calibration** (follow-up). A persona's Style Guidelines (tone / verbosity) previously reached the LLM only as *text* in the system prompt; the model's `temperature`/`maxTokens` came solely from the `TurLLMInstance`. New per-persona flag `calibrateModelParams` (Liquibase v2026.3.1.85, default 0 = unchanged): when on, a pure mapper `TurPersonaModelCalibration` derives per-turn sampling overrides — **verbosity → maxTokens** (1→256 … 5→4096 via `128<<v`) and **tone → temperature** (TECHNICAL 0.2 / FORMAL 0.3 / EXECUTIVE 0.4 / CASUAL 0.8; null tone leaves temperature untouched). Applied in `TurAgentChatExecutor.providerToolOptions` by layering `.temperature()`/`.maxTokens()` onto the provider-seeded `ToolCallingChatOptions` builder, so it overrides only when opted in and otherwise keeps the instance's values (CALL path — covers agent chat, persona chat, and the dialogue). Admin toggle "Calibrate model from style" on the persona **Style** section; en/pt i18n. Mapper unit-tested (15 cases); persona prompt-cache tests unchanged. Backend + `tsc` green.
- **T604** — **Persona dialogue: N personas + live streaming** (follow-up to T585). The dialogue now supports **two or more** speakers (an unbounded roster with add/remove, min 2) driven **round-robin** — each persona replies to the previous speaker's utterance, cycling through the roster — instead of exactly two, and it **streams live**: `POST /api/v2/persona-dialogue` became an SSE endpoint returning `Flux<TurDialogueEvent>` (`TURN` per completed utterance, then `DONE` / `ERROR`), so the transcript builds turn by turn in the UI rather than arriving all at once. The blocking `TurDialogueTranscript`/`TurDialogueTurn` records were replaced by `TurDialogueEvent`; the service orchestrates on a bounded-elastic worker (the per-turn executor call still blocks-and-collects the CALL-mode token). Frontend: the page grew a dynamic speaker list (each slot a persona picker, "Add persona" button) and consumes the SSE via a `fetch` reader that primes CSRF (`X-XSRF-TOKEN`), appending colour-coded bubbles as turns arrive with a "next persona is thinking…" indicator; turn cap raised 20→40. 8-case Mockito test updated for round-robin + streamed events. Backend `mvn compile` + test green; `tsc -b --noEmit` green.
- **T585** — **Automatic persona↔persona conversation** — a **global** dialogue surface (two peer personas + a topic, so deliberately NOT scoped to one persona instance). Backend `TurPersonaDialogueService` + `POST /api/v2/persona-dialogue` (`TurPersonaDialogueAPI`) orchestrates a bounded, alternating dialogue by driving the same shared `TurAgentChatExecutor` the persona-chat MVP (T578) uses — one transient, never-persisted `TurAIAgent` per turn carrying the active speaker via `setDefaultPersona`, stateless (`conversationId=null`), no native tools (pure voice). The topic seeds persona A's opening turn; each persona then replies to the other's last utterance. Two flow rules: a shared **base system prompt** requires every reply to end with a question so the conversation self-sustains (the persona voice is fused on top by `TurChatPromptAssembler`, so it augments — not replaces — the persona), and the **turn budget** is configurable (default 10, clamped [2, 20]). The executor runs in its default `CALL` mode, so each utterance is collected from a single `"token"` event and joined; a mid-dialogue failure returns the partial transcript flagged with `llmError`. Frontend: global route `/bento/persona/dialogue` (`BentoPersonaDialoguePage`) — topic + two speaker pickers (AUDIENCE personas filtered out) + turns + model, rendering a two-sided transcript; reached from a new `headerAction` button on the persona **list** page (`BentoListPage` gained the optional slot) — a dialogue is between peer personas so it is deliberately a global surface, NOT on an individual persona's launchpad. 7-case Mockito test covers alternation/threading/turn-budget/errors/guards. en/pt i18n added; backend `mvn compile` + `tsc -b --noEmit` green.

## Block AH — LLM instance model picker

- **T577** — **Model field is now a vendor-aware editable combobox** (was free text). New provider seam `TurGenAiLlmProvider.listModels(...)` (empty default) with **live** implementations for OpenAI / OpenAI-compatible / Gemini-OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Ollama via a shared, resilient `TurLlmModelListingSupport` (plain `RestClient` per vendor — `/v1/models`, `x-api-key`+`anthropic-version`, `models.list`, `/api/tags` — never SDK-coupled, never throws). `TurLlmModelDiscoveryService` resolves the vendor, runs the live query with the typed-but-unsaved key (or the saved instance's decrypted key) and **falls back to the bundled `resources/llm/models-catalog.json`** (curated per plugin, loaded once by `TurLlmModelCatalog`) when live returns nothing — surfacing the source (`LIVE`/`CATALOG`/`NONE`) so the UI can badge it. Exposed at `POST /api/llm/models`. Frontend adds a dependency-free `ModelCombobox` (design-system Popover + filterable list, **stays editable** so any unlisted id can still be typed) fed by a React Query hook (`useLlmModels`, key never stores the secret; refetches with the latest key on open), wired into both the bento and console LLM instance forms. en/pt i18n added. Also fixes the runtime fallout of surfacing current models: Anthropic Opus 4.7/4.8, Sonnet 5, Fable 5 and the OpenAI o-series **reject `temperature`/`top_p`/`top_k` with a 400** ("`temperature` is deprecated for this model"), so a stored instance's sampling params would fail every call — `TurSamplingParamFallbackChatModel` now wraps the Anthropic + OpenAI chat/stream models and transparently retries once without sampling params on that rejection (runtime detection, no per-model capability table to rot). Backend `mvn compile` + frontend `tsc -b --noEmit` green.
